## 28. Co-Evolving Acting and Forecasting for Language Agents

**中文标题**: Co-Evolving Acting and Forecasting for Language Agents

**作者**: Haofei Yu, Yixing Chen, Yining Zhao, Xuyan Ye, Yuxiang Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20597v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20597v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20597v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/ulab-uiuc/CoPE.

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Training language agents is bottlenecked by the high cost of long-horizon rollouts, yet standard agentic reinforcement learning (RL) learns only from a terminal scalar reward, leaving the supervision in intermediate steps largely unused. Prior work exploits this supervision through value targets that estimate per-step success probabilities or observation targets that fit environment response. Both are hard to construct well: (1) value targets come from the same single scalar signal for each step in the trajectory, and (2) observation targets, such as web pages and terminal logs, are long and noisy and reach only one step ahead. We propose \name, which adds a third kind of multi-step action targets. On successful on-policy trajectories, \name trains the policy backbone to generate the action sequences that follows each history. Each action is also supervised when forecast from the preceding histories, forming a temporal ensemble that the shared backbone absorbs during training, and this enables acting and forecasting to co-evolve. The policy still acts one step at a time at inference, with no overhead. Across five benchmarks (ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, WebShop, $τ^2$-bench, and AppWorld), \name (1) achieves stronger aggregate performance than GRPO and RL baselines with predictive auxiliary targets across simulated environments, while improving overall performance on both realistic applications; (2) delivers up to $1.9\times$ and $2.0\times$ higher sample efficiency than ECHO and GRPO, respectively, on ScienceWorld; and (3) achieves these gains with a single policy backbone, without additional models, separate pretraining, or inference overhead. The codebase is available at https://github.com/ulab-uiuc/CoPE.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The codebase is available at https://github.com/ulab-uiuc/CoPE.

</details>

## 29. AFCS: Atomic-Fact-Conditioned Synthesis for Factual Knowledge Pretraining

**中文标题**: AFCS: Atomic-Fact-Conditioned Synthesis for Factual Knowledge Pretraining

**作者**: Yunhao Wang, Feng Zhang, Tinghao Yu, Xing Wu, Jiang Zhou

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20086v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20086v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20086v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Synthetic data is increasingly used in language-model pretraining. Existing knowledge-corpus synthesis pipelines, including WRAP and Nemotron-CC, usually take a source passage or document segment as input and generate rewritten or QA-style samples from that text. This can improve surface form and prompt diversity, but it does not explicitly require fine-grained source facts -- especially secondary entities, list/table entries, and precise temporal attributes -- to appear in the synthesized corpus. We propose AFCS (Atomic-Fact-Conditioned Synthesis), a pipeline that first extracts source-supported atomic facts and then uses each retained fact to drive generation. AFCS uses structure-aware extraction over the document tree, source-grounded verification, local context expansion, and multi-format generation. In continued-pretraining experiments with OLMo-3-7B and a 32B base on the same FineWiki source corpus, AFCS outperforms WRAP and Nemotron-CC on SimpleQA, SimpleQA Verified, PopQA, and HotpotQA. Granularity ablations show that fact-level synthesis improves over chunk-level, paragraph-level, and entity-level synthesis; and a 100B-token mid-training integration improves factual benchmarks and raises the 12-task general benchmark average by +1.09.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Synthetic data is increasingly used in language-model pretraining. Existing knowledge-corpus synthesis pipelines, including WRAP and Nemotron-CC, usually take a source passage or document segment as input and generate rewritten or QA-style samples from that text. This can improve surface form and prompt diversity, but it does not explicitly require fine-grained source facts -- especially secondary entities, list/table entries, and precise temporal attributes -- to appear in the synthesized corpus. We propose AFCS (Atomic-Fact-Conditioned Synthesis), a pipeline that first extracts source-supported atomic facts and then uses each retained fact to drive generation. AFCS uses structure-aware extraction over the document tree, source-grounded verification, local context expansion, and multi-format generation. In continued-pretraining experiments with OLMo-3-7B and a 32B base on the same FineWiki source corpus, AFCS outperforms WRAP and Nemotron-CC on SimpleQA, SimpleQA Verified, PopQA, and HotpotQA. Granularity ablations show that fact-level synthesis improves over chunk-level, paragraph-level, and entity-level synthesis; and a 100B-token mid-training integration improves factual benchmarks and raises the 12-task general benchmark average by +1.09.

</details>

---

## 30. On the Convergence of Semi-Gradient TD: A Mean Field Perspective

**中文标题**: On the Convergence of Semi-Gradient TD: A Mean Field Perspective

**作者**: Haruki Settai, Tadashi Kozuno, Shinji Ito

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20014v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20014v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20014v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Semi-gradient temporal-difference learning is a core algorithmic component of reinforcement learning, but bootstrapping with function approximation generally induces a non-conservative vector field and can lead to instability. We analyze this effect for two-layer neural networks in the mean-field regime and identify a simple stability mechanism in which entropy-induced relaxation dominates the sensitivity of the moving bootstrap target. Under this condition, we prove exponential convergence to a unique fixed point and quantify its bias relative to the unregularized Bellman solution. We also show that the same mechanism extends to entropy-regularized off-policy control through soft Q-learning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Semi-gradient temporal-difference learning is a core algorithmic component of reinforcement learning, but bootstrapping with function approximation generally induces a non-conservative vector field and can lead to instability. We analyze this effect for two-layer neural networks in the mean-field regime and identify a simple stability mechanism in which entropy-induced relaxation dominates the sensitivity of the moving bootstrap target. Under this condition, we prove exponential convergence to a unique fixed point and quantify its bias relative to the unregularized Bellman solution. We also show that the same mechanism extends to entropy-regularized off-policy control through soft Q-learning.

</details>

## 31. Neurosymbolic Learning of Interpretable World Models

**中文标题**: Neurosymbolic Learning of Interpretable World Models

**作者**: Riccardo Andreoni, Massimiliano Ronzani, Chiara Ghidini, Alessandro Daniele, Emile van Krieken

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19955v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19955v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19955v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World models aim to learn compact representations of an environment from observations, enabling agents to predict future states and plan their behaviour. However, most existing approaches learn black-box representations, with limited ability to incorporate prior knowledge about the world. We introduce Concept-Grounded World Models (CGWMs), a neurosymbolic world model design that instead learns interpretable concept representations. We learn these concepts from observations using explicit and complete knowledge, expressed in temporal logic, that specifies the valid world states and the temporal relations governing their dynamics. Furthermore, we directly use this knowledge by formulating planning as differentiable reasoning, optimising the agent's actions by gradient ascent to satisfy a goal. We experimentally compare CGWM with a purely neural baseline and a neurosymbolic baseline on two game environments. We find that CGWMs ground concepts much more accurately, even over long horizons, resulting in significantly more robust planning both in and out of distribution.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World models aim to learn compact representations of an environment from observations, enabling agents to predict future states and plan their behaviour. However, most existing approaches learn black-box representations, with limited ability to incorporate prior knowledge about the world. We introduce Concept-Grounded World Models (CGWMs), a neurosymbolic world model design that instead learns interpretable concept representations. We learn these concepts from observations using explicit and complete knowledge, expressed in temporal logic, that specifies the valid world states and the temporal relations governing their dynamics. Furthermore, we directly use this knowledge by formulating planning as differentiable reasoning, optimising the agent's actions by gradient ascent to satisfy a goal. We experimentally compare CGWM with a purely neural baseline and a neurosymbolic baseline on two game environments. We find that CGWMs ground concepts much more accurately, even over long horizons, resulting in significantly more robust planning both in and out of distribution.

</details>

## 32. DeepPV-MRF: End-to-End Partial-Volume Fraction Estimation from MR Fingerprinting Time Series

**中文标题**: DeepPV-MRF: End-to-End Partial-Volume Fraction Estimation from MR Fingerprinting Time Series

**作者**: Tianyi Ding, Yang Gao, Zhuang Xiong, Feng Liu, Martijn A. Cloos et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19894v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19894v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19894v1)

**类别**: eess.SP, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Background: Estimating subvoxel tissue fractions from magnetic resonance fingerprinting (MRF) is challenging. Conventional dictionary matching and segmentation pipelines can propagate errors from intermediate parametric maps into tissue fraction estimates.   Purpose: We introduce DeepPV-MRF, a deep learning framework that directly estimates voxel-wise tissue fractions from raw MRF signals.   Methods: A cascaded spatial-temporal network was trained on subject-derived Bloch simulations with radial k-space undersampling. The network combines temporal encoding and spatial U-Net refinement to estimate cerebrospinal fluid (CSF), gray matter (GM), and white matter (WM) fractions without intermediate T1/T2 maps. We compared DeepPV-MRF with partial-volume dictionary matching (PV-DM) and pseudoinverse estimation (PV-Pinv) using simulated and in vivo data.   Results: DeepPV-MRF outperformed both methods across acquisition settings. In single-shot simulations, CSF/WM/GM mean absolute errors were 0.0418/0.0744/0.0757, versus 0.1281/0.2443/0.2958 for PV-DM and 0.2021/0.2793/0.2630 for PV-Pinv. Corresponding structural similarity values were 0.960/0.916/0.922. In vivo results showed sharper anatomical boundaries and smoother tissue transitions, while cross-shot analysis indicated improved consistency across volunteers.   Conclusions: DeepPV-MRF enables accurate and efficient direct tissue fraction estimation from MRF signals, particularly with limited shots. Further validation is needed before clinical deployment.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Background: Estimating subvoxel tissue fractions from magnetic resonance fingerprinting (MRF) is challenging. Conventional dictionary matching and segmentation pipelines can propagate errors from intermediate parametric maps into tissue fraction estimates.   Purpose: We introduce DeepPV-MRF, a deep learning framework that directly estimates voxel-wise tissue fractions from raw MRF signals.   Methods: A cascaded spatial-temporal network was trained on subject-derived Bloch simulations with radial k-space undersampling. The network combines temporal encoding and spatial U-Net refinement to estimate cerebrospinal fluid (CSF), gray matter (GM), and white matter (WM) fractions without intermediate T1/T2 maps. We compared DeepPV-MRF with partial-volume dictionary matching (PV-DM) and pseudoinverse estimation (PV-Pinv) using simulated and in vivo data.   Results: DeepPV-MRF outperformed both methods across acquisition settings. In single-shot simulations, CSF/WM/GM mean absolute errors were 0.0418/0.0744/0.0757, versus 0.1281/0.2443/0.2958 for PV-DM and 0.2021/0.2793/0.2630 for PV-Pinv. Corresponding structural similarity values were 0.960/0.916/0.922. In vivo results showed sharper anatomical boundaries and smoother tissue transitions, while cross-shot analysis indicated improved consistency across volunteers.   Conclusions: DeepPV-MRF enables accurate and efficient direct tissue fraction estimation from MRF signals, particularly with limited shots. Further validation is needed before clinical deployment.

</details>

## 33. LAGO: Language Agents with Reusable Growing Options

**中文标题**: LAGO: Language Agents with Reusable Growing Options

**作者**: Yuran Wu, Mengkang Hu, Qionglin Qiu, Fanqing Meng, Qiguang Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19843v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19843v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19843v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.MA

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-horizon tasks expose a weakness of monolithic tool-use loops: one agent must decompose, execute, and verify a task within a single trajectory. We introduce LAGO, which treats specialized language agents as reusable temporally extended options. A meta-agent retrieves options from a persistent library or spawns new ones, dispatches sub-tasks, verifies results, and admits successful novel options. The library grows across tasks without updating model weights. On 500 SWE-bench Verified tasks, frozen Qwen3.5-35B-A3B in both roles resolves 58.6% under single-attempt evaluation with the official Docker evaluator. On a fixed 150-task subset, we re-run mini-SWE-agent, Terminus 2, and LAGO with the same policy, temperature, task IDs, and evaluator; their resolve rates are 49.3%, 57.3%, and 60.3%, respectively. Cross-model experiments provide evidence consistent with a role for decomposition quality. A 50-task component ablation finds four fewer resolved tasks without decomposition, two fewer with shuffled reused content, and six fewer without the library. These results support reusable agent-options as a practical abstraction for task streams, while leaving component-level causality task- and model-dependent.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Long-horizon tasks expose a weakness of monolithic tool-use loops: one agent must decompose, execute, and verify a task within a single trajectory. We introduce LAGO, which treats specialized language agents as reusable temporally extended options. A meta-agent retrieves options from a persistent library or spawns new ones, dispatches sub-tasks, verifies results, and admits successful novel options. The library grows across tasks without updating model weights. On a fixed 150-task subset, we re-run mini-SWE-agent, Terminus 2, and LAGO with the same policy, temperature, task IDs, and evaluator; their resolve rates are 49.3%, 57.3%, and 60.3%, respectively. Cross-model experiments provide evidence consistent with a role for decomposition quality. These results support reusable agent-options as a practical abstraction for task streams, while leaving component-level causality task- and model-dependent.

</details>

## 34. One Monitor Does Not Fit All: Formalism-Agnostic Runtime Verification for Agentic AI

**中文标题**: One Monitor Does Not Fit All: Formalism-Agnostic Runtime Verification for Agentic AI

**作者**: Angelo Ferrando

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19818v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19818v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19818v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Agentic AI systems combine model decisions, mutable state, tool calls, and delegation to external agents. Monitoring them requires observations from different execution boundaries, while prevention requires control before an operation produces its effect. We present AgenticRV, a runtime-verification architecture that separates these boundaries from the monitoring formalism. Adapters emit canonical events; each monitor receives its own projection and abstraction; and a decision policy uses normalised verdicts to select a reaction supported by the responsible adapter. We formalise when observations suffice, monitor replacement preserves decisions, and blocking prevents unsafe effects. We also distinguish rejected attempts from completed effects, avoiding the assumption that a blocked request must leave every monitor in a permanent violation state. The Python implementation supports LangGraph, MCP, and A2A. A controlled LangGraph experiment combines an external Reelay temporal monitor with a programmatic report monitor; ablations show why both are needed for the selected requirements. Differential checks and cross-adapter tests provide further integration evidence. An LLM-driven incident-response example illustrates the same enforcement path, while existing microbenchmarks isolate framework costs. The results support reuse of monitoring methods at explicit control boundaries, subject to the stated observation and deployment assumptions.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Agentic AI systems combine model decisions, mutable state, tool calls, and delegation to external agents. Monitoring them requires observations from different execution boundaries, while prevention requires control before an operation produces its effect. We present AgenticRV, a runtime-verification architecture that separates these boundaries from the monitoring formalism. Adapters emit canonical events; each monitor receives its own projection and abstraction; and a decision policy uses normalised verdicts to select a reaction supported by the responsible adapter. We formalise when observations suffice, monitor replacement preserves decisions, and blocking prevents unsafe effects. We also distinguish rejected attempts from completed effects, avoiding the assumption that a blocked request must leave every monitor in a permanent violation state. The Python implementation supports LangGraph, MCP, and A2A. A controlled LangGraph experiment combines an external Reelay temporal monitor with a programmatic report monitor; ablations show why both are needed for the selected requirements. Differential checks and cross-adapter tests provide further integration evidence. An LLM-driven incident-response example illustrates the same enforcement path, while existing microbenchmarks isolate framework costs. The results support reuse of monitoring methods at explicit control boundaries, subject to the stated observation and deployment assumptions.

</details>

## 35. VaryST: Generalizable Spatio-Temporal Forecasting Beyond the Closed-World Setting

**中文标题**: VaryST: Generalizable Spatio-Temporal Forecasting Beyond the Closed-World Setting

**作者**: Shengnan Guo, Jinjun Cheng, Xiaowei Mao, Jianhao Zhang, Haochen Lv et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19813v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19813v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19813v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Spatio-temporal forecasting is fundamental to many real-world systems, yet deployment environments are inherently varying: temporal distributions may evolve, spatial domains may change, and observation availability may shift from training conditions. Existing models are often tightly coupled to their training environments through node-specific representations, predefined spatial support sets, and reliance on consistently available observations, limiting generalization to changing environments. To this end, we propose VaryST, a compact spatio-temporal forecasting model trained from scratch that achieves strong generalization by dynamically constructing predictive context from available long-term observations. To accommodate temporal changes, VaryST derives node representations from long-term histories, capturing current behavior in the context of longer-term patterns. To adapt to spatial changes, it dynamically selects functionally relevant support nodes beyond predefined neighborhoods and integrates their information with recent and long-term target states and global context. To handle varying observation availability, VaryST selectively masks historical patches on which the model relies most during training, encouraging it to exploit alternative temporal and spatial dependencies for accurate forecasting when key observations become unavailable. Extensive experiments on six real-world datasets against seventeen baselines show that VaryST achieves strong in-domain forecasting accuracy, substantially improves zero- and few-shot generalization under spatial and temporal changes, and remains robust to varying observation availability.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Spatio-temporal forecasting is fundamental to many real-world systems, yet deployment environments are inherently varying: temporal distributions may evolve, spatial domains may change, and observation availability may shift from training conditions. Existing models are often tightly coupled to their training environments through node-specific representations, predefined spatial support sets, and reliance on consistently available observations, limiting generalization to changing environments. To this end, we propose VaryST, a compact spatio-temporal forecasting model trained from scratch that achieves strong generalization by dynamically constructing predictive context from available long-term observations. To accommodate temporal changes, VaryST derives node representations from long-term histories, capturing current behavior in the context of longer-term patterns. To adapt to spatial changes, it dynamically selects functionally relevant support nodes beyond predefined neighborhoods and integrates their information with recent and long-term target states and global context. To handle varying observation availability, VaryST selectively masks historical patches on which the model relies most during training, encouraging it to exploit alternative temporal and spatial dependencies for accurate forecasting when key observations become unavailable. Extensive experiments on six real-world datasets against seventeen baselines show that VaryST achieves strong in-domain forecasting accuracy, substantially improves zero- and few-shot generalization under spatial and temporal changes, and remains robust to varying observation availability.

</details>

## 36. Runtime Monitoring of Supermartingale Certificates

**中文标题**: Runtime Monitoring of Supermartingale Certificates

**作者**: Mirco Giacobbe, Thomas A. Henzinger, Konstantin Kueffner, Emily Yu, Đorđe Žikelić

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19701v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19701v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19701v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, eess.SY

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Supermartingale certificates formally guarantee temporal properties of discrete and continuous-time stochastic dynamical systems. However, these guarantees rely on a mathematical model of the world, leaving a sim-to-real gap that can invalidate them for the deployed system. We formulate the runtime monitoring problem for supermartingale certificates, in which a monitor observes a single system trajectory and raises an alarm when there is sufficient statistical evidence that the supermartingale certificate has been violated during execution. We present an algorithm to automatically construct a monitor using betting-based sequential tests that bound the probability of ever raising a false alarm at a user-specified level. Within this betting framework, we formulate strategies that allow the monitor to exploit spatial regularity to improve detection power while preserving this statistical guarantee. We demonstrate the efficacy of our approach on supermartingale certificate benchmarks from the literature.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Supermartingale certificates formally guarantee temporal properties of discrete and continuous-time stochastic dynamical systems. However, these guarantees rely on a mathematical model of the world, leaving a sim-to-real gap that can invalidate them for the deployed system. We formulate the runtime monitoring problem for supermartingale certificates, in which a monitor observes a single system trajectory and raises an alarm when there is sufficient statistical evidence that the supermartingale certificate has been violated during execution. We present an algorithm to automatically construct a monitor using betting-based sequential tests that bound the probability of ever raising a false alarm at a user-specified level. Within this betting framework, we formulate strategies that allow the monitor to exploit spatial regularity to improve detection power while preserving this statistical guarantee. We demonstrate the efficacy of our approach on supermartingale certificate benchmarks from the literature.

</details>

## 37. Function-Space Temporal Self-Distillation for Policy Optimization

**中文标题**: Function-Space Temporal Self-Distillation for Policy Optimization

**作者**: Aryan Das, Arnab Bhattacharya, Vinay Kumar Verma

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19687v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19687v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19687v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CL, stat.ML

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) improves reasoning in language models, but scalar outcomes provide coarse supervision over response trajectories. Self-distillation offers denser token-level guidance by comparing an unassisted policy with a feedback-informed self-teacher, yet the teacher evolves with the student, creating unstable targets and motivating additional regularization. We introduce Function-Space Policy Optimization (FSPO), a single-policy framework that combines current privileged adaptation with function-space temporal consolidation. FSPO stores sparse historical next-token distributions as fixed temporal targets, providing temporal separation without maintaining a persistent teacher model. It further introduces privileged disagreement correction to explicitly suppress locally overvalued actions, reliability-adaptive grounding that strengthens reference supervision when self-generated evidence repeatedly fails, and quality and coverage-aware temporal memory that retains verified behaviors and improves privileged demonstrations. Across reasoning and tool-use benchmarks, FSPO achieves the highest average accuracy under a 5-hour training budget, improving over the strongest baseline by approximately 4% points on average while reducing peak memory from about 70 GB to 56 GB on Olmo3-7B-Instruct.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) improves reasoning in language models, but scalar outcomes provide coarse supervision over response trajectories. Self-distillation offers denser token-level guidance by comparing an unassisted policy with a feedback-informed self-teacher, yet the teacher evolves with the student, creating unstable targets and motivating additional regularization. We introduce Function-Space Policy Optimization (FSPO), a single-policy framework that combines current privileged adaptation with function-space temporal consolidation. FSPO stores sparse historical next-token distributions as fixed temporal targets, providing temporal separation without maintaining a persistent teacher model. It further introduces privileged disagreement correction to explicitly suppress locally overvalued actions, reliability-adaptive grounding that strengthens reference supervision when self-generated evidence repeatedly fails, and quality and coverage-aware temporal memory that retains verified behaviors and improves privileged demonstrations. Across reasoning and tool-use benchmarks, FSPO achieves the highest average accuracy under a 5-hour training budget, improving over the strongest baseline by approximately 4% points on average while reducing peak memory from about 70 GB to 56 GB on Olmo3-7B-Instruct.

</details>

## 38. Eye, Robot: Learning Expressive Gaze Dynamics via a VLM-Guided Transformer-VAE

**中文标题**: Eye, Robot: Learning Expressive Gaze Dynamics via a VLM-Guided Transformer-VAE

**作者**: Yuhang Hu, Junlin Lyu, Yuan Fang, Xiaoying Shen, Lechen Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19635v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19635v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19635v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Gaze coordinates attention in human interaction through coupled eye, head, and eyelid motion. A humanoid robot must choose where to look while generating a suitable movement toward that target; fixed tracking rules alone do not model the temporal structure of human gaze. We separate these tasks with an asynchronous vision-language model that selects a scene referent, a reactive perception module that maintains fixation, and a Realistic Gaze Transformer (RGT) that generates target-conditioned motion. RGT is a Transformer-VAE trained on markerless human recordings retargeted to the robot's 11-DoF joint space: self-supervised pretraining encodes recent full-head history, and autoregressive fine-tuning exposes the model to its own predictions during rollout. At inference, a four-dimensional binocular-eye trajectory conditions coordinated neck and eye motion; an auxiliary blink predictor triggers eyelid templates during playback. On a two-second benchmark in which all controllers receive the same ground-truth target, RGT-Base obtains the lowest reported overall seven-joint MSE, and physical demonstrations show the integrated targeting and control pipeline. The evidence establishes robot-space kinematic fidelity and real-time RGT inference; perceived naturalness and open-vocabulary target-selection accuracy remain questions for controlled evaluation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Gaze coordinates attention in human interaction through coupled eye, head, and eyelid motion. A humanoid robot must choose where to look while generating a suitable movement toward that target; fixed tracking rules alone do not model the temporal structure of human gaze. We separate these tasks with an asynchronous vision-language model that selects a scene referent, a reactive perception module that maintains fixation, and a Realistic Gaze Transformer (RGT) that generates target-conditioned motion. RGT is a Transformer-VAE trained on markerless human recordings retargeted to the robot's 11-DoF joint space: self-supervised pretraining encodes recent full-head history, and autoregressive fine-tuning exposes the model to its own predictions during rollout. At inference, a four-dimensional binocular-eye trajectory conditions coordinated neck and eye motion; an auxiliary blink predictor triggers eyelid templates during playback. On a two-second benchmark in which all controllers receive the same ground-truth target, RGT-Base obtains the lowest reported overall seven-joint MSE, and physical demonstrations show the integrated targeting and control pipeline. The evidence establishes robot-space kinematic fidelity and real-time RGT inference; perceived naturalness and open-vocabulary target-selection accuracy remain questions for controlled evaluation.

</details>

## 39. Beyond Static Positional Encoding: Bayesian Time-aware Attention for Non-Stationary Long-term Time Series Forecasting

**中文标题**: Beyond Static Positional Encoding: Bayesian Time-aware Attention for Non-Stationary Long-term Time Series Forecasting

**作者**: Xinjie Lan, Yunbo Mei, Leheng Zhou, Hao Qi

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19603v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19603v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19603v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Transformers have demonstrated strong performance in long-term time series forecasting (LTSF), yet modeling non-stationary temporal dependencies remains challenging. In this paper, we interpret self-attention as a Bayesian posterior over attended positions, combining a content-based prior induced by scaled dot-product similarity with a temporal-evidence likelihood induced by positional encoding. This formulation provides a probabilistic interpretation of how positional encoding shapes the temporal dependencies captured by self-attention. Building on this perspective, we derive a population-level evidence lower bound (ELBO) that characterizes the role of positional encoding in forecasting weakly stationary time series. Our analysis further highlights a limitation of static positional encoding: when temporal dependence structures vary across input windows, a fixed temporal bias must accommodate heterogeneous dynamics and can therefore be suboptimal for non-stationary forecasting. Motivated by this analysis, we propose Bayesian Time-aware Attention (BTA), which employs a mixture-of-experts (MoE) router to adaptively combine temporal kernels and generate input-conditioned, head-specific positional biases. Extensive experiments on standard LTSF benchmarks demonstrate that BTA consistently improves forecasting performance across multiple Transformer backbones and achieves competitive overall performance with modest computational overhead and stable training.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Transformers have demonstrated strong performance in long-term time series forecasting (LTSF), yet modeling non-stationary temporal dependencies remains challenging. In this paper, we interpret self-attention as a Bayesian posterior over attended positions, combining a content-based prior induced by scaled dot-product similarity with a temporal-evidence likelihood induced by positional encoding. This formulation provides a probabilistic interpretation of how positional encoding shapes the temporal dependencies captured by self-attention. Building on this perspective, we derive a population-level evidence lower bound (ELBO) that characterizes the role of positional encoding in forecasting weakly stationary time series. Our analysis further highlights a limitation of static positional encoding: when temporal dependence structures vary across input windows, a fixed temporal bias must accommodate heterogeneous dynamics and can therefore be suboptimal for non-stationary forecasting. Motivated by this analysis, we propose Bayesian Time-aware Attention (BTA), which employs a mixture-of-experts (MoE) router to adaptively combine temporal kernels and generate input-conditioned, head-specific positional biases. Extensive experiments on standard LTSF benchmarks demonstrate that BTA consistently improves forecasting performance across multiple Transformer backbones and achieves competitive overall performance with modest computational overhead and stable training.

</details>

## 40. Learning Across Temporal Graph Representation Silos

**中文标题**: Learning Across Temporal Graph Representation Silos

**作者**: Minwoo Yu, Young-guk Ha

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19576v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19576v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19576v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Temporal graph data are recorded in different representations, but whether these differences also determine the scope of learned knowledge reuse remains unclear. This study investigates whether predictive structure learned from relational histories can be reused between continuous-time dynamic graphs (CTDGs) and temporal knowledge graphs (TKGs). Bidirectional reuse emerges while retaining distinct input and prediction interfaces and independent vocabularies, and sources with the same representation are not always more useful. Controlled reorganizations of source histories show that the organization contributing to reuse varies across source-target conditions. We show that learned aspects of Relational Dynamics, the predictive structure present in relational histories, can be reused across conventional representation boundaries. Independently pretrained TKG and CTDG learners can also be fused at the score level to improve prediction on a third dataset without entity or relation-semantic alignment. Two proofs of concept in recommendation and security detection illustrate that external temporal sources without direct alignment can also become useful pretraining resources.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Temporal graph data are recorded in different representations, but whether these differences also determine the scope of learned knowledge reuse remains unclear. This study investigates whether predictive structure learned from relational histories can be reused between continuous-time dynamic graphs (CTDGs) and temporal knowledge graphs (TKGs). Bidirectional reuse emerges while retaining distinct input and prediction interfaces and independent vocabularies, and sources with the same representation are not always more useful. Controlled reorganizations of source histories show that the organization contributing to reuse varies across source-target conditions. We show that learned aspects of Relational Dynamics, the predictive structure present in relational histories, can be reused across conventional representation boundaries. Independently pretrained TKG and CTDG learners can also be fused at the score level to improve prediction on a third dataset without entity or relation-semantic alignment. Two proofs of concept in recommendation and security detection illustrate that external temporal sources without direct alignment can also become useful pretraining resources.

</details>

---

## 41. TEX-MEX: Similarity-Guided Temporal Contrastive Learning for Time Series

**中文标题**: TEX-MEX: Similarity-Guided Temporal Contrastive Learning for Time Series

**作者**: Aditya Radhakrishnan, Maxwell A. Xu, Wanting Mao, Santosh Kumar, Harish Haresamudram et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19557v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19557v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19557v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Contrastive learning for time series depends on deciding which pairs of inputs should be treated as similar and dissimilar. Because few augmentations reliably preserve meaning in time series, prior methods have relied on two disjoint notions of similarity between windows: structural similarity, which relates windows with similar morphology, and temporal similarity, which relates windows that occur close together in time. We introduce TEX-MEX, to our knowledge the first approach to unify the two into a single relationship. Given a random batch of windows drawn across the dataset, TEX-MEX uses the encoder to identify which windows are structurally similar or dissimilar to each other. It then pulls each window toward the temporally shifted versions of similar windows and pushes it away from those of dissimilar ones, learning what typically accompanies similar windows in time. Across PPG, EEG, ECG, accelerometry, and continuous glucose monitoring, TEX-MEX broadly achieves state-of-the-art performance against contrastive, reconstructive, JEPA-based, and modality-specific approaches, establishing it as a strong general SSL objective. We further train a 560K-parameter PPG foundation model that achieves state-of-the-art performance across a broad range of downstream benchmarks while being roughly 50x smaller than the next-best unimodally pretrained PPG foundation model in our evaluation. We will release this foundation model open-source and open-weight to the community to support future work and practical applications.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Contrastive learning for time series depends on deciding which pairs of inputs should be treated as similar and dissimilar. Because few augmentations reliably preserve meaning in time series, prior methods have relied on two disjoint notions of similarity between windows: structural similarity, which relates windows with similar morphology, and temporal similarity, which relates windows that occur close together in time. We introduce TEX-MEX, to our knowledge the first approach to unify the two into a single relationship. Given a random batch of windows drawn across the dataset, TEX-MEX uses the encoder to identify which windows are structurally similar or dissimilar to each other. It then pulls each window toward the temporally shifted versions of similar windows and pushes it away from those of dissimilar ones, learning what typically accompanies similar windows in time. Across PPG, EEG, ECG, accelerometry, and continuous glucose monitoring, TEX-MEX broadly achieves state-of-the-art performance against contrastive, reconstructive, JEPA-based, and modality-specific approaches, establishing it as a strong general SSL objective. We further train a 560K-parameter PPG foundation model that achieves state-of-the-art performance across a broad range of downstream benchmarks while being roughly 50x smaller than the next-best unimodally pretrained PPG foundation model in our evaluation. We will release this foundation model open-source and open-weight to the community to support future work and practical applications.

</details>

## 42. KoAGE: Do Large Language Models Know, Learn, and Coin Korean Neologisms?

**中文标题**: KoAGE: Do Large Language Models Know, Learn, and Coin Korean Neologisms?

**作者**: SungHo Kim, Eojin Jeon, SangKeun Lee

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19399v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19399v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19399v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Korean neologisms arise through diverse mechanisms, a demanding test of language models beyond lexical memorization. To enable systematic evaluation, we introduce NeoTrace, an automated, rerunnable framework that collects and dates neologisms across genres. Its 4,271 entries support KoAGE, a cutoff-aware benchmark with three acquisition and two generation tasks---the latter, to our knowledge, the first to compare model coinages with attested neologisms under their original materials and community context. Across 21 models, lexical recall diverges from contextual inference: in a five-model temporal analysis, models define only 13-30% of cutoff-year words, yet can infer meaning from one usage. However, structural understanding remains limited: even correct definitions barely improve formation analysis of pre-cutoff neologisms. Given word-building materials, single models often follow human formation mechanisms, whereas multi-agent coinage favors abbreviation and depends on interaction framing. KoAGE thus separates what models know, learn from use, and coin.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Korean neologisms arise through diverse mechanisms, a demanding test of language models beyond lexical memorization. To enable systematic evaluation, we introduce NeoTrace, an automated, rerunnable framework that collects and dates neologisms across genres. Its 4,271 entries support KoAGE, a cutoff-aware benchmark with three acquisition and two generation tasks---the latter, to our knowledge, the first to compare model coinages with attested neologisms under their original materials and community context. Across 21 models, lexical recall diverges from contextual inference: in a five-model temporal analysis, models define only 13-30% of cutoff-year words, yet can infer meaning from one usage. However, structural understanding remains limited: even correct definitions barely improve formation analysis of pre-cutoff neologisms. Given word-building materials, single models often follow human formation mechanisms, whereas multi-agent coinage favors abbreviation and depends on interaction framing. KoAGE thus separates what models know, learn from use, and coin.

</details>

## 43. Seeing, Maintaining, and Learning: The Evolution of Multimodal Agent Memory

**中文标题**: Seeing, Maintaining, and Learning: The Evolution of Multimodal Agent Memory

**作者**: Minghao Guo, Qingyue Jiao, Xiujin Liu, Jianhong Pang, Ruimeng Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19366v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19366v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19366v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal models and agents have emerged to enable AI systems to learn from and interact with the real world more like humans by integrating information across multiple modalities. However, enabling multimodal agents to continually acquire useful information from complex, heterogeneous multimodal data remains a significant challenge as they move toward long-horizon tasks and lifelong settings. Addressing this challenge requires effective memory mechanisms that can maintain continuity across sessions, adapt to changing users and environments, and refine memory-management policies through accumulated experience. We introduce a two-axis analytical framework for multimodal agent memory structured around two key questions: how multimodal information is formed and represented (Axis I, Formation and Representation), and how its management adapts with accumulated experience (Axis II, Memory Management). Axis I, Formation and Representation, characterizes what information is retained and how it is encoded (e.g., modality-specific embeddings, textual and structured records, latent and parametric representations) to support subsequent reasoning and action. Axis II, Memory Management, distinguishes three tiers: T1 Static Memory reuses fixed memory; T2 Dynamic Memory updates memory through a stable policy; and T3 Self-Evolving Memory updates memory through an evolving policy. Based on this framework, we synthesize recurring design patterns and derive principles for balancing source fidelity with abstraction, maintaining temporal validity and provenance, grounding memory use in recoverable evidence, and evolving memory policies over time. Furthermore, we discuss the challenges and potential future directions for the memory mechanism of multimodal agents.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multimodal models and agents have emerged to enable AI systems to learn from and interact with the real world more like humans by integrating information across multiple modalities. However, enabling multimodal agents to continually acquire useful information from complex, heterogeneous multimodal data remains a significant challenge as they move toward long-horizon tasks and lifelong settings. Addressing this challenge requires effective memory mechanisms that can maintain continuity across sessions, adapt to changing users and environments, and refine memory-management policies through accumulated experience. We introduce a two-axis analytical framework for multimodal agent memory structured around two key questions: how multimodal information is formed and represented (Axis I, Formation and Representation), and how its management adapts with accumulated experience (Axis II, Memory Management). Axis I, Formation and Representation, characterizes what information is retained and how it is encoded (e.g., modality-specific embeddings, textual and structured records, latent and parametric representations) to support subsequent reasoning and action. Axis II, Memory Management, distinguishes three tiers: T1 Static Memory reuses fixed memory; T2 Dynamic Memory updates memory through a stable policy; and T3 Self-Evolving Memory updates memory through an evolving policy. Based on this framework, we synthesize recurring design patterns and derive principles for balancing source fidelity with abstraction, maintaining temporal validity and provenance, grounding memory use in recoverable evidence, and evolving memory policies over time. Furthermore, we discuss the challenges and potential future directions for the memory mechanism of multimodal agents.

</details>

## 44. CoWear: Towards Collaborative Wearable Inertial Localization with Multiple Smart Devices

**中文标题**: CoWear: Towards Collaborative Wearable Inertial Localization with Multiple Smart Devices

**作者**: Zhuoyuan She, Zhanpeng Zhang, Yapeng Chen, Jiale Wang, Xin Xie et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20596v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20596v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20596v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Consumer smart devices observe the same human motion from different body locations, yet existing inertial-localization benchmarks rarely provide synchronized heterogeneous wearables with device-specific motion ground truth. We present CoWear, a dataset and reference framework for collaborative inertial localization using a smartphone, smartwatch, and smart glasses. The dataset contains over 30 participants, over 300 walking sessions, and more than 30 hours and 100 km of aggregate device trajectories, with each device individually tracked by motion capture. To enable collaboration across distinct body motions, we train role-specific estimators to predict the displacement of a common reference device over synchronized temporal windows. Their predictions are transformed to the world frame using gyroscope integration and fused according to learned uncertainty. Experiments using phone, glasses, and watch as reference targets show that multi-device fusion consistently improves both displacement and trajectory rollout accuracy over individual-device estimation. CoWear provides a dataset and reproducible baseline for studying collaborative inertial localization across heterogeneous consumer wearables.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Consumer smart devices observe the same human motion from different body locations, yet existing inertial-localization benchmarks rarely provide synchronized heterogeneous wearables with device-specific motion ground truth. We present CoWear, a dataset and reference framework for collaborative inertial localization using a smartphone, smartwatch, and smart glasses. The dataset contains over 30 participants, over 300 walking sessions, and more than 30 hours and 100 km of aggregate device trajectories, with each device individually tracked by motion capture. To enable collaboration across distinct body motions, we train role-specific estimators to predict the displacement of a common reference device over synchronized temporal windows. Their predictions are transformed to the world frame using gyroscope integration and fused according to learned uncertainty. Experiments using phone, glasses, and watch as reference targets show that multi-device fusion consistently improves both displacement and trajectory rollout accuracy over individual-device estimation. CoWear provides a dataset and reproducible baseline for studying collaborative inertial localization across heterogeneous consumer wearables.

</details>

## 45. KinoGen: Customizable Humanoid Loco-Manipulation References via Kinematically Grounded Video Generation

**中文标题**: KinoGen: Customizable Humanoid Loco-Manipulation References via Kinematically Grounded Video Generation

**作者**: Qi Wu, Lulu Zhao, Sanghyun Hahn, Rishi Yennu, Emmy Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20552v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20552v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20552v1)

**项目主页**: https://kinogen-humanoid.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present KinoGen, a framework that synthesizes customizable humanoid loco-manipulation references through kinematically grounded video generation. KinoGen conditions a pretrained video generator on calibrated renders of the target robot and object in a simulated task instance and recovers the reference by fitting the same models to the generated two-view video, so that the reference lies in the robot configuration space by construction without a human body proxy or retargeting. Body, hand, and object priors initialize the reconstruction, and direct optimization against cross-view evidence refines it. Since an edited keyframe is simply another rendered state, a user can prescribe a goal object pose, regenerate the video around the edited keyframe, and obtain a reference that satisfies the pose exactly. Across eight loco-manipulation tasks, KinoGen yields lower penetration and foot skating than video-based baselines and preserves over 90% of annotated contacts, and its customized references satisfy every prescribed target with substantially less penetration than direct interpolation. Tracking policies trained on the synthesized references are deployed on a Unitree G1 humanoid with dexterous hands. Project webpage: https://kinogen-humanoid.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present KinoGen, a framework that synthesizes customizable humanoid loco-manipulation references through kinematically grounded video generation. KinoGen conditions a pretrained video generator on calibrated renders of the target robot and object in a simulated task instance and recovers the reference by fitting the same models to the generated two-view video, so that the reference lies in the robot configuration space by construction without a human body proxy or retargeting. Body, hand, and object priors initialize the reconstruction, and direct optimization against cross-view evidence refines it. Since an edited keyframe is simply another rendered state, a user can prescribe a goal object pose, regenerate the video around the edited keyframe, and obtain a reference that satisfies the pose exactly. Across eight loco-manipulation tasks, KinoGen yields lower penetration and foot skating than video-based baselines and preserves over 90% of annotated contacts, and its customized references satisfy every prescribed target with substantially less penetration than direct interpolation. Tracking policies trained on the synthesized references are deployed on a Unitree G1 humanoid with dexterous hands. Project webpage: https://kinogen-humanoid.github.io/.

</details>

## 46. Physical Time as a Missing Dimension of Robot Policy Memory Dynamics

**中文标题**: Physical Time as a Missing Dimension of Robot Policy Memory Dynamics

**作者**: Yushen Liang, Yue Peng, Baosheng Jin, Tianluo Zhang, Xinyu Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20524v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20524v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20524v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Existing robot policy memories typically evolve with observation sequences, implicitly treating each update as the same amount of physical time. Yet identical observation histories can span different durations and require different actions, exposing physical time as a missing dimension of policy memory. We introduce Temporal Flow Policy (TFP), which learns memory dynamics to retain past events and evolve the memory state over measured time intervals. Observations and prior memory determine the content and time scales of each transition; elapsed time determines how far the state advances. TFP conditions diffusion and flow-matching policies through this memory state and is trained with their native imitation objectives. We evaluate TFP with Diffusion Policy and $π_{0.5}$ through controlled comparisons of memory designs. These experiments show that successful timing depends on both measured intervals and retained history. TFP achieves higher average success than the recurrent and state-space memories we equip with the same intervals and than a variant that feeds the interval to a learned gate. Memory readouts recover the time since an event and motion information after occlusion. Changing the supplied intervals systematically delays or advances actions, linking time inputs to behavior. Real-robot tasks requiring timing or memory, together with LIBERO evaluations, demonstrate benefits in physical and general manipulation. These results suggest viewing robot memory not as stored observation history, but as a state that retains past events and evolves with physical time.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing robot policy memories typically evolve with observation sequences, implicitly treating each update as the same amount of physical time. Yet identical observation histories can span different durations and require different actions, exposing physical time as a missing dimension of policy memory. We introduce Temporal Flow Policy (TFP), which learns memory dynamics to retain past events and evolve the memory state over measured time intervals. Observations and prior memory determine the content and time scales of each transition; elapsed time determines how far the state advances. TFP conditions diffusion and flow-matching policies through this memory state and is trained with their native imitation objectives. We evaluate TFP with Diffusion Policy and $π_{0.5}$ through controlled comparisons of memory designs. These experiments show that successful timing depends on both measured intervals and retained history. TFP achieves higher average success than the recurrent and state-space memories we equip with the same intervals and than a variant that feeds the interval to a learned gate. Memory readouts recover the time since an event and motion information after occlusion. Changing the supplied intervals systematically delays or advances actions, linking time inputs to behavior. Real-robot tasks requiring timing or memory, together with LIBERO evaluations, demonstrate benefits in physical and general manipulation. These results suggest viewing robot memory not as stored observation history, but as a state that retains past events and evolves with physical time.

</details>

## 47. Safe Reinforcement Learning with Receding-Horizon Safety Critic and Differentiable Action Shields

**中文标题**: Safe Reinforcement Learning with Receding-Horizon Safety Critic and Differentiable Action Shields

**作者**: Siwei Ju, Jan Peters, Oleg Arenz

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20255v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20255v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20255v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Despite the Markov assumption, safety failures in robot reinforcement learning rarely occur instantaneously; they typically emerge over several steps as the policy enters unfamiliar states under disturbances and domain shift. We address this limitation by learning a receding-horizon safety critic that predicts whether a state will remain non-terminating over the next receding horizon, enabling anticipatory detection of impending locomotion failures. The critic is trained from rollout-derived horizon labels with a composite objective that combines horizon classification, control-barrier-function-inspired temporal consistency, and smoothness regularization, and we aggregate an ensemble of critics with a conservative Conditional Value at Risk estimate to account for uncertainty. Building on the learned critic, we derive a closed-form, differentiable CBF-style safety filter that minimally modifies the policy action to improve predicted safety, and integrate it into PPO with an anticipation loss to reduce policy-filter mismatch during training. In simulated quadruped locomotion, our method improves robustness under action noise, external perturbations, and terrain shifts, reducing catastrophic failures while maintaining competitive returns.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Despite the Markov assumption, safety failures in robot reinforcement learning rarely occur instantaneously; they typically emerge over several steps as the policy enters unfamiliar states under disturbances and domain shift. We address this limitation by learning a receding-horizon safety critic that predicts whether a state will remain non-terminating over the next receding horizon, enabling anticipatory detection of impending locomotion failures. The critic is trained from rollout-derived horizon labels with a composite objective that combines horizon classification, control-barrier-function-inspired temporal consistency, and smoothness regularization, and we aggregate an ensemble of critics with a conservative Conditional Value at Risk estimate to account for uncertainty. Building on the learned critic, we derive a closed-form, differentiable CBF-style safety filter that minimally modifies the policy action to improve predicted safety, and integrate it into PPO with an anticipation loss to reduce policy-filter mismatch during training. In simulated quadruped locomotion, our method improves robustness under action noise, external perturbations, and terrain shifts, reducing catastrophic failures while maintaining competitive returns.

</details>

---

## 48. ObjDream: Guiding Vision-Language-Action Models with Object-Level Understanding and Future State Prediction

**中文标题**: ObjDream: Guiding Vision-Language-Action Models with Object-Level Understanding and Future State Prediction

**作者**: Huailiang Ma, Yuxuan Li, Ziyi Zhao, Xinkai Wang, Mingyu Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20223v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20223v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20223v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for robotic manipulation, enabling robots to generate actions from visual observations and language instructions. However, most existing VLA models still operate on image-level visual representations inherited from pretrained vision-language backbones, while lacking fine-grained object understanding for manipulation. As a result, their robustness can degrade under out-of-distribution object layouts, clutter, and background variations. To address these limitations, we introduce ObjDream, an object-centric VLA framework centered on object-level understanding and future object state prediction. ObjDream uses a spatio-temporal object encoder to organize visual observations into temporal object slots, where persistent object identity and dynamic object state are represented through separate channels. These structured object representations are inserted into the token sequence of the vision-language model (VLM), where identity-guided routing modulates VLM attention to the corresponding object tokens. In addition, an action-conditioned object dynamics module predicts compact future object states relevant to manipulation during training, explicitly supervising object state evolution under robot actions without requiring future prediction at inference. Experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin 2.0 show that ObjDream improves generalization under diverse distribution shifts and scene perturbations. Real-world robot experiments further demonstrate that ObjDream transfers to physical manipulation settings with novel object arrangements and background changes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for robotic manipulation, enabling robots to generate actions from visual observations and language instructions. However, most existing VLA models still operate on image-level visual representations inherited from pretrained vision-language backbones, while lacking fine-grained object understanding for manipulation. As a result, their robustness can degrade under out-of-distribution object layouts, clutter, and background variations. To address these limitations, we introduce ObjDream, an object-centric VLA framework centered on object-level understanding and future object state prediction. ObjDream uses a spatio-temporal object encoder to organize visual observations into temporal object slots, where persistent object identity and dynamic object state are represented through separate channels. These structured object representations are inserted into the token sequence of the vision-language model (VLM), where identity-guided routing modulates VLM attention to the corresponding object tokens. In addition, an action-conditioned object dynamics module predicts compact future object states relevant to manipulation during training, explicitly supervising object state evolution under robot actions without requiring future prediction at inference. Experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin 2.0 show that ObjDream improves generalization under diverse distribution shifts and scene perturbations. Real-world robot experiments further demonstrate that ObjDream transfers to physical manipulation settings with novel object arrangements and background changes.

</details>

## 49. Atomic-WAM: Execution-Grounded Atomic-Motion Reasoning for World Action Models

**中文标题**: Atomic-WAM: Execution-Grounded Atomic-Motion Reasoning for World Action Models

**作者**: Shijun Shi, Zhe Liu, Zhi Hou, Yinan Chen, Tao Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20157v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20157v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20157v1)

**项目主页**: https://ssj9596.github.io/Atomic-WAM-project

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) typically adapt video foundation models to robotic control through joint video and action prediction. However, task-level instructions may leave the next physical interaction underspecified, requiring semantic grounding and state-dependent motion selection. To this end, we introduce Atomic-WAM, an understand-then-execute framework that connects task reasoning to action generation through atomic-motion descriptions. Specifically, a vision-language reasoner describes the next local motion from the task instruction and visual context, guiding a WAM to predict future video and an action chunk. Furthermore, an automatic annotation pipeline curates temporally aligned, contact-enriched supervision from multi-view DROID demonstrations. To make atomic-motion predictions executable by the WAM, we post-train the reasoner with Execution-Grounded GRPO (EG-GRPO). With the WAM fixed, EG-GRPO adapts the reasoner through rewards for motion appropriateness and execution success. Finally, Atomic-WAM achieves 39.6% task success under default instructions and 31.6% under vague instructions in a real-to-sim evaluation on RoboLab. Project page: https://ssj9596.github.io/Atomic-WAM-project

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) typically adapt video foundation models to robotic control through joint video and action prediction. However, task-level instructions may leave the next physical interaction underspecified, requiring semantic grounding and state-dependent motion selection. To this end, we introduce Atomic-WAM, an understand-then-execute framework that connects task reasoning to action generation through atomic-motion descriptions. Specifically, a vision-language reasoner describes the next local motion from the task instruction and visual context, guiding a WAM to predict future video and an action chunk. Furthermore, an automatic annotation pipeline curates temporally aligned, contact-enriched supervision from multi-view DROID demonstrations. To make atomic-motion predictions executable by the WAM, we post-train the reasoner with Execution-Grounded GRPO (EG-GRPO). With the WAM fixed, EG-GRPO adapts the reasoner through rewards for motion appropriateness and execution success. Finally, Atomic-WAM achieves 39.6% task success under default instructions and 31.6% under vague instructions in a real-to-sim evaluation on RoboLab. Project page: https://ssj9596.github.io/Atomic-WAM-project

</details>

## 50. Complementary Future Token Pruning for World Action Models

**中文标题**: Complementary Future Token Pruning for World Action Models

**作者**: Baekseung Kim, Junhoo Lee, Sohyeon Hong, Suhyun Jeon, Minkyu Kim et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19957v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19957v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19957v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) jointly predict future visual states and robot actions, but processing dense future-frame tokens incurs substantial inference latency. We propose CF-Prune, a training-free framework for pruning future-frame tokens in pretrained WAMs. Our key insight is that future visual predictions support action generation and therefore need not preserve every visual detail required for high-fidelity video synthesis. CF-Prune combines instruction and action attention to identify task-relevant tokens during the initial denoising forward pass, even when future-frame and action representations remain highly noisy. An entropy-based phase identification mechanism further retains dense computation during precision phases to mitigate pruning-induced performance degradation. Experiments on RoboLab, RoboCasa, and real-world manipulation tasks demonstrate consistent inference speedups. At a 35% pruning ratio, CF-Prune achieves average speedups of 1.19-1.27x while improving task success rates. At a 50% pruning ratio, it achieves 1.29-1.33x average speedups on both simulation benchmarks, with overall success rates within 1.0 percentage point of the baseline.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) jointly predict future visual states and robot actions, but processing dense future-frame tokens incurs substantial inference latency. We propose CF-Prune, a training-free framework for pruning future-frame tokens in pretrained WAMs. Our key insight is that future visual predictions support action generation and therefore need not preserve every visual detail required for high-fidelity video synthesis. CF-Prune combines instruction and action attention to identify task-relevant tokens during the initial denoising forward pass, even when future-frame and action representations remain highly noisy. An entropy-based phase identification mechanism further retains dense computation during precision phases to mitigate pruning-induced performance degradation. Experiments on RoboLab, RoboCasa, and real-world manipulation tasks demonstrate consistent inference speedups. At a 35% pruning ratio, CF-Prune achieves average speedups of 1.19-1.27x while improving task success rates. At a 50% pruning ratio, it achieves 1.29-1.33x average speedups on both simulation benchmarks, with overall success rates within 1.0 percentage point of the baseline.

</details>

## 51. Refine Actions If Needed: Adaptive Action Chunk Refinement for Robot Manipulation

**中文标题**: Refine Actions If Needed: Adaptive Action Chunk Refinement for Robot Manipulation

**作者**: Hanlin Liu, Zichen Zhou, Jiacheng Zhang, Liuyuan Na, Jin Jin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19765v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19765v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19765v1)

**项目主页**: https://rain-policy.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Computationally lightweight manipulation policies predict executable actions with low inference latency, but prediction errors can limit task success. Iterative generative policies can achieve higher task success rates, but repeated network evaluations increase inference latency. Refining action proposals from computationally lightweight policies can combine these strengths, but the amount of refinement needed varies across states. We introduce RAIN (Refine Actions If Needed), a framework that predicts action errors at different refinement levels to decide whether and how much to refine each executable proposal, before performing any refinement. We train an ensemble to predict these errors by comparing action proposals and their refined versions with expert actions from offline demonstrations, without requiring explicit labels of refinement needs. Calibration balances uncertainty, computation, and temporal consistency. We evaluate RAIN on eight ManiSkill tasks and four real-robot tasks. In simulation, our policy achieves 66.6% success, approaching the best evaluated diffusion-policy success rate with 5.71 times lower policy latency. On the real robot, success increases from 55.0% with Action Chunking with Transformers (ACT) to 72.5%, with comparable average successful completion time. Matched controls show that dynamic routing maintains the strongest observed fixed-refinement success level with 26.6% fewer refiner evaluations. Project page: https://rain-policy.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Computationally lightweight manipulation policies predict executable actions with low inference latency, but prediction errors can limit task success. Iterative generative policies can achieve higher task success rates, but repeated network evaluations increase inference latency. Refining action proposals from computationally lightweight policies can combine these strengths, but the amount of refinement needed varies across states. We introduce RAIN (Refine Actions If Needed), a framework that predicts action errors at different refinement levels to decide whether and how much to refine each executable proposal, before performing any refinement. We train an ensemble to predict these errors by comparing action proposals and their refined versions with expert actions from offline demonstrations, without requiring explicit labels of refinement needs. Calibration balances uncertainty, computation, and temporal consistency. We evaluate RAIN on eight ManiSkill tasks and four real-robot tasks. In simulation, our policy achieves 66.6% success, approaching the best evaluated diffusion-policy success rate with 5.71 times lower policy latency. On the real robot, success increases from 55.0% with Action Chunking with Transformers (ACT) to 72.5%, with comparable average successful completion time. Matched controls show that dynamic routing maintains the strongest observed fixed-refinement success level with 26.6% fewer refiner evaluations. Project page: https://rain-policy.github.io/.

</details>

---

## 52. Coordi-pi: Coordination-Aware Dual-Hand Action Representation for Bimanual Dexterous Manipulation

**中文标题**: Coordi-pi: Coordination-Aware Dual-Hand Action Representation for Bimanual Dexterous Manipulation

**作者**: Yiming Meng, Yanming Shao, Weijie Shen, Kunyang Lin, Jiayu Sun et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19753v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19753v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19753v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have shown strong potential for general-purpose robotic manipulation, but extending them to bimanual dexterous manipulation introduces a high-dimensional and structured action space. Existing bimanual VLA policies commonly concatenate right- and left-side arm--hand actions at each control timestep, leaving cross-hand interactions implicit within a single action vector. We introduce Coordi-$π$, a structured action representation for bimanual dexterous manipulation. Coordi-$π$ represents the right- and left-side arm--hand actions as individual action tokens and organizes them into an interleaved sequence, explicitly exposing the two sides within the action sequence. To explicitly encode hand identity and temporal synchronization in this interleaved representation, a learnable hand-identity embedding distinguishes the two sides, while sequence--control-timestep factorized rotary positional encoding (RoPE) separates sequence position from control timestep, allowing actions at the same control timestep to share a temporal coordinate while retaining distinct sequence positions. Across all five bimanual dexterous manipulation tasks in DexJoCo, Coordi-$π$ achieves state-of-the-art performance under both \textit{rand-obj} and \textit{rand-full}, improving the average success rate over $π_{0.5}$ from 31.9\% to 51.3\% and from 18.3\% to 32.3\%, respectively. Coordi-$π$ also consistently outperforms $π_{0.5}$ across four real-world bimanual dexterous manipulation tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have shown strong potential for general-purpose robotic manipulation, but extending them to bimanual dexterous manipulation introduces a high-dimensional and structured action space. Existing bimanual VLA policies commonly concatenate right- and left-side arm--hand actions at each control timestep, leaving cross-hand interactions implicit within a single action vector. We introduce Coordi-$π$, a structured action representation for bimanual dexterous manipulation. Coordi-$π$ represents the right- and left-side arm--hand actions as individual action tokens and organizes them into an interleaved sequence, explicitly exposing the two sides within the action sequence. To explicitly encode hand identity and temporal synchronization in this interleaved representation, a learnable hand-identity embedding distinguishes the two sides, while sequence--control-timestep factorized rotary positional encoding (RoPE) separates sequence position from control timestep, allowing actions at the same control timestep to share a temporal coordinate while retaining distinct sequence positions. Across all five bimanual dexterous manipulation tasks in DexJoCo, Coordi-$π$ achieves state-of-the-art performance under both \textit{rand-obj} and \textit{rand-full}, improving the average success rate over $π_{0.5}$ from 31.9\% to 51.3\% and from 18.3\% to 32.3\%, respectively. Coordi-$π$ also consistently outperforms $π_{0.5}$ across four real-world bimanual dexterous manipulation tasks.

</details>
