## 53. ForceBench: Benchmarking Force Integration for Robotic Manipulation

**中文标题**: ForceBench: Benchmarking Force Integration for Robotic Manipulation

**作者**: Xuan Han, Zinuo Jin, Tianlin Li, Yunfan Lou, Xin Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19604v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19604v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19604v1)

**项目主页**: https://forcebench-platform.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Force feedback provides vision-language-action (VLA) policies with physical observations that can guide action selection and execution. However, differences in tasks, demonstrations, and control settings across existing systems hinder reliable comparisons of force integration designs. We introduce ForceBench, a simulation benchmark for evaluating six-axis wrist-force integration under a unified protocol. ForceBench combines eight tasks spanning physical-property inference, resistance-dependent interaction, and contact-rich assembly with 1,600 synchronized demonstrations and task-specific success criteria. We compare one no-force baseline and six force integration designs on a shared $π_{0.5}$ backbone. Prompt Fusion achieves the highest observed mean simulation success of 35.31%, compared with 23.25% for the baseline, and improves success rates across all three task categories. Predictive and temporal gains in simulation concentrate on physical-property inference, while both temporal designs underperform the baseline on assembly. Real-world evaluation of eight systems across five tasks supports the main qualitative trends: Prompt Fusion and Dual-Path Fusion achieve 63.00% mean success, compared with 40.00% for the baseline, but only Prompt Fusion improves every task. These findings support ForceBench's practical value for identifying the benefits and limitations of force integration across manipulation demands. The project page is available at https://forcebench-platform.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Force feedback provides vision-language-action (VLA) policies with physical observations that can guide action selection and execution. However, differences in tasks, demonstrations, and control settings across existing systems hinder reliable comparisons of force integration designs. We introduce ForceBench, a simulation benchmark for evaluating six-axis wrist-force integration under a unified protocol. ForceBench combines eight tasks spanning physical-property inference, resistance-dependent interaction, and contact-rich assembly with 1,600 synchronized demonstrations and task-specific success criteria. We compare one no-force baseline and six force integration designs on a shared $π_{0.5}$ backbone. Prompt Fusion achieves the highest observed mean simulation success of 35.31%, compared with 23.25% for the baseline, and improves success rates across all three task categories. Predictive and temporal gains in simulation concentrate on physical-property inference, while both temporal designs underperform the baseline on assembly. Real-world evaluation of eight systems across five tasks supports the main qualitative trends: Prompt Fusion and Dual-Path Fusion achieve 63.00% mean success, compared with 40.00% for the baseline, but only Prompt Fusion improves every task. These findings support ForceBench's practical value for identifying the benefits and limitations of force integration across manipulation demands. The project page is available at https://forcebench-platform.github.io/.

</details>

## 54. RoboSpan: Learning Modular Coordination Policies for High-DoF Whole-Body Mobile Manipulation with Teleoperation

**中文标题**: RoboSpan: Learning Modular Coordination Policies for High-DoF Whole-Body Mobile Manipulation with Teleoperation

**作者**: Keqiang Ren, Xuecheng Ye, Hanyu Liu, Zhihua Wang, Tao Jian et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19594v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19594v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19594v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Whole-body mobile manipulation suffers from two fundamental challenges: inherent motion heterogeneity across locomotion, arm, and hand modules, and progressive error accumulation over long-horizon multi-stage tasks. Existing teleoperation platforms also lack integrated dexterous tactile sensing to support coordinated whole-body decision-making. This paper presents RoboSpan, a high-DoF whole-body teleoperation and demonstration platform, and the Hierarchical Modular Framework (HMF) for learning modular coordination policies. HMF functionally decouples the 29-DoF action space into fine hand manipulation, arm pose adjustment, and mobile-base spatial configuration branches, and introduces the Coordinated Modular Embodied Transformer (COMET) with inter-modular cross-attention to enable dynamic information exchange and action coordination. Vision-language model driven hierarchical task decomposition structurally suppresses temporal error propagation, while a full-palm 2D tactile representation encodes distributed contact signals for cross-module tactile empowerment. Experiments on a physical 29-DoF robot demonstrate that the proposed method achieves over 20\% higher task success rate than mainstream baselines across a range of long-horizon whole-body tasks, markedly improving stability and robustness in contact-rich manipulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Whole-body mobile manipulation suffers from two fundamental challenges: inherent motion heterogeneity across locomotion, arm, and hand modules, and progressive error accumulation over long-horizon multi-stage tasks. Existing teleoperation platforms also lack integrated dexterous tactile sensing to support coordinated whole-body decision-making. This paper presents RoboSpan, a high-DoF whole-body teleoperation and demonstration platform, and the Hierarchical Modular Framework (HMF) for learning modular coordination policies. HMF functionally decouples the 29-DoF action space into fine hand manipulation, arm pose adjustment, and mobile-base spatial configuration branches, and introduces the Coordinated Modular Embodied Transformer (COMET) with inter-modular cross-attention to enable dynamic information exchange and action coordination. Vision-language model driven hierarchical task decomposition structurally suppresses temporal error propagation, while a full-palm 2D tactile representation encodes distributed contact signals for cross-module tactile empowerment. Experiments on a physical 29-DoF robot demonstrate that the proposed method achieves over 20\% higher task success rate than mainstream baselines across a range of long-horizon whole-body tasks, markedly improving stability and robustness in contact-rich manipulation.

</details>

## 55. Dual-arm Cooperation for Tabletop Wiping with Temporal Object Removal

**中文标题**: Dual-arm Cooperation for Tabletop Wiping with Temporal Object Removal

**作者**: Pengqi Zhang, Longchang Yu, Daohan Li, Liang Yan, Hangxin Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19527v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19527v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19527v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We devise a dual-arm cooperative planning framework for wiping table surface in the presence of scattered objects. Unlike conventional surface-cleaning strategies that only cover free areas and detour around obstacles, our framework explicitly assigns one arm to rearrange objects while the other performs coverage wiping, thereby enabling access to previously obstructed regions. Specifically, we first construct a set of wiping and object-rearrangement action primitives. Based on these primitives, we introduce Geo-MILP, a geometry-aware task planner formulated as Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP). By integrating Logic-Based Benders Decomposition (LBBD), Geo-MILP iteratively optimizes action sequencingand timing to resolve geometric conflicts, promoting efficient cooperation between wiping and object rearrangement. We then employ K-Order Markov Optimization (KOMO) to generate smooth, collision-free motions to ensure feasible execution. Simulation and physical experiments demonstrate that the proposed framework not only increases reachable coverage by enabling access to obstructed areas, but also significantly improves time efficiency compared to Task and Motion Planning (TAMP) baselines, reducing planning time by up to 9 times and execution makespan by approximately 40%.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We devise a dual-arm cooperative planning framework for wiping table surface in the presence of scattered objects. Unlike conventional surface-cleaning strategies that only cover free areas and detour around obstacles, our framework explicitly assigns one arm to rearrange objects while the other performs coverage wiping, thereby enabling access to previously obstructed regions. Specifically, we first construct a set of wiping and object-rearrangement action primitives. Based on these primitives, we introduce Geo-MILP, a geometry-aware task planner formulated as Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP). By integrating Logic-Based Benders Decomposition (LBBD), Geo-MILP iteratively optimizes action sequencingand timing to resolve geometric conflicts, promoting efficient cooperation between wiping and object rearrangement. We then employ K-Order Markov Optimization (KOMO) to generate smooth, collision-free motions to ensure feasible execution. Simulation and physical experiments demonstrate that the proposed framework not only increases reachable coverage by enabling access to obstructed areas, but also significantly improves time efficiency compared to Task and Motion Planning (TAMP) baselines, reducing planning time by up to 9 times and execution makespan by approximately 40%.

</details>

## 56. A VR Training System for Robot-Assisted Vascular Intervention with Dual-Mode Control and Model-Based Interaction Response

**中文标题**: A VR Training System for Robot-Assisted Vascular Intervention with Dual-Mode Control and Model-Based Interaction Response

**作者**: Yining Shang, Shuxiang Guo, Sheng Cao, Pengcheng Li, Bin Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19413v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19413v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19413v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Virtual reality training for robot-assisted vascular intervention requires accurate robotic control and effective feedback on guidewire--vessel interactions. This paper presents a VR training system combining asynchronous dual-mode command mapping with sensorless interaction estimation. The system supports velocity-controlled navigation and position-driven incremental manipulation. Guidewire deformation and a Hunt--Crossley-type contact response are combined and temporally filtered to estimate interaction intensity, while trajectory-based visualization supports post-procedure review. Tests confirmed distinct command-dispatch patterns across three velocity gears and the position mode. During hybrid-scene navigation, the estimated response remained low during straight insertion and increased as the guidewire bent within the vascular model. Trajectory analysis yielded a model-derived cumulative trauma work (CTW) value of 15.166 in normalized units and localized regions of elevated interaction. In a preliminary study with 15 novices, physical-phantom task success increased from 53.3% to 93.3% after VR training, while mean completion time decreased from 132.4 s to 96.7 s. These results demonstrate the system's dual-mode operation, deformation-sensitive feedback, and potential to improve subsequent physical-phantom task performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Virtual reality training for robot-assisted vascular intervention requires accurate robotic control and effective feedback on guidewire--vessel interactions. During hybrid-scene navigation, the estimated response remained low during straight insertion and increased as the guidewire bent within the vascular model. These results demonstrate the system's dual-mode operation, deformation-sensitive feedback, and potential to improve subsequent physical-phantom task performance.

</details>

## 57. Revisiting Simple Baselines for Relational Deep Learning

**中文标题**: Revisiting Simple Baselines for Relational Deep Learning

**作者**: Viet The Nguyen, Vijay Prakash Dwivedi, Gunther Gust

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20534v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20534v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20534v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Relational deep learning has emerged as a powerful paradigm for learning end-to-end predictive models over relational databases by representing them as temporal heterogeneous graphs. Recent advances based on graph neural networks and Transformers have achieved strong performance across diverse domains. However, the effectiveness of simpler relational baselines remains underexplored, making it difficult to assess how much architectural complexity is needed to achieve strong predictive performance. Motivated by the observation that predictive signals arise from target attributes, relational attributes, and historical labels, we revisit how effectively these signals can be exploited using simple propagation methods. We adapt fixed low-pass feature propagation to relational entity graphs and train an MLP for prediction. The resulting predictions are further refined using Correct-and-Smooth, a graph-based label propagation algorithm that incorporates historical labels over task-specific meta-graphs connecting target entities. Across classification and regression tasks on RelBench, an MLP trained on neighborhood-filtered features outperforms one using only target-entity features on most tasks and recovers a substantial portion of the performance of heterogeneous GNNs. Correct-and-Smooth provides further gains from historical labels, although applying it requires a separately constructed meta-graph because connections among target entities are not directly specified by the relational entity graph. Together, our studies revisit the effectiveness of established propagation methods as simple RDL baselines while identifying canonical meta-graph construction as a key challenge for effective label propagation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Relational deep learning has emerged as a powerful paradigm for learning end-to-end predictive models over relational databases by representing them as temporal heterogeneous graphs. Recent advances based on graph neural networks and Transformers have achieved strong performance across diverse domains. However, the effectiveness of simpler relational baselines remains underexplored, making it difficult to assess how much architectural complexity is needed to achieve strong predictive performance. Motivated by the observation that predictive signals arise from target attributes, relational attributes, and historical labels, we revisit how effectively these signals can be exploited using simple propagation methods. Correct-and-Smooth provides further gains from historical labels, although applying it requires a separately constructed meta-graph because connections among target entities are not directly specified by the relational entity graph. Together, our studies revisit the effectiveness of established propagation methods as simple RDL baselines while identifying canonical meta-graph construction as a key challenge for effective label propagation.

</details>

## 58. Graph-Enhanced Disease Trajectory Discovery and Temporal Endpoint Forecasting from Longitudinal Electronic Health Records

**中文标题**: Graph-Enhanced Disease Trajectory Discovery and Temporal Endpoint Forecasting from Longitudinal Electronic Health Records

**作者**: Jin-Jia Zheng, Vincent S. Tseng

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20502v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20502v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20502v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Longitudinal electronic health records (EHRs) can reveal disease-progression structure, while useful forecasting requires leakage-controlled discovery, censor-aware outcomes, and inspectable trajectory evidence. We developed a graph-enhanced framework in MIMIC-IV v3.1 that splits patients before graph construction, learns train-only consecutive-admission transitions, and freezes endpoint-specific branching-motif policies selected on validation data. The final catalog contained 99,527 motifs, and a time-aware Transformer retrieved at most 24 patient-compatible motifs per endpoint through endpoint-specific cross-attention. Across five training seeds, micro-AUPRC increased from 0.9330 to 0.9357, while event-time MAE decreased from 676.9 to 657.7 days (reduction 19.2 days; 95% paired patient-cluster bootstrap CI, 13.8-24.8). Timing effects were heterogeneous: MAE worsened for events within 730 days but improved by 58.9 days for events occurring after 730 days. In an exploratory internal temporal split, branch conditioning reduced conditional event-time MAE by 26.9 days (95% CI, 23.0-30.8) without evidence of better micro-AUPRC. Corrected survival evaluation over the supported 0-2,555-day interval did not establish macro C-index or integrated Brier score superiority over a matched history-only discrete-hazard model. A directed, degree-matched GNN repair substantially improved unseen-edge discovery, yet replacing GNN-derived scores within fixed motif identities did not improve downstream forecasting. These results position branching motifs as structured trajectory context whose main value is long-range timing support rather than uniformly stronger endpoint discrimination, survival prediction, or causal explanation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Longitudinal electronic health records (EHRs) can reveal disease-progression structure, while useful forecasting requires leakage-controlled discovery, censor-aware outcomes, and inspectable trajectory evidence. We developed a graph-enhanced framework in MIMIC-IV v3.1 that splits patients before graph construction, learns train-only consecutive-admission transitions, and freezes endpoint-specific branching-motif policies selected on validation data. The final catalog contained 99,527 motifs, and a time-aware Transformer retrieved at most 24 patient-compatible motifs per endpoint through endpoint-specific cross-attention. In an exploratory internal temporal split, branch conditioning reduced conditional event-time MAE by 26.9 days (95% CI, 23.0-30.8) without evidence of better micro-AUPRC. Corrected survival evaluation over the supported 0-2,555-day interval did not establish macro C-index or integrated Brier score superiority over a matched history-only discrete-hazard model. </details>

## 59. NeuroDETR: Multiscale Discrete Spatiotemporal Tokenization for Asynchronous EEG Event Detection

**中文标题**: NeuroDETR: Multiscale Discrete Spatiotemporal Tokenization for Asynchronous EEG Event Detection

**作者**: Yaozhi Wen, Kun Zhao, Wei Wei, Haoyang Chen, Kangning Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20349v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20349v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20349v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Asynchronous EEG event detection supports practical brain-computer interfaces by identifying event classes and temporal boundaries directly from continuous recordings without predefined event boundaries at inference time. The primary challenge lies in extracting event-related patterns embedded in ongoing background activity. To address this, we propose \textbf{NeuroDETR}, a discrete spatiotemporal multiscale framework that combines structured tokenization with event-aware feature refinement. Multiscale temporal codebooks capture brief responses and sustained dynamics, while Spatial-Pyramid VQ hierarchically encodes spatial structure across EEG channels. These complementary representations form a compact event memory for jointly classifying events and estimating their onsets and offsets. Before tokenization, we apply FOCAL, an event-aware sparse attention module that uses predicted event presence to guide temporal feature refinement and spatial aggregation, emphasizing likely event periods while preserving context across the recording. Experiments on eight EEG datasets that span diverse paradigms demonstrate state-of-the-art performance. These findings provide a basis for the development of BCI systems that recognize meaningful neural activity during continuous interaction and monitoring.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Asynchronous EEG event detection supports practical brain-computer interfaces by identifying event classes and temporal boundaries directly from continuous recordings without predefined event boundaries at inference time. The primary challenge lies in extracting event-related patterns embedded in ongoing background activity. To address this, we propose \textbf{NeuroDETR}, a discrete spatiotemporal multiscale framework that combines structured tokenization with event-aware feature refinement. Multiscale temporal codebooks capture brief responses and sustained dynamics, while Spatial-Pyramid VQ hierarchically encodes spatial structure across EEG channels. These complementary representations form a compact event memory for jointly classifying events and estimating their onsets and offsets. Before tokenization, we apply FOCAL, an event-aware sparse attention module that uses predicted event presence to guide temporal feature refinement and spatial aggregation, emphasizing likely event periods while preserving context across the recording. Experiments on eight EEG datasets that span diverse paradigms demonstrate state-of-the-art performance. These findings provide a basis for the development of BCI systems that recognize meaningful neural activity during continuous interaction and monitoring.

</details>

## 60. Adversarial Reinforcement Learning for Robust Planetary Landing

**中文标题**: Adversarial Reinforcement Learning for Robust Planetary Landing

**作者**: Miguel Leiva-Vélez, Nicolas Gaston-Rozado, Andrea Scorsoglio, Hodei Urrutxua, Víctor Rodríguez-Fernández

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.20252v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20252v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.20252v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, eess.SY

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Deep reinforcement learning (DRL) policies for planetary landing and other aerospace guidance, navigation, and control tasks are commonly trained under randomized disturbances to improve robustness. However, typical stochastic perturbation sampling assumes independent variations, whereas real mission disturbances often exhibit spatial and temporal correlation and cannot be reproduced through independent random draws. This work investigates adversarial reinforcement learning to discover challenging off-nominal conditions. A secondary agent generates physically constrained perturbations that degrade the performance of a landing controller, forming an asymmetric sequential zero-sum game. Training proceeds in three stages: baseline controller learning, robustness enhancement with stochastic perturbations, and an iterative adversarial phase in which the controller and disturbance generator are alternately optimized. Preliminary experiments in a three-degree-of-freedom landing environment show that adversarial disturbances reveal vulnerabilities missed by conventional randomization and provide early indications that, with a more extensive training campaign, the approach can yield improved robustness and stronger generalization under previously unseen perturbation regimes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Deep reinforcement learning (DRL) policies for planetary landing and other aerospace guidance, navigation, and control tasks are commonly trained under randomized disturbances to improve robustness. However, typical stochastic perturbation sampling assumes independent variations, whereas real mission disturbances often exhibit spatial and temporal correlation and cannot be reproduced through independent random draws. This work investigates adversarial reinforcement learning to discover challenging off-nominal conditions. A secondary agent generates physically constrained perturbations that degrade the performance of a landing controller, forming an asymmetric sequential zero-sum game. Training proceeds in three stages: baseline controller learning, robustness enhancement with stochastic perturbations, and an iterative adversarial phase in which the controller and disturbance generator are alternately optimized. Preliminary experiments in a three-degree-of-freedom landing environment show that adversarial disturbances reveal vulnerabilities missed by conventional randomization and provide early indications that, with a more extensive training campaign, the approach can yield improved robustness and stronger generalization under previously unseen perturbation regimes.

</details>

## 61. CTIA-ODE: Continuous-Time Inter-Variable Attention for Long-Term Time Series Forecasting

**中文标题**: CTIA-ODE: Continuous-Time Inter-Variable Attention for Long-Term Time Series Forecasting

**作者**: Chengyi Pan, Huangqimei Zheng, Hao Xue

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19831v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19831v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19831v1)

**项目主页**: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/CTIA-ODE-15A6/.

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Although multivariate time series forecasting has broad application prospects, effectively incorporating exogenous variables remains challenging. Prevailing discrete-time architectures often impose rigid temporal alignment across heterogeneous variables, which may limit their ability to capture continuously evolving external effects. To address this limitation, we introduce CTIA-ODE, a continuous-time forecasting framework centered on Continuous-Time Inter-Variable Attention (CTIA). Specifically, CTIA is embedded within an ordinary differential equation (ODE) solver, enabling the continuously evolving endogenous trajectory to dynamically query a static exogenous context along the integration path without requiring explicit temporal alignment. To improve the stability of the continuous latent dynamics, we further introduce a dual-stabilization strategy: architecturally, a Linear Residual Augmentation (LRA) module provides a macroscopic trend anchor, while an Outlier-Robust Hybrid Loss (ORHL) mitigates the influence of heavy-tailed anomalies during optimization. Extensive experiments across eight real-world datasets demonstrate that CTIA-ODE achieves state-of-the-art forecasting performance across diverse datasets and prediction horizons, while maintaining a favorable accuracy-efficiency trade-off with a compact memory footprint and low inference latency, particularly under long-horizon forecasting settings. Anonymous code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/CTIA-ODE-15A6/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Although multivariate time series forecasting has broad application prospects, effectively incorporating exogenous variables remains challenging. Prevailing discrete-time architectures often impose rigid temporal alignment across heterogeneous variables, which may limit their ability to capture continuously evolving external effects. To address this limitation, we introduce CTIA-ODE, a continuous-time forecasting framework centered on Continuous-Time Inter-Variable Attention (CTIA). Specifically, CTIA is embedded within an ordinary differential equation (ODE) solver, enabling the continuously evolving endogenous trajectory to dynamically query a static exogenous context along the integration path without requiring explicit temporal alignment. To improve the stability of the continuous latent dynamics, we further introduce a dual-stabilization strategy: architecturally, a Linear Residual Augmentation (LRA) module provides a macroscopic trend anchor, while an Outlier-Robust Hybrid Loss (ORHL) mitigates the influence of heavy-tailed anomalies during optimization. Extensive experiments across eight real-world datasets demonstrate that CTIA-ODE achieves state-of-the-art forecasting performance across diverse datasets and prediction horizons, while maintaining a favorable accuracy-efficiency trade-off with a compact memory footprint and low inference latency, particularly under long-horizon forecasting settings. Anonymous code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/CTIA-ODE-15A6/.

</details>

---

## 62. Digital Self-Interference Cancellation for In-Band Full-Duplex Systems Based on Transformer Neural Operators

**中文标题**: Digital Self-Interference Cancellation for In-Band Full-Duplex Systems Based on Transformer Neural Operators

**作者**: Zhijie Su, Shiquan Zhang, Qiaolin He

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19490v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19490v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19490v1)

**类别**: cs.IT, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Digital-domain self-interference cancellation is essential in in-band full-duplex radios after passive suppression and analog cancellation. It is particularly challenging when the desired signal is strong relative to the residual self-interference. In this regime, conventional iterative digital cancellers update their coefficients from desired-signal-contaminated received samples and may suffer substantial performance degradation. This paper reformulates digital-domain self-interference cancellation as an operator-learning problem. The proposed framework learns the temporal evolution of the residual self-interference channel from historical observations and uses the learned operator together with the known local transmit signal to reconstruct the current residual self-interference. A Transformer neural operator is used to realize this framework. The resulting method is trained in a desired-signal-free setting and then applied in the presence of a desired signal. A continuity-based analysis characterizes the sensitivity of the learned operator to the resulting input perturbation. Numerical experiments in static and time-varying channels, including time-varying SIR conditions, demonstrate stable cancellation performance over a wide range of interference levels. At 30 dB SIR, the proposed method improves the cancellation performance by 5--15 dB over the considered conventional methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Digital-domain self-interference cancellation is essential in in-band full-duplex radios after passive suppression and analog cancellation. It is particularly challenging when the desired signal is strong relative to the residual self-interference. In this regime, conventional iterative digital cancellers update their coefficients from desired-signal-contaminated received samples and may suffer substantial performance degradation. This paper reformulates digital-domain self-interference cancellation as an operator-learning problem. The proposed framework learns the temporal evolution of the residual self-interference channel from historical observations and uses the learned operator together with the known local transmit signal to reconstruct the current residual self-interference. A Transformer neural operator is used to realize this framework. The resulting method is trained in a desired-signal-free setting and then applied in the presence of a desired signal. A continuity-based analysis characterizes the sensitivity of the learned operator to the resulting input perturbation. Numerical experiments in static and time-varying channels, including time-varying SIR conditions, demonstrate stable cancellation performance over a wide range of interference levels. At 30 dB SIR, the proposed method improves the cancellation performance by 5--15 dB over the considered conventional methods.

</details>

## 63. State-Matched Sequence Conformal Prediction

**中文标题**: State-Matched Sequence Conformal Prediction

**作者**: Ruihang Han, Li-Hsiang Lin

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19484v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19484v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19484v1)

**类别**: stat.ML, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Sequential conformal prediction needs to decide which past errors should calibrate uncertainty for the current target. Recency and online error feedback are useful when recent observations remain relevant, but the recent past can be a poor reference when comparable states recur at nonadjacent times, with the recurrence pattern generally unknown. We propose State-Matched Sequence Conformal Prediction (SMSCP), which constructs an explicit one-sided local-linear state from pre-target information, uses state similarity to select a matched reference set of past calibration scores, and then applies the ordinary unweighted conformal order statistic. In contrast to existing approaches, SMSCP uses state similarity to identify calibration observations relevant to the target score quantile. This motivates a theoretical analysis of whether nearby states have comparable target score quantiles under temporal dependence. We establish control of the resulting conformal cutoff and characterize predictive information not captured by the chosen state. Simulations show that state matching is most effective when score-relevant states recur across nonadjacent times, while revealing settings in which its advantage weakens or reverses. Across solar irradiance and bike-demand benchmarks, SMSCP improves interval score over recency, weighted, adaptive, and HopCPT comparators, and achieves coverage closer to the nominal 0.90 level than ResCP on five of six benchmarks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recency and online error feedback are useful when recent observations remain relevant, but the recent past can be a poor reference when comparable states recur at nonadjacent times, with the recurrence pattern generally unknown. We propose State-Matched Sequence Conformal Prediction (SMSCP), which constructs an explicit one-sided local-linear state from pre-target information, uses state similarity to select a matched reference set of past calibration scores, and then applies the ordinary unweighted conformal order statistic. In contrast to existing approaches, SMSCP uses state similarity to identify calibration observations relevant to the target score quantile. This motivates a theoretical analysis of whether nearby states have comparable target score quantiles under temporal dependence. We establish control of the resulting conformal cutoff and characterize predictive information not captured by the chosen state. Across solar irradiance and bike-demand benchmarks, SMSCP improves interval score over recency, weighted, adaptive, and HopCPT comparators, and achieves coverage closer to the nominal 0.90 level than ResCP on five of six benchmarks.

</details>

## 64. Dynamic Graph World Modelling: Learning Future Dynamics from Temporal Context

**中文标题**: Dynamic Graph World Modelling: Learning Future Dynamics from Temporal Context

**作者**: Bo Li, Yue Yang, Yujin Huang, Siao Liu, David Lemphers et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-14

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19364v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19364v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19364v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic graph learning models how entities and their relations evolve from historical observations to predict future graph states. Existing methods do not fully address dynamic graph world modelling, which requires learning transitions from temporal context to future latent graph states. This is difficult for three reasons: nodes and relations rely on different historical evidence, structural evolution is path-dependent, and future snapshots provide sparse and realization-specific targets. We propose Dynamic Graph World Modelling (DyG-WM), the first framework to jointly predict future node and relation representations from temporal context by answering three questions: what has evolved, how it evolved, and what will evolve next. To capture what has evolved, a historical node-relation context encoder summarizes node-neighborhood trajectories and pair-specific histories. To characterize how it evolved, a node-relation path-signature encoder captures ordered structural changes. To predict what will evolve next, a dual-level joint-embedding objective predicts target-snapshot node and relation representations without reconstructing the raw graph. Experiments on 12 link-prediction and two node-classification datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of DyG-WM across both tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dynamic graph learning models how entities and their relations evolve from historical observations to predict future graph states. Existing methods do not fully address dynamic graph world modelling, which requires learning transitions from temporal context to future latent graph states. This is difficult for three reasons: nodes and relations rely on different historical evidence, structural evolution is path-dependent, and future snapshots provide sparse and realization-specific targets. We propose Dynamic Graph World Modelling (DyG-WM), the first framework to jointly predict future node and relation representations from temporal context by answering three questions: what has evolved, how it evolved, and what will evolve next. To capture what has evolved, a historical node-relation context encoder summarizes node-neighborhood trajectories and pair-specific histories. To characterize how it evolved, a node-relation path-signature encoder captures ordered structural changes. To predict what will evolve next, a dual-level joint-embedding objective predicts target-snapshot node and relation representations without reconstructing the raw graph. Experiments on 12 link-prediction and two node-classification datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of DyG-WM across both tasks.

</details>

## 65. Kandinsky S2V 1.0: Audio-Driven Avatar Video Generation

**中文标题**: Kandinsky S2V 1.0: Audio-Driven Avatar Video Generation

**作者**: Nikolay Semenov, Artem Riabinin, Sergey Bassenko, Anna Averchenkova, Julia Agafonova et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19290v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19290v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19290v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present Kandinsky S2V 1.0, an open family of speech-to-video models for controllable audio-driven human animation, comprising Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite (2B parameters) and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro (21B parameters). From a single reference image, a speech waveform, and a text prompt, the models generate long-form 25 FPS video in which lip motion, expression, pose, and gesture follow the speech. The architecture draws text and audio conditioning from one Qwen2.5-Omni model and adds a reference-aware three-scale visual memory. Training starts from the Kandinsky 5.0 Video foundation models [1] and combines mixed reference-memory layouts with an area-adaptive face loss. The training corpus contains 1.17M single-speaker clips selected by active-speaker detection, with captions generated directly in English and Russian. A side-by-side human evaluation against the strongest open-source baseline and two proprietary commercial models shows that Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro performs comparably to or better than these models on average and is also preferred for lip synchronization. On common speech to video benchmarks, Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro rank among the top performing models according to mean rank over five reported metrics. Beyond the base models, we provide π-Flow distilled variants of both Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro. The distilled Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro model generates video in 16 sampling steps without classifier-free guidance, achieving a 5.4 times end-to-end speedup over the base model, while matching or exceeding it on nearly all reported benchmark metrics. To accelerate open research and deployment in speech-to-video generation, we release the code, model checkpoints, and diffusers integration under the MIT license.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

From a single reference image, a speech waveform, and a text prompt, the models generate long-form 25 FPS video in which lip motion, expression, pose, and gesture follow the speech. The architecture draws text and audio conditioning from one Qwen2.5-Omni model and adds a reference-aware three-scale visual memory. Training starts from the Kandinsky 5.0 Video foundation models [1] and combines mixed reference-memory layouts with an area-adaptive face loss. The training corpus contains 1.17M single-speaker clips selected by active-speaker detection, with captions generated directly in English and Russian. Beyond the base models, we provide π-Flow distilled variants of both Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Lite and Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro. The distilled Kandinsky S2V 1.0 Pro model generates video in 16 sampling steps without classifier-free guidance, achieving a 5.4 times end-to-end speedup over the base model, while matching or exceeding it on nearly all reported benchmark metrics. To accelerate open research and deployment in speech-to-video generation, we release the code, model checkpoints, and diffusers integration under the MIT license.

</details>

## 66. Deep Learning for Atmospheric Turbulence Mitigation: A Comparison Between GANs and Transformers

**中文标题**: Deep Learning for Atmospheric Turbulence Mitigation: A Comparison Between GANs and Transformers

**作者**: Juan S. Carrillo, Jérôme Gilles

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19274v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19274v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19274v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Atmospheric turbulence creates complex spatial and temporal distortions that significantly reduce image quality. This work compares two deep learning methods for turbulence reduction: Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) and Transformer-based models. Both methods are trained and tested on the SOTIS dataset, which includes multi-frame sequences under different turbulence conditions with ground truth references. Our results show an important finding: GAN-based models perform better in severely degraded, low-quality situations, while Transformer-based models achieve better results in moderate to high-quality settings, especially in correcting geometric distortions. We also enhance the Turbulence Mitigation Transformer (TMT) by introducing a refined training strategy, an augmented input design that includes a temporally averaged frame as prior structural information, and a modified loss function that incorporates the Structural Similarity Index (SSIM). These improvements lead to consistent performance gains across various turbulence conditions. Overall, this study provides an in-depth evaluation of deep learning methods for turbulence reduction and offers practical guidance for selecting models in different imaging environments.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Atmospheric turbulence creates complex spatial and temporal distortions that significantly reduce image quality. This work compares two deep learning methods for turbulence reduction: Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) and Transformer-based models. Both methods are trained and tested on the SOTIS dataset, which includes multi-frame sequences under different turbulence conditions with ground truth references. Our results show an important finding: GAN-based models perform better in severely degraded, low-quality situations, while Transformer-based models achieve better results in moderate to high-quality settings, especially in correcting geometric distortions. We also enhance the Turbulence Mitigation Transformer (TMT) by introducing a refined training strategy, an augmented input design that includes a temporally averaged frame as prior structural information, and a modified loss function that incorporates the Structural Similarity Index (SSIM). These improvements lead to consistent performance gains across various turbulence conditions. Overall, this study provides an in-depth evaluation of deep learning methods for turbulence reduction and offers practical guidance for selecting models in different imaging environments.

</details>

## 67. Long-horizon Video Generation with Slot Memory Addressed By Region Tokens

**中文标题**: Long-horizon Video Generation with Slot Memory Addressed By Region Tokens

**作者**: Eren Cetin, Julius Erbach, Vukasin Bozic, Hyojun Go, Zixiang Zhao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19257v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19257v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19257v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Memory for long-horizon video generation must satisfy two competing requirements: it must remain bounded so generation can continue indefinitely, while still recovering what was observed at a place when the camera returns, regardless of how long ago it was seen. Reconstruction-based memories provide spatial recall by caching and reprojecting explicit geometry, but require growing storage and depend on estimated 3D structure. Recurrent memories keep a constant-size state, but retention is governed by temporal recurrence, causing old observations to fade with time. We introduce SMART, a slot memory addressed by region tokens, which combines bounded storage with spatially addressed recall. SMART stores content in a constant-size slot recurrence whose writes are addressed by the observing camera and whose reads are queried by the rendering camera, without constructing an explicit 3D representation or maintaining a growing cache. Consequently, recall is conditioned on inter-view geometry rather than on recency. Each read additionally provides a confidence estimate that conditions a chunk-causal diffusion transformer, allowing it to selectively trust retrieved content while relying on its generative prior elsewhere. On long trajectories in Memory Maze and Minecraft, SMART maintains high-fidelity recall far beyond its training horizon while using constant memory and per-step cost.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Memory for long-horizon video generation must satisfy two competing requirements: it must remain bounded so generation can continue indefinitely, while still recovering what was observed at a place when the camera returns, regardless of how long ago it was seen. Reconstruction-based memories provide spatial recall by caching and reprojecting explicit geometry, but require growing storage and depend on estimated 3D structure. Recurrent memories keep a constant-size state, but retention is governed by temporal recurrence, causing old observations to fade with time. We introduce SMART, a slot memory addressed by region tokens, which combines bounded storage with spatially addressed recall. SMART stores content in a constant-size slot recurrence whose writes are addressed by the observing camera and whose reads are queried by the rendering camera, without constructing an explicit 3D representation or maintaining a growing cache. Consequently, recall is conditioned on inter-view geometry rather than on recency. Each read additionally provides a confidence estimate that conditions a chunk-causal diffusion transformer, allowing it to selectively trust retrieved content while relying on its generative prior elsewhere. On long trajectories in Memory Maze and Minecraft, SMART maintains high-fidelity recall far beyond its training horizon while using constant memory and per-step cost.

</details>

## 68. Into the LidarVerse: A Real-Time Unified LiDAR World Model Across Tasks and Sensors

**中文标题**: Into the LidarVerse: A Real-Time Unified LiDAR World Model Across Tasks and Sensors

**作者**: Henry Che, Xuanchi Ren, Sanja Fidler, Shenlong Wang, Laura Leal-Taixé et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19201v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19201v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19201v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present LidarVerse, a real-time unified LiDAR world model for sensor simulation across tasks and sensors. Existing LiDAR simulators largely follow one of two complementary paradigms: reconstruction-based methods preserve the geometry of a captured scene but require lengthy per-scene optimization, whereas generative methods amortize learning across data but lack the consistency needed for long-horizon rollouts. LidarVerse brings both paradigms into a single, unified conditional generative framework. A universal tokenizer maps LiDAR range sequences into a shared latent space, where a bidirectional teacher learns a common interface for conditioning on a LiDAR prefix, an HD-Map, a persistent spatial cache, and optionally, text. Varying the available scene evidence turns the same model into a generator of new worlds, a point-cloud forecaster, or a generalizable resimulator of observed scenes without per-scene optimization. We distill the teacher into a four-step autoregressive causal student and maintain both a rolling temporal K/V cache and a world-aligned spatial cache, enabling persistent long-horizon rollouts with real-time inference at 11 FPS on a single H100 GPU. LidarVerse achieves state-of-the-art performance on most reported metrics across all three evaluation protocols, while its tokenizer supports 32-, 64-, and 128-beam LiDAR and generalizes to an unseen scanning pattern. These results demonstrate that generative and reconstruction-grounded LiDAR simulation can be realized within one model, providing the core capabilities for future closed-loop sensor simulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present LidarVerse, a real-time unified LiDAR world model for sensor simulation across tasks and sensors. Existing LiDAR simulators largely follow one of two complementary paradigms: reconstruction-based methods preserve the geometry of a captured scene but require lengthy per-scene optimization, whereas generative methods amortize learning across data but lack the consistency needed for long-horizon rollouts. LidarVerse brings both paradigms into a single, unified conditional generative framework. A universal tokenizer maps LiDAR range sequences into a shared latent space, where a bidirectional teacher learns a common interface for conditioning on a LiDAR prefix, an HD-Map, a persistent spatial cache, and optionally, text. We distill the teacher into a four-step autoregressive causal student and maintain both a rolling temporal K/V cache and a world-aligned spatial cache, enabling persistent long-horizon rollouts with real-time inference at 11 FPS on a single H100 GPU. LidarVerse achieves state-of-the-art performance on most reported metrics across all three evaluation protocols, while its tokenizer supports 32-, 64-, and 128-beam LiDAR and generalizes to an unseen scanning pattern. These results demonstrate that generative and reconstruction-grounded LiDAR simulation can be realized within one model, providing the core capabilities for future closed-loop sensor simulation.

</details>

## 69. The Gap Is in the Geometry: A Convergent Meta-Analysis of Multi-Facet Aerial and Remote Sensing Video Understanding

**中文标题**: The Gap Is in the Geometry: A Convergent Meta-Analysis of Multi-Facet Aerial and Remote Sensing Video Understanding

**作者**: Georgios Angelidis, Aggelos Psiris, Maria Vakalopoulou, Georgios Th. Papadopoulos, Giorgos Sfikas et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19102v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19102v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19102v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Aerial and remote sensing video is increasingly evaluated like web video, by posing multiple-choice questions to multimodal large language models (MLLMs) over sampled frames and reporting accuracy. This paper argues that this recipe measures the wrong aspect, and supports the claim with a convergent meta-analysis of a broad set of published results, organized in four evidence tiers, in which a claim becomes a position only when collected evidence agrees. Parameter scale exhibits a median of +3.4 accuracy points per tenfold increase on aerial benchmarks, far below a human gap of at least 19 points, whereas acquisition conditions control perception, as 315 of 318 evaluations in 19 within-paper contrasts lose accuracy when conditions such as altitude, view, object size or time horizon become harder. No aerial benchmark in the corpus combines video input with per-task human and random baselines, competence collapses from answering to acting in closed-loop navigation, and the same MLLMs score higher on the aerial benchmark than on ground spatial ones, but lower on studies that test cross-view geometry. From these findings the current study derives five falsifiable principles and proposes the AVU-Card, a reporting card for aerial video benchmarks

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Aerial and remote sensing video is increasingly evaluated like web video, by posing multiple-choice questions to multimodal large language models (MLLMs) over sampled frames and reporting accuracy. This paper argues that this recipe measures the wrong aspect, and supports the claim with a convergent meta-analysis of a broad set of published results, organized in four evidence tiers, in which a claim becomes a position only when collected evidence agrees. Parameter scale exhibits a median of +3.4 accuracy points per tenfold increase on aerial benchmarks, far below a human gap of at least 19 points, whereas acquisition conditions control perception, as 315 of 318 evaluations in 19 within-paper contrasts lose accuracy when conditions such as altitude, view, object size or time horizon become harder. No aerial benchmark in the corpus combines video input with per-task human and random baselines, competence collapses from answering to acting in closed-loop navigation, and the same MLLMs score higher on the aerial benchmark than on ground spatial ones, but lower on studies that test cross-view geometry. From these findings the current study derives five falsifiable principles and proposes the AVU-Card, a reporting card for aerial video benchmarks

</details>

## 70. WorldMemBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Memory of Video World Models

**中文标题**: WorldMemBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Memory of Video World Models

**作者**: Nan Zhou, Jianxiong Gao, Qiuyu Wang, Wen Wang, Jiahao Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19063v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19063v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19063v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As video models enable longer and more realistic interactive experiences, maintaining a world consistent with its history becomes essential. This requires both preserving previously observed content and ensuring coherent world evolution over time. World memory is a critical capability to evaluate alongside visual fidelity and video duration. We introduce WorldMemBench, a benchmark for systematically evaluating world memory in video world models. Across 260 test cases, it assesses static memory through the retention of human identity and appearance, object appearance and geometry, and environmental content and geometry, and dynamic memory through event progression and state persistence. Using controlled camera departures and multi-revisit trajectories, the benchmark evaluates static content against fixed initial references and dynamic events against expected state transitions. It combines appearance and geometry metrics with event-specific assessments, and reports video quality and camera-following accuracy as complementary diagnostics. Evaluation of nine representative models shows that strong static retention does not imply reliable dynamic state evolution, while repeated revisits reveal memory degradation beyond the first return. Human studies support the alignment between our automatic evaluations and human judgments. WorldMemBench provides a systematic testbed for diagnosing memory failures and guiding the development of persistent, temporally coherent video worlds.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 71. Focus, Align, and Diffuse: Time-Series-Aware Keyframe Diffusion for Cardiac Dynamic Synthesis from Sparse Observations

**中文标题**: Focus, Align, and Diffuse: Time-Series-Aware Keyframe Diffusion for Cardiac Dynamic Synthesis from Sparse Observations

**作者**: Junkai Liu, Haofan Wu, Nay Aung, Joao A. C. Lima, Steffen E. Petersen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19050v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19050v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19050v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dense cardiac dynamics are essential for assessing cardiac function, yet in practice clinicians often observe only extremely sparse measurements. However, recovering full dynamics from such sparsity is a highly ill-posed problem due to the lack of temporal cues. While auxiliary time-series signals like ECG provide physiological guidance, effectively exploiting them is challenging due to the cross-modal representation gap and phase misalignment. In this work, we present CardioFAD, a Focus--Align--Diffuse paradigm that reformulates sparse cardiac dynamic synthesis as a recoverability--alignability anchor discovery problem. Our core insight is that resolving extreme temporal ambiguity requires a compact set of anchor states that are both sufficient to recover the visual trajectory and reliable for ECG--visual alignment. We therefore learn motion-salient keyframes under two complementary principles: trajectory sufficiency, which preserves trajectory-critical intra-modal motion content, and anchor alignment, which promotes consistent inter-modal correspondence at these anchors. We implement the Focus and Align modules optimized by the two aspects. Diffuse then performs two-stage generation by first synthesizing keyframes and subsequently interpolating the remaining frames. Extensive experiments on cardiac MRI and echocardiography videos demonstrate superior visual quality, temporal coherence, and improved functional consistency under the extreme one-shot regime.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dense cardiac dynamics are essential for assessing cardiac function, yet in practice clinicians often observe only extremely sparse measurements. However, recovering full dynamics from such sparsity is a highly ill-posed problem due to the lack of temporal cues. While auxiliary time-series signals like ECG provide physiological guidance, effectively exploiting them is challenging due to the cross-modal representation gap and phase misalignment. In this work, we present CardioFAD, a Focus--Align--Diffuse paradigm that reformulates sparse cardiac dynamic synthesis as a recoverability--alignability anchor discovery problem. Our core insight is that resolving extreme temporal ambiguity requires a compact set of anchor states that are both sufficient to recover the visual trajectory and reliable for ECG--visual alignment. We therefore learn motion-salient keyframes under two complementary principles: trajectory sufficiency, which preserves trajectory-critical intra-modal motion content, and anchor alignment, which promotes consistent inter-modal correspondence at these anchors. We implement the Focus and Align modules optimized by the two aspects. Diffuse then performs two-stage generation by first synthesizing keyframes and subsequently interpolating the remaining frames. Extensive experiments on cardiac MRI and echocardiography videos demonstrate superior visual quality, temporal coherence, and improved functional consistency under the extreme one-shot regime.

</details>

---

## 72. EgoExplorer: A Vision-Motion-Touch Observatory for Human Loco-Manipulation In Situ

**中文标题**: EgoExplorer: A Vision-Motion-Touch Observatory for Human Loco-Manipulation In Situ

**作者**: Yunsong Zhou, Xuekun Jiang, Haiqi Shen, Hangxu Liu, Chaojian Shi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18872v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18872v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18872v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Learning humanoid loco-manipulation from human demonstrations rests on observing how locomotion, posture, and object interaction evolve together as tasks unfold. Scaling collection across diverse environments, in turn, favors lightweight, portable capture systems. Yet, existing datasets provide only partial views of this coordination. Thus, we introduce EgoExplorer, a 100-hour vision-motion-touch observatory of human loco-manipulation built with a wearable system. With an emphasis on professional activities, its continuous recordings align egocentric video with reconstructed whole-body motion, fine-grained finger articulation, and tactile observations, accompanied by step-level descriptions. To achieve this coverage with sparse body sensing, we develop the Calibrate-Complete-Constrain pipeline. Concretely, reusable calibration fixes participant geometry and sensor attachments before motion inference to disentangle mounting offsets from articulation. A temporal prior then completes unobserved joint motion, while layered constraints promote measurement agreement, temporal continuity, and geometric consistency, properties that motion plausibility alone does not guarantee. Against tracker-fitted references, the method reduces body-point error by 71.9% relative to the strongest baseline; matched evaluations also show gains in hand stability and contact estimation. Preliminary studies explore multimodal generation and conditioning alongside humanoid retargeting with whole-body control in simulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Learning humanoid loco-manipulation from human demonstrations rests on observing how locomotion, posture, and object interaction evolve together as tasks unfold. Scaling collection across diverse environments, in turn, favors lightweight, portable capture systems. Yet, existing datasets provide only partial views of this coordination. Thus, we introduce EgoExplorer, a 100-hour vision-motion-touch observatory of human loco-manipulation built with a wearable system. With an emphasis on professional activities, its continuous recordings align egocentric video with reconstructed whole-body motion, fine-grained finger articulation, and tactile observations, accompanied by step-level descriptions. To achieve this coverage with sparse body sensing, we develop the Calibrate-Complete-Constrain pipeline. Concretely, reusable calibration fixes participant geometry and sensor attachments before motion inference to disentangle mounting offsets from articulation. A temporal prior then completes unobserved joint motion, while layered constraints promote measurement agreement, temporal continuity, and geometric consistency, properties that motion plausibility alone does not guarantee. Against tracker-fitted references, the method reduces body-point error by 71.9% relative to the strongest baseline; matched evaluations also show gains in hand stability and contact estimation. Preliminary studies explore multimodal generation and conditioning alongside humanoid retargeting with whole-body control in simulation.

</details>

## 73. Visual Tokens on Demand: Visual Budgeting for Scalable and Efficient Multi-Image Reasoning in Multimodal LLMs

**中文标题**: Visual Tokens on Demand: Visual Budgeting for Scalable and Efficient Multi-Image Reasoning in Multimodal LLMs

**作者**: Aimon Rahman, Joymallya Chakraborty, Vidit Bansal, Abhishek Dan

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18858v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18858v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18858v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal large language models have advanced rapidly, but most remain optimized for single-image inputs. Multi-image reasoning is costly because each image adds many visual tokens, increasing encoding and attention demands. Existing techniques that address the above issue, including visual token pruning, token merging, visual token compression, learned resampling, hierarchical compression, retrieval-augmented visual reasoning, and memory-based architectures, have largely been designed for single-image scenarios, where redundancy is primarily spatial and local. Multi-image reasoning introduces additional challenges: redundancy may occur across images, task-relevant evidence may be sparse or distributed, and aggressive per-image compression can remove details needed for comparison, counting, temporal reasoning, retrieval, or evidence grounding. Moreover, existing token-reduction methods are often either (i) training-free but inference-inefficient, typically relying on accessing attention matrices to get token-importance that may be impractical to access with memory-efficient attention implementations such as FlashAttention, or (ii) training-dependent, requiring the base MLLM to be retrained or adapted to a new compressed-token paradigm. In this work, we study scalable multi-image reasoning and understanding without retraining the base model and without accessing attention matrices at inference time. To this end, we propose mmVB, a lightweight plug-and-play module that assigns query-conditioned per-image token budgets via cross-image context, adding only 1.6M trainable parameters while reducing visual input tokens by 9x. This reduction yields 5x faster time-to-first-token and 7x fewer prefill FLOPs, while maintaining 94.4% of full-resolution accuracy across eight multi-image reasoning benchmarks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multimodal large language models have advanced rapidly, but most remain optimized for single-image inputs. Multi-image reasoning is costly because each image adds many visual tokens, increasing encoding and attention demands. Existing techniques that address the above issue, including visual token pruning, token merging, visual token compression, learned resampling, hierarchical compression, retrieval-augmented visual reasoning, and memory-based architectures, have largely been designed for single-image scenarios, where redundancy is primarily spatial and local. Multi-image reasoning introduces additional challenges: redundancy may occur across images, task-relevant evidence may be sparse or distributed, and aggressive per-image compression can remove details needed for comparison, counting, temporal reasoning, retrieval, or evidence grounding. Moreover, existing token-reduction methods are often either (i) training-free but inference-inefficient, typically relying on accessing attention matrices to get token-importance that may be impractical to access with memory-efficient attention implementations such as FlashAttention, or (ii) training-dependent, requiring the base MLLM to be retrained or adapted to a new compressed-token paradigm. In this work, we study scalable multi-image reasoning and understanding without retraining the base model and without accessing attention matrices at inference time. To this end, we propose mmVB, a lightweight plug-and-play module that assigns query-conditioned per-image token budgets via cross-image context, adding only 1.6M trainable parameters while reducing visual input tokens by 9x. This reduction yields 5x faster time-to-first-token and 7x fewer prefill FLOPs, while maintaining 94.4% of full-resolution accuracy across eight multi-image reasoning benchmarks.

</details>

## 74. RepProbe: Can Video Language Models Count Repeated Actions? A Diagnostic Benchmark

**中文标题**: RepProbe: Can Video Language Models Count Repeated Actions? A Diagnostic Benchmark

**作者**: Mahsa Khoshnoodi, Sarah Adel Bargal

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18844v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18844v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18844v1)

**项目主页**: https://arenaa.github.io/RepProbe-webpage/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Repetition counting requires identifying and enumerating action cycles over time. While state-of-the-art video language models describe actions fluently, they fail to reliably count how many times an action repeats. To investigate this limitation, we introduce RepProbe, a benchmark of 42K real and synthetic video clips containing exactly k in [1, 20] complete repetitions, with controls for clip position, temporal coverage, and duration. These controls allow us to study the counting failure directly and locate where in the model it arises. Counting accuracy collapses at high counts across the video language models we evaluate, and controlled experiments show this collapse is not explained by frame sampling alone. We compare model outputs with linear probes trained on the final-layer representations used to generate those outputs. We find a consistent representation-to-output dissociation: substantial count information remains linearly decodable even when models fail to report the correct count. Further analysis shows this failure arises primarily at the output stage, where answers concentrate on a small set of preferred numbers rather than the available count information. The dissociation holds on both real and synthetic videos and is not explained by clip duration, position, or scene content. These results indicate that models encode repetition structure but do not reliably report it in their outputs. Project page: https://arenaa.github.io/RepProbe-webpage/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Repetition counting requires identifying and enumerating action cycles over time. While state-of-the-art video language models describe actions fluently, they fail to reliably count how many times an action repeats. To investigate this limitation, we introduce RepProbe, a benchmark of 42K real and synthetic video clips containing exactly k in [1, 20] complete repetitions, with controls for clip position, temporal coverage, and duration. These controls allow us to study the counting failure directly and locate where in the model it arises. Counting accuracy collapses at high counts across the video language models we evaluate, and controlled experiments show this collapse is not explained by frame sampling alone. We compare model outputs with linear probes trained on the final-layer representations used to generate those outputs. We find a consistent representation-to-output dissociation: substantial count information remains linearly decodable even when models fail to report the correct count. Further analysis shows this failure arises primarily at the output stage, where answers concentrate on a small set of preferred numbers rather than the available count information. The dissociation holds on both real and synthetic videos and is not explained by clip duration, position, or scene content. These results indicate that models encode repetition structure but do not reliably report it in their outputs. Project page: https://arenaa.github.io/RepProbe-webpage/

</details>

## 75. Plan-Bench: Can Existing VLMs Go Well Beyond Perception to Reason and Plan in Embodied Worlds?

**中文标题**: Plan-Bench: Can Existing VLMs Go Well Beyond Perception to Reason and Plan in Embodied Worlds?

**作者**: Juntong Feng, Qingan Liu, Jingru Dong, Yue Zhu, Weiming Zheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18836v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18836v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18836v2)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/MinneFeng/Plan-Bench.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have achieved strong performance in visual perception and spatial reasoning, leading to growing interest in using them as high-level planners for embodied AI. However, physical tasks require models to integrate spatial information, reason over temporal states, and generate multi-step plans under various constraints, raising the question of whether current VLMs can reliably support such reasoning and planning independently of execution. We introduce Plan-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating the high-level reasoning and multi-step planning capabilities of VLMs relevant to embodied worlds, while isolating the influence of action execution. It covers three complementary dimensions, spanning 16 subclasses and 699 curated samples. Extensive evaluation of 28 VLMs reveals room for improvement, with even the strongest model achieving only 75.0% accuracy. We further find that model scaling generally improves these capabilities. Ablation studies also indicate larger models show increasing potential as high-level planners. Overall, Plan-Bench provides a lightweight probe for evaluating VLMs' reasoning and planning capabilities relevant to embodied environments and assessing their potential as decision makers for physical tasks. Our benchmark and code are available at https://github.com/MinneFeng/Plan-Bench.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have achieved strong performance in visual perception and spatial reasoning, leading to growing interest in using them as high-level planners for embodied AI. However, physical tasks require models to integrate spatial information, reason over temporal states, and generate multi-step plans under various constraints, raising the question of whether current VLMs can reliably support such reasoning and planning independently of execution. We introduce Plan-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating the high-level reasoning and multi-step planning capabilities of VLMs relevant to embodied worlds, while isolating the influence of action execution. It covers three complementary dimensions, spanning 16 subclasses and 699 curated samples. Extensive evaluation of 28 VLMs reveals room for improvement, with even the strongest model achieving only 75.0% accuracy. We further find that model scaling generally improves these capabilities. Ablation studies also indicate larger models show increasing potential as high-level planners. Overall, Plan-Bench provides a lightweight probe for evaluating VLMs' reasoning and planning capabilities relevant to embodied environments and assessing their potential as decision makers for physical tasks. Our benchmark and code are available at https://github.com/MinneFeng/Plan-Bench.

</details>

## 76. OmniCompass: A Holistic Benchmark for Omni-Modal Video Grounding and Understanding

**中文标题**: OmniCompass: A Holistic Benchmark for Omni-Modal Video Grounding and Understanding

**作者**: Xuyang Liu, Shihao Wang, Xinyu Wei, Minghan Li, Zeqing Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18829v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18829v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18829v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

With the rapid development of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), evaluating their video understanding capabilities has become increasingly important. Existing video understanding benchmarks cover only part of the evaluation dimensions, limiting systematic diagnosis of omni-modal video understanding. We introduce OmniCompass, a holistic benchmark for omni-modal video grounding and understanding. OmniCompass defines two evaluation tracks: OmniCompass-VL assesses vision-language models on tasks requiring visual evidence, while OmniCompass-AV assesses omni-modal models on tasks requiring joint audio-visual evidence. Each track includes three complementary tasks: temporal grounding (TG), spatial grounding (SG), and question answering (QA), assessing when a referenced target appears, where it is, and what happens. QA covers five perception and three reasoning categories in OmniCompass-VL, and three perception and two reasoning categories in OmniCompass-AV. OmniCompass-VL contains 820 videos and 2,460 instances across domains and three duration types: short, medium, and long. A narrative- and audio-rich subset forms OmniCompass-AV with 240 videos and 720 instances. Each instance is evaluated under two paired settings: text-only and text+image, specifying the same target through text and visual-language guidance. All instances are manually annotated with multi-round quality verification. Evaluations of 50 MLLMs show that open-source models still lag behind proprietary counterparts, particularly in audio-visual understanding. Precise temporal and spatial grounding remains challenging even for leading models, and their predictions still exhibit inconsistencies across the paired target specifications.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

With the rapid development of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), evaluating their video understanding capabilities has become increasingly important. Evaluations of 50 MLLMs show that open-source models still lag behind proprietary counterparts, particularly in audio-visual understanding. </details>
