## 77. GAD: Geometry Attention Distillation for Consistent Video Generation

**中文标题**: GAD: Geometry Attention Distillation for Consistent Video Generation

**作者**: Junfeng Ni, Ziyu Zhu, Fan Jiang, Yu Liu, Chiyu Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18779v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18779v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18779v1)

**项目主页**: https://fantasy-amap.github.io/GAD/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video diffusion models generate visually compelling short-term sequences but often struggle to preserve scene geometry and object structure over time. In this paper, we first demonstrate that current video attention cannot identify reliable correspondences across frames, with responses dispersed across multiple regions even after recognizable scene structure has emerged. We argue that reliable cross-frame associations are essential for information exchange as scene structure forms in video generation. Thus, we introduce Geometry Attention Distillation (GAD), which transfers correspondences from a frozen geometry model's attention to video attention. Extracted from clean videos, these correspondences link the same static surface or moving object across frames. Since structure formation begins in deeper layers at higher noise and shallower layers at lower noise, we apply attention distillation at a student layer selected according to the noise timestep. After training, video attention focuses more consistently on corresponding locations. Experiments across all three video backbones demonstrate improved long-range scene consistency and dynamic temporal consistency, with geometric gains across generation lengths. On Wan2.2 5B, GAD improves cross-frame matching accuracy by at least 30% over the Wan baseline on both long-range benchmarks. Notably, GAD achieves these gains without additional inference modules, offering a promising approach to efficient and consistent video-based world simulation. Project page: https://fantasy-amap.github.io/GAD/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video diffusion models generate visually compelling short-term sequences but often struggle to preserve scene geometry and object structure over time. After training, video attention focuses more consistently on corresponding locations. On Wan2.2 5B, GAD improves cross-frame matching accuracy by at least 30% over the Wan baseline on both long-range benchmarks. Notably, GAD achieves these gains without additional inference modules, offering a promising approach to efficient and consistent video-based world simulation. Project page: https://fantasy-amap.github.io/GAD/.

</details>

## 78. ST-GRU PredRNN-V2: A GRU-Based Reformulation of PredRNN-V2 for Video Prediction

**中文标题**: ST-GRU PredRNN-V2: A GRU-Based Reformulation of PredRNN-V2 for Video Prediction

**作者**: Augusta van Haren, Hanna Hoogen, Luca Pattavina, Quincy Rommen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18645v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18645v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18645v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video prediction requires models to capture complex spatiotemporal dependencies and to retain relevant information over long sequences. PredRNN and its successor PredRNN-V2 address this challenge with spatiotemporal Long Short-Term Memory (ST-LSTM) units that maintain two separate memory states, at the cost of a relatively complex recurrent building block. In this work, we investigate whether the simpler gating of Gated Recurrent Units (GRUs) can be transferred to spatiotemporal predictive learning. To this end, we develop a spatiotemporal GRU (ST-GRU) unit as a GRU-based alternative to the ST-LSTM. The ST-GRU replaces the ST-LSTM's three gates per memory state with two GRU-style gates (update and reset), and merges the temporal memory state into the hidden state, which reduces the number of parameters by about 5%. We incorporate the ST-GRU into PredRNN-V2 and evaluate the resulting model on the Moving MNIST video prediction benchmark, following the original experimental protocol. Averaged over ten predicted frames, the ST-GRU-based PredRNN-V2 achieves an MSE of 55.1, an SSIM of 0.883, an LPIPS of 0.065 and a PSNR of 19.53dB. It does not surpass the original ST-LSTM-based PredRNN-V2 on MSE, SSIM or PSNR, but it achieves a lower LPIPS and performs on par with the earlier ST-LSTM-based PredRNN, while using a simpler recurrent cell. These results suggest that ST-GRUs are a promising building block for spatiotemporal predictive learning and motivate further work on their design and integration.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video prediction requires models to capture complex spatiotemporal dependencies and to retain relevant information over long sequences. PredRNN and its successor PredRNN-V2 address this challenge with spatiotemporal Long Short-Term Memory (ST-LSTM) units that maintain two separate memory states, at the cost of a relatively complex recurrent building block. In this work, we investigate whether the simpler gating of Gated Recurrent Units (GRUs) can be transferred to spatiotemporal predictive learning. To this end, we develop a spatiotemporal GRU (ST-GRU) unit as a GRU-based alternative to the ST-LSTM. The ST-GRU replaces the ST-LSTM's three gates per memory state with two GRU-style gates (update and reset), and merges the temporal memory state into the hidden state, which reduces the number of parameters by about 5%. We incorporate the ST-GRU into PredRNN-V2 and evaluate the resulting model on the Moving MNIST video prediction benchmark, following the original experimental protocol. Averaged over ten predicted frames, the ST-GRU-based PredRNN-V2 achieves an MSE of 55.1, an SSIM of 0.883, an LPIPS of 0.065 and a PSNR of 19.53dB. It does not surpass the original ST-LSTM-based PredRNN-V2 on MSE, SSIM or PSNR, but it achieves a lower LPIPS and performs on par with the earlier ST-LSTM-based PredRNN, while using a simpler recurrent cell. These results suggest that ST-GRUs are a promising building block for spatiotemporal predictive learning and motivate further work on their design and integration.

</details>

## 79. MajorTOM:Elliot Pretrain, A Multi-Sensor, Multi-Temporal Pretraining Dataset for Earth Observation

**中文标题**: MajorTOM:Elliot Pretrain, A Multi-Sensor, Multi-Temporal Pretraining Dataset for Earth Observation

**作者**: Carmen Oliver Huidobro, Mikolaj Czerkawski, Kevin Monsalvez-Pozo, Cesar Aybar, Gustau Camps-Valls et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18624v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18624v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18624v1)

**项目主页**: https://source.coop/major-tom/elliot-pretrain.

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Pretraining generalist Earth observation foundation models requires data that combine several sensors, varied temporal sampling and, increasingly, text, yet open datasets rarely provide all three in one consistent, easily accessible form. We introduce MajorTOM:Elliot Pretrain, a collection of 279,166 tiles of 10 km drawn from the Major-TOM grid by clustering AlphaEarth embeddings. Each tile stacks Sentinel-2 L1C, Landsat-8/9, Sentinel-1 RTC, Copernicus DEM and ESA WorldCover on a shared 1056x1056-pixel, 10 m grid, complemented by cloud masks, OpenStreetMap entities and ERA5 weather. The tiles are partitioned into three temporal regimes, Monotemporal (250,000 tiles, one acquisition), Monthly (12,500 tiles, twelve acquisitions spread over the annual cycle) and Burst (16,666 tiles, six acquisitions within 30 days), and are released as 6.3 TB of Cloud Optimized GeoTIFFs indexed by TACO v3 Parquet metadata, a standard that allows cloud streaming, efficient data access, and filtering before any pixel is read. A companion library derives sensor-tagged fact sheets, captions and 16 families of vision-language tasks from these layers at load time. We test our design choices in short pretraining runs of OlmoEarth-Base. First, a 25% subset of Major-TOM Core-S2L2A curated with AlphaEarth embeddings matches or exceeds the full corpus on 8 of 9 benchmarks. Second, on Elliot-Pretrain, adding Landsat-8/9 improves or matches Sentinel-2 only pretraining on every benchmark. Third, using DEM and WorldCover as prediction targets during pretraining further improves 8 of 9 benchmarks, while giving the same maps to the encoder as inputs degrades 5 of 9. Fourth, Sentinel-1 helps on 7 of 9 benchmarks, particularly on benchmarks with radar inputs. Fifth, rule-based captions match a small language model at no cost. The dataset is openly available at https://source.coop/major-tom/elliot-pretrain.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 80. VideoJev: System One Model for Video Understanding and Interaction

**中文标题**: VideoJev: System One Model for Video Understanding and Interaction

**作者**: Yuhan Zhu, Xiangyu Zeng, Qingyi Si, Chenxu Yang, Yifei Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18615v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18615v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18615v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Visual agents must turn video observations into timely decisions to track progress and choose what to do next. Building on the emerging System One paradigm, we introduce VideoJev, a family of models that cast video understanding and interaction as structured decision tasks. We adapt pretrained multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by replacing autoregressive answer decoding with a shared head that scores temporal intervals and textual options against video evidence. These scores yield event spans or answer choices, unifying temporal grounding and multiple-choice video question answering. This combines the semantic flexibility of MLLMs with efficient decisions that agents can use directly. As an interaction unfolds, new goals and intermediate results prompt further queries about the same visual history. VideoJev therefore reuses visual computation through Cached inference for queries as they arrive and Joint inference for multiple queries in a single forward pass. Across three temporal grounding benchmarks, VideoJev-4B and VideoJev-35B-A3B improve mean mIoU over their backbones by 6.7 and 10.8 points, respectively, with measured inference speedups of $2.8$-$3.1\times$. On four video QA benchmarks, both achieve performance comparable to their backbones in Thinking mode, with measured inference speedups exceeding $100\times$. These results offer a unified, reusable decision interface for composing structured visual judgments into complex, open-ended workflows.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Visual agents must turn video observations into timely decisions to track progress and choose what to do next. Building on the emerging System One paradigm, we introduce VideoJev, a family of models that cast video understanding and interaction as structured decision tasks. We adapt pretrained multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by replacing autoregressive answer decoding with a shared head that scores temporal intervals and textual options against video evidence. These scores yield event spans or answer choices, unifying temporal grounding and multiple-choice video question answering. This combines the semantic flexibility of MLLMs with efficient decisions that agents can use directly. As an interaction unfolds, new goals and intermediate results prompt further queries about the same visual history. VideoJev therefore reuses visual computation through Cached inference for queries as they arrive and Joint inference for multiple queries in a single forward pass. Across three temporal grounding benchmarks, VideoJev-4B and VideoJev-35B-A3B improve mean mIoU over their backbones by 6.7 and 10.8 points, respectively, with measured inference speedups of $2.8$-$3.1\times$. On four video QA benchmarks, both achieve performance comparable to their backbones in Thinking mode, with measured inference speedups exceeding $100\times$. These results offer a unified, reusable decision interface for composing structured visual judgments into complex, open-ended workflows.

</details>

## 81. Observation Quality in DSM-Assisted Aerial Localization: A Benchmark and Base-Anchored Uncertainty Calibration

**中文标题**: Observation Quality in DSM-Assisted Aerial Localization: A Benchmark and Base-Anchored Uncertainty Calibration

**作者**: Pengyu Yin, Junxiang Tan, Jie Pan, Mengqi Fu, Kun Cheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18564v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18564v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18564v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Aerial map matching provides position observations for navigation, but a position error alone does not determine how an observation should be weighted. We define five benchmark tasks: position availability and agreement, grouped error structure, predictive uncertainty, controlled updates, and temporal accumulation. A case study in digital-surface-model-assisted localization evaluates base-anchored uncertainty calibration within this framework. The method combines propagated image and map sensitivities with the second moment of a compact configuration subset relative to the reported position. The reported position remains unchanged, and scale is fitted using separate position references without local surface-height ground truth. Thirteen covariance models are evaluated on two held-out query sets from locations represented in the calibration data. Relative to a constant full-matrix baseline, the method reduces equal-location normalized position-update risk by 7.70% on 728 conditions and 5.46% on a separate 142 conditions under an independent prior, with all localization failures included. On the primary set, equal-location negative log likelihood improves by 2.571 nat. These results show improved reference-based weighting at seen locations. Exploratory base-quality and conditional-addition controls leave the incremental update benefit of configuration disagreement unresolved. Navigation benefit and real temporal accumulation remain untested.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Aerial map matching provides position observations for navigation, but a position error alone does not determine how an observation should be weighted. We define five benchmark tasks: position availability and agreement, grouped error structure, predictive uncertainty, controlled updates, and temporal accumulation. A case study in digital-surface-model-assisted localization evaluates base-anchored uncertainty calibration within this framework. The method combines propagated image and map sensitivities with the second moment of a compact configuration subset relative to the reported position. The reported position remains unchanged, and scale is fitted using separate position references without local surface-height ground truth. Thirteen covariance models are evaluated on two held-out query sets from locations represented in the calibration data. Relative to a constant full-matrix baseline, the method reduces equal-location normalized position-update risk by 7.70% on 728 conditions and 5.46% on a separate 142 conditions under an independent prior, with all localization failures included. On the primary set, equal-location negative log likelihood improves by 2.571 nat. These results show improved reference-based weighting at seen locations. Exploratory base-quality and conditional-addition controls leave the incremental update benefit of configuration disagreement unresolved. Navigation benefit and real temporal accumulation remain untested.

</details>

## 82. Meta-Video: Efficient Standardized Evaluation for Video Understanding

**中文标题**: Meta-Video: Efficient Standardized Evaluation for Video Understanding

**作者**: Soumya Shamarao Jahagirdar, Anna Kukleva, Vishaal Udandarao, Samuel Thomas, Brian Kingsbury et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18535v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18535v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18535v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video understanding benchmarks are the primary yardstick for measuring progress in video-language modeling, and a growing body of work relies on them to claim improvements. The reported benchmark scores in these works, however, are almost always produced by frameworks (e.g., lmms eval) used as black boxes: certain evaluation choices like frame sampling, preprocessing, and input formatting are inherited from preset defaults and seldom inspected carefully. We show these unexamined choices are a major source of variance. Even for the same model checkpoint, evaluation configuration alone can shift accuracy by up to 19.5pp. Hence, it is unclear whether reported gains reflect genuine improvements in capability or differences in evaluation setup. Moreover, many benchmark samples offer little evidence of visual capability, since they can be answered without the video, and the full suites are expensive to evaluate. We address these issues in two parts. First, we curate existing suites for discriminability and efficiency, producing Meta-Video, a balanced 1,000-question benchmark over 791 videos spanning diverse tasks and durations, which largely preserves model rankings, at 5 times inference speedup for a full-frame count sweep experiment (4-128 frames). Second, we establish a standardized evaluation protocol by sweeping the configuration space to isolate choices that measurably affect results, distill them into best practices and reporting standards, and release reproducible reference scores across five models and seven benchmarks. We hope that the proposed evaluation framework and the Meta-Video benchmark will support video understanding research by providing a reproducible and efficient foundation to measure progress in a reliable way.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video understanding benchmarks are the primary yardstick for measuring progress in video-language modeling, and a growing body of work relies on them to claim improvements. The reported benchmark scores in these works, however, are almost always produced by frameworks (e.g., lmms eval) used as black boxes: certain evaluation choices like frame sampling, preprocessing, and input formatting are inherited from preset defaults and seldom inspected carefully. We show these unexamined choices are a major source of variance. Even for the same model checkpoint, evaluation configuration alone can shift accuracy by up to 19.5pp. Hence, it is unclear whether reported gains reflect genuine improvements in capability or differences in evaluation setup. Moreover, many benchmark samples offer little evidence of visual capability, since they can be answered without the video, and the full suites are expensive to evaluate. We address these issues in two parts. First, we curate existing suites for discriminability and efficiency, producing Meta-Video, a balanced 1,000-question benchmark over 791 videos spanning diverse tasks and durations, which largely preserves model rankings, at 5 times inference speedup for a full-frame count sweep experiment (4-128 frames). Second, we establish a standardized evaluation protocol by sweeping the configuration space to isolate choices that measurably affect results, distill them into best practices and reporting standards, and release reproducible reference scores across five models and seven benchmarks. We hope that the proposed evaluation framework and the Meta-Video benchmark will support video understanding research by providing a reproducible and efficient foundation to measure progress in a reliable way.

</details>

## 83. Dual-Domain Sparse-View 4D CBCT Reconstruction with Spatial-Temporal Consistency Learning

**中文标题**: Dual-Domain Sparse-View 4D CBCT Reconstruction with Spatial-Temporal Consistency Learning

**作者**: Qi Lai, YuTong He

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18518v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18518v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18518v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Four-dimensional cone-beam computed tomography (4D CBCT) provides phase-resolved volumetric images for image-guided radiotherapy. Yet good reconstruction quality usually needs many projections. This increases scan time and radiation dose. We propose STCNet, a sparse-view 4D CBCT reconstruction framework that uses spatial-temporal consistency learning. STCNet reconstructs high-quality images from only 16 projections in each respiratory phase. STCNet integrates physics-driven positional encoding to enforce geometric consistency in the projection domain and introduces temporal encoding in latent space to capture respiratory motion dynamics across phases to capture dynamic respiratory motion across phases. A dual-domain collaborative strategy jointly optimizes both projection and image domains, enhancing structural fidelity and temporal coherence. We tested STCNet on the TCIA-LUNG dataset and a CIRS thorax phantom dataset. STCNet performed better than conventional methods and recent deep learning baselines in image quality, artifact reduction, and structural preservation. On TCIA-LUNG, STCNet reached PSNR values of 33.311, 32.695, and 32.658 dB at phases 2, 5, and 8. The corresponding SSIM values were 0.845, 0.823, and 0.825. On CIRS, STCNet achieved PSNR values up to 36.497 dB and SSIM values up to 0.877. These results support STCNet as a promising framework for low-dose, phase-resolved 4D CBCT reconstruction in image-guided radiotherapy.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Four-dimensional cone-beam computed tomography (4D CBCT) provides phase-resolved volumetric images for image-guided radiotherapy. Yet good reconstruction quality usually needs many projections. This increases scan time and radiation dose. We propose STCNet, a sparse-view 4D CBCT reconstruction framework that uses spatial-temporal consistency learning. STCNet reconstructs high-quality images from only 16 projections in each respiratory phase. STCNet integrates physics-driven positional encoding to enforce geometric consistency in the projection domain and introduces temporal encoding in latent space to capture respiratory motion dynamics across phases to capture dynamic respiratory motion across phases. A dual-domain collaborative strategy jointly optimizes both projection and image domains, enhancing structural fidelity and temporal coherence. We tested STCNet on the TCIA-LUNG dataset and a CIRS thorax phantom dataset. STCNet performed better than conventional methods and recent deep learning baselines in image quality, artifact reduction, and structural preservation. On TCIA-LUNG, STCNet reached PSNR values of 33.311, 32.695, and 32.658 dB at phases 2, 5, and 8. The corresponding SSIM values were 0.845, 0.823, and 0.825. On CIRS, STCNet achieved PSNR values up to 36.497 dB and SSIM values up to 0.877. These results support STCNet as a promising framework for low-dose, phase-resolved 4D CBCT reconstruction in image-guided radiotherapy.

</details>

## 84. EgoOmniWorld: Unifying Egocentric Understanding via Ego-Action-World Interactions

**中文标题**: EgoOmniWorld: Unifying Egocentric Understanding via Ego-Action-World Interactions

**作者**: Songruo Chen, Yuqian Fu, Yu Li, Mingkang Dong, Sifan Zhou et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18499v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18499v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18499v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Egocentric video understanding spans diverse capabilities, modalities, and evaluation formats, yet these are often studied in isolation despite their tight coupling in real-world interaction. We propose the Ego-Action-World interaction loop as a unifying principle: the wearer perceives the world, forms goals, acts, changes the world, and uses feedback to guide subsequent behavior. Based on this view, we introduce EgoOmniWorld, a benchmark organized around four complementary interaction structures: Node captures ego, action, and world states; Edge captures directed relations among them; Chain captures linked dependencies; and Loop captures feedback-closed interactions. We develop an interaction-first, evidence-grounded data pipeline that extracts structured interactions from egocentric videos, grounds them in visual and auditory evidence, and generates questions according to their interaction structure. Required modalities and single- or multi-turn formats follow naturally from the supporting evidence and dependencies. EgoOmniWorld contains 24,166 QA groups, including 20,284 training and 3,882 test samples. Experiments on representative open- and closed-source multimodal large language models reveal substantial and uneven limitations across interaction structures. Models trained on EgoOmniWorld improve in-domain interaction understanding and generalize to existing egocentric benchmarks. EgoOmniWorld provides a unified foundation for evaluating and advancing egocentric video understanding. Data, code, and models will be released.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Egocentric video understanding spans diverse capabilities, modalities, and evaluation formats, yet these are often studied in isolation despite their tight coupling in real-world interaction. We propose the Ego-Action-World interaction loop as a unifying principle: the wearer perceives the world, forms goals, acts, changes the world, and uses feedback to guide subsequent behavior. Based on this view, we introduce EgoOmniWorld, a benchmark organized around four complementary interaction structures: Node captures ego, action, and world states; Edge captures directed relations among them; Chain captures linked dependencies; and Loop captures feedback-closed interactions. We develop an interaction-first, evidence-grounded data pipeline that extracts structured interactions from egocentric videos, grounds them in visual and auditory evidence, and generates questions according to their interaction structure. Required modalities and single- or multi-turn formats follow naturally from the supporting evidence and dependencies. EgoOmniWorld contains 24,166 QA groups, including 20,284 training and 3,882 test samples. Experiments on representative open- and closed-source multimodal large language models reveal substantial and uneven limitations across interaction structures. Models trained on EgoOmniWorld improve in-domain interaction understanding and generalize to existing egocentric benchmarks. EgoOmniWorld provides a unified foundation for evaluating and advancing egocentric video understanding. Data, code, and models will be released.

</details>

## 85. Simplifying and Improving On-Policy Distillation for Few-Step Diffusion

**中文标题**: Simplifying and Improving On-Policy Distillation for Few-Step Diffusion

**作者**: Jiawei Zhang, Ziyu Wan, Nanye Ma, Long Zhao, Dong Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18472v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18472v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18472v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG, eess.IV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Few-step distillation reduces the sampling cost of diffusion and flow models, but existing approaches often require auxiliary score models or specialized architectures. We show that simple on-policy velocity regression already provides a strong foundation for 4-NFE generation. To represent teacher velocity variation within each coarse transition, we introduce Pivot, a training-only residual proxy that separates local corrections from the student's interval-mean prediction. We further repel the student from weaker teacher predictions, providing a lightweight mechanism for avoiding undesirable modes. The resulting framework, Simplified Matching Distillation (SMD), remains entirely regression-based, requires neither online score estimation nor adversarial training, and leaves the deployed generator unchanged. We evaluate SMD on image generation and editing, 3D generation, video synthesis, and joint audio--video generation, scaling to 33 billion parameters. At 4-NFE, SMD outperforms strong few-step baselines and surpasses multi-step teachers on several benchmarks. It also supports cross-model, multi-teacher, and causal-video distillation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Few-step distillation reduces the sampling cost of diffusion and flow models, but existing approaches often require auxiliary score models or specialized architectures. We show that simple on-policy velocity regression already provides a strong foundation for 4-NFE generation. To represent teacher velocity variation within each coarse transition, we introduce Pivot, a training-only residual proxy that separates local corrections from the student's interval-mean prediction. We further repel the student from weaker teacher predictions, providing a lightweight mechanism for avoiding undesirable modes. The resulting framework, Simplified Matching Distillation (SMD), remains entirely regression-based, requires neither online score estimation nor adversarial training, and leaves the deployed generator unchanged. We evaluate SMD on image generation and editing, 3D generation, video synthesis, and joint audio--video generation, scaling to 33 billion parameters. At 4-NFE, SMD outperforms strong few-step baselines and surpasses multi-step teachers on several benchmarks. It also supports cross-model, multi-teacher, and causal-video distillation.

</details>

## 86. MFVSR: A Multi-Style and Fine-Grained Benchmark for Video Subtitle Removal

**中文标题**: MFVSR: A Multi-Style and Fine-Grained Benchmark for Video Subtitle Removal

**作者**: Xiyan Huang, Qingdong He, Peng Tang, Bo Yin, Chaoyi Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18461v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18461v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18461v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video subtitle removal (VSR) is an important video restoration task, yet existing evaluation remains limited by low-resolution datasets, fragmented test sets, and generic video-inpainting protocols that fail to adequately capture localized restoration errors, unintended modifications, temporal instability, and incomplete outputs. In this paper, we introduce MFVSR, a large-scale multi-style benchmark for fine-grained and reliability-aware video subtitle removal evaluation. MFVSR distinguishes itself through three key features: (1) Large-scale and diverse data, comprising 3,000 paired videos from Real-HQ, Real-LQ, and AI-Generated sources, with an auxiliary set of 953 in-the-wild real videos for real-world coverage analysis; (2) Controlled subtitle diversity, spanning 16 languages and four factors covering spatial layout, temporal behavior, visual style, and language mode; (3) Fine-grained evaluation, introducing an information-conditioned, spatially decoupled protocol that separates blind from mask-guided removal and measures Restoration Fidelity, Preservation Fidelity, Temporal Fidelity, coverage, and failures. Experiments on six representative methods reveal substantial discrepancies between local restoration, outside-region preservation, temporal consistency, and full-set reliability that are obscured by conventional full-frame metrics. MFVSR therefore provides a more comprehensive and diagnostic foundation for evaluating and advancing video subtitle removal methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Experiments on six representative methods reveal substantial discrepancies between local restoration, outside-region preservation, temporal consistency, and full-set reliability that are obscured by conventional full-frame metrics. MFVSR therefore provides a more comprehensive and diagnostic foundation for evaluating and advancing video subtitle removal methods.

</details>

## 87. LongForcing: Scaling Temporal Manifold Matching for Long-Horizon World Rollout

**中文标题**: LongForcing: Scaling Temporal Manifold Matching for Long-Horizon World Rollout

**作者**: Ziyu Zhu, Zhaoxu Sun, Fan Jiang, Mengchao Wang, Junfeng Ni et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18289v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18289v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18289v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/Fantasy-AMAP/LongForcing.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autoregressive video diffusion models have emerged as a foundation for long-horizon world rollout. However, when extended far beyond their training windows, these models suffer from progressive drift: visual quality deteriorates, motion becomes inconsistent, and object identity gradually collapses as generated frames are recursively reused as context. We study a supervision-horizon gap: short-horizon distillation leaves later rollout states without direct supervision. We show that matching short-window marginals need not determine long-horizon behavior, while longer on-policy trajectories expose delayed failures to training. This motivates temporal manifold matching at scale for long-horizon world models. Building on this insight, we introduce LongForcing, a distillation approach that scales teacher supervision to long student self-rollouts. LongForcing preserves the short-horizon teacher-forcing and causal ODE-distillation stages, while replacing the realscore teacher in the final distribution-matching stage with an extended-horizon bidirectional teacher. The long-horizon teacher supervises extended student self-rollouts using the standard distribution-matching formulation, without modifying the student architecture or increasing inference cost. We evaluate LongForcing on text-to-video generation and interactive action-conditioned world models, achieving a VBench Quality score of 85.52 and improving WorldRoamBench 60s action score by 23.9 points in first-person ablations. Qualitative stress tests further sample interactive rollouts up to 24 hours, far beyond the training horizon. These results suggest that scaling supervision along the temporal axis is a practical step toward long-horizon world models. The project website and code are at https://github.com/Fantasy-AMAP/LongForcing.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autoregressive video diffusion models have emerged as a foundation for long-horizon world rollout. The long-horizon teacher supervises extended student self-rollouts using the standard distribution-matching formulation, without modifying the student architecture or increasing inference cost. We evaluate LongForcing on text-to-video generation and interactive action-conditioned world models, achieving a VBench Quality score of 85.52 and improving WorldRoamBench 60s action score by 23.9 points in first-person ablations. These results suggest that scaling supervision along the temporal axis is a practical step toward long-horizon world models. The project website and code are at https://github.com/Fantasy-AMAP/LongForcing.

</details>

## 88. An Initial Study of Physiological Markers for Deepfake Speech Detection

**中文标题**: An Initial Study of Physiological Markers for Deepfake Speech Detection

**作者**: Vojtěch Staněk, Michal Frič, Kamil Malinka, Anton Firc

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18934v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18934v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18934v1)

**类别**: cs.SD, cs.CR, cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Conventional deepfake speech detectors primarily target synthesis artifacts and often degrade under unseen attacks or recording conditions. Human speech, however, contains physiological markers associated with respiration, phonation, and temporal coordination arising from the speech-production system that synthesizers may not reproduce faithfully. This motivates the hypothesis that deepfake speech can be detected by identifying inconsistencies in these patterns, which we test using 36 handcrafted physiological markers organized into respiratory, vocal, and temporal categories. We evaluate the marker vectors with logistic regression and SVM classifiers on pooled English recordings. The best in-domain system achieves a 21.40% equal error rate (EER), while logistic regression achieves 32.77% on the out-of-domain dataset pair. Since the markers describe specific speech-production properties, the logistic-regression coefficients indicate whether higher marker values push the classifier toward bona fide or deepfake speech. These results provide initial evidence that physiological markers offer a useful, interpretable signal for deepfake speech detection.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 89. VARI: Robust Text-to-Video Generation via Group-Structured Intent Alignment

**中文标题**: VARI: Robust Text-to-Video Generation via Group-Structured Intent Alignment

**作者**: Mingxuan Zhu, Hohei Chan, Shiyuan Liu, Yuanxing Zhang, Lu Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18189v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18189v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18189v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Modern text-to-video (T2V) models generate visually compelling videos, yet meaning-preserving changes in prompt wording can substantially alter semantic realization and overall quality. Existing prompt rewriting modifies the inference input, whereas conventional post-training constructs supervision from one prompt formulation at a time; neither explicitly optimizes reliability across equivalent forms. We introduce VARI (Verified Alignment over Representations of Intent), a group-structured framework that treats automatically verified formulations of the same visual intent as a shared supervision unit. VARI builds matched form-seed rollout pools, cross-scores each video against all forms, and applies a wording-independent structured intent audit to identify valid realizations and hard semantic failures. Group-Consistent Distillation (GCD) aligns every verified form with interchangeable valid realizations, while Form-Contrastive Flow-DPO (FC-DPO) learns group-level preferences from audited positive-negative pairs, prioritizing same-seed, different-form contrasts. On HunyuanVideo-1.5, VARI attains the highest T2V-CompBench, VBench, and StoryEval scores among the evaluated methods. On prompt-form extensions of T2V-CompBench and VBench, it improves weakest-form alignment over the strongest prompt-level baselines by 2.45 and 3.55 points and reduces cross-form drop by 30.5% and 32.2%, respectively, while preserving visual quality. Human evaluation, natural-caption controls, prompt-enhancement experiments, diversity analyses, and transfer to LTX-2.3 further support the robustness and transferability of these gains, while VARI requires no additional inference-time module.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 90. FlowCred: Hierarchical Credit Assignment and Functional Coordination for Joint Text-to-Video Post-Training

**中文标题**: FlowCred: Hierarchical Credit Assignment and Functional Coordination for Joint Text-to-Video Post-Training

**作者**: Mingxuan Zhu, Yanwei Huang, Shiyuan Liu, Xinzhi Zhang, Shujie Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18142v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18142v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18142v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Prompt optimization and generator post-training are two complementary ways to improve text-to-video (T2V) generation, and recent systems increasingly train both modules jointly. However, optimizing a prompt policy and a video generator from the same terminal reward creates two ambiguities: prompt quality is confounded with stochastic generation, and the two modules may realize overlapping local improvements. We introduce FlowCred, a two-level framework for joint T2V post-training. First, hierarchical credit assignment uses matched prompt-seed rollout grids to separate prompt-level credit from prompt-conditioned generator residuals under shared randomness. Second, functional coordination probes alternative current-policy prompts at states from actual generation trajectories, providing generator-aware evidence for prompt optimization while selectively attenuating generator updates already supported by conditioning changes. Across multiple T2V benchmarks, FlowCred consistently improves matched joint-training baselines. On LTX-2.5, FlowCred improves a strictly matched joint-training control from 61.47 to 64.46 on T2V-CompBench and from 55.58 to 58.08 on StoryEval, without increasing decoded-video or reward-model-call budgets. Factorial and realized-update analyses show complementary contributions from prompt shaping and generator routing, while human evaluation and transfer across backbones and updater families provide further supporting evidence.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 91. H-DDCM: Hadamard Codebooks for Fast Generative Image and Video Compression

**中文标题**: H-DDCM: Hadamard Codebooks for Fast Generative Image and Video Compression

**作者**: Or Ronai, Vladimir Kulikov, Tomer Michaeli

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18125v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18125v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18125v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Training-free diffusion-based compression methods repurpose the generative priors of a diffusion or a flow model to compress data without retraining them as task-specific codecs. Recent codebook-based approaches such as Turbo-DDCM achieve strong perceptual quality at ultra-low bitrates, but their reliance on dense Gaussian codebooks remains costly in both memory and computation, and scales poorly with the large latent dimensions of modern image and video generators. Their combinatorial bitstream encoding further becomes computationally heavy at this scale. We introduce H-DDCM, a scalable generative compression framework that replaces Gaussian codebooks with implicit randomized Hadamard dictionaries. The fast Walsh-Hadamard transform allows for implicitly synthesizing full-dimensional dictionaries with $O(d \log d)$ compute for projection and reconstruction and $O(d)$ memory, without materializing the full-dimensional dictionary. We further introduce an efficient table-free lexicographic coding scheme that makes combinatorial encoding practical at larger dimensions. Beyond images, we adapt the framework to an autoregressive video flow model and develop a streaming compression scheme that periodically re-encodes recently reconstructed frames into a fresh KV-cache prefix, preserving temporal context across segments at no additional transmitted bits. This enables long-form, close to real-time generative video compression on a single GPU. Across image and video benchmarks, H-DDCM achieves competitive rate-distortion-perception performance while substantially reducing the memory and computational overhead of codebook-based training-free generative compression.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Their combinatorial bitstream encoding further becomes computationally heavy at this scale. The fast Walsh-Hadamard transform allows for implicitly synthesizing full-dimensional dictionaries with $O(d \log d)$ compute for projection and reconstruction and $O(d)$ memory, without materializing the full-dimensional dictionary. This enables long-form, close to real-time generative video compression on a single GPU. Across image and video benchmarks, H-DDCM achieves competitive rate-distortion-perception performance while substantially reducing the memory and computational overhead of codebook-based training-free generative compression.

</details>

## 92. Data-Free Continual Learning for World Action Model with Rectified Generative Replay

**中文标题**: Data-Free Continual Learning for World Action Model with Rectified Generative Replay

**作者**: Kaipeng Fang, Hangxu Liu, Chaojian Shi, Yunsong Zhou, Hongzi Zhu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18038v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18038v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18038v2)

**项目主页**: https://kaipengfang.github.io/r2-wam/

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) built on pretrained video generation models adapt quickly to new robotic tasks, but they forget previously learned tasks under sequential fine-tuning. Experience replay (ER) mitigates such forgetting by rehearsing data of previous tasks, yet this data is often unavailable in robotics, where a policy is deployed as weights only and its training data is not shared along with it. In this work, we ask whether a WAM can achieve data-free continual learning by regenerating its own past. Our key observation is that a WAM can indeed autoregressively regenerate complete rollouts of previously learned tasks and replay them to mitigate forgetting. However, replaying such raw rollouts still lags far behind replaying real data, which we attribute to two issues. First, the WAM is unable to recognize task completion and keeps generating purposeless motion after the task is done. Second, under open-loop generation, the generated actions gradually drift away from the generated observations. To address both issues, we present rectified generative replay (R$^2$-WAM), which truncates generated rollouts at task completion with an agentic evaluator and filters out rollouts whose actions are physically inconsistent with the observations. On the four LIBERO suites and five real-world tasks, R$^2$-WAM reduces forgetting by over 90% relative to sequential fine-tuning and nearly matches ER without accessing any data from previous tasks. Our analysis further reveals that forgetting in WAMs concentrates in inverse dynamics modeling: a forgotten WAM still produces visually plausible observations of previous tasks while its generated actions fail. Project page: https://kaipengfang.github.io/r2-wam/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) built on pretrained video generation models adapt quickly to new robotic tasks, but they forget previously learned tasks under sequential fine-tuning. Experience replay (ER) mitigates such forgetting by rehearsing data of previous tasks, yet this data is often unavailable in robotics, where a policy is deployed as weights only and its training data is not shared along with it. In this work, we ask whether a WAM can achieve data-free continual learning by regenerating its own past. First, the WAM is unable to recognize task completion and keeps generating purposeless motion after the task is done. Second, under open-loop generation, the generated actions gradually drift away from the generated observations. To address both issues, we present rectified generative replay (R$^2$-WAM), which truncates generated rollouts at task completion with an agentic evaluator and filters out rollouts whose actions are physically inconsistent with the observations. On the four LIBERO suites and five real-world tasks, R$^2$-WAM reduces forgetting by over 90% relative to sequential fine-tuning and nearly matches ER without accessing any data from previous tasks. Our analysis further reveals that forgetting in WAMs concentrates in inverse dynamics modeling: a forgotten WAM still produces visually plausible observations of previous tasks while its generated actions fail. Project page: https://kaipengfang.github.io/r2-wam/

</details>

## 93. Saliency-Guided Replay for Continual Learning in Deep Spiking Neural Networks

**中文标题**: Saliency-Guided Replay for Continual Learning in Deep Spiking Neural Networks

**作者**: Francesco Panetta, Federico Di Valerio, Michela Proietti

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17998v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17998v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17998v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.NE

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) are promising for energy-efficient neuromorphic computing, but they still suffer from catastrophic forgetting when learning tasks sequentially. This work investigates the integration of Experience Packing and Replay (EPR) as a memory-efficient continual learning strategy for deep SNNs under low-latency constraints. The proposed framework combines Batch Normalization Through Time stabilized VGG9 SNNs with Spike Activation Maps (SAM), which provide forward-only explanations based on spike activity. These explanations are temporally aggregated into spatial attention maps and used to select the most informative image patches for replay, instead of storing full images. Experiments on Split CIFAR10 and Split CIFAR100 show that SAM-guided EPR improves the accuracy-memory trade-off under tight memory budgets, and remains competitive while requiring substantially less storage.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) are promising for energy-efficient neuromorphic computing, but they still suffer from catastrophic forgetting when learning tasks sequentially. This work investigates the integration of Experience Packing and Replay (EPR) as a memory-efficient continual learning strategy for deep SNNs under low-latency constraints. The proposed framework combines Batch Normalization Through Time stabilized VGG9 SNNs with Spike Activation Maps (SAM), which provide forward-only explanations based on spike activity. These explanations are temporally aggregated into spatial attention maps and used to select the most informative image patches for replay, instead of storing full images. Experiments on Split CIFAR10 and Split CIFAR100 show that SAM-guided EPR improves the accuracy-memory trade-off under tight memory budgets, and remains competitive while requiring substantially less storage.

</details>

## 94. Sea-Net: A Small and Inherently Interpretable Neural Network for Time Series Classification

**中文标题**: Sea-Net: A Small and Inherently Interpretable Neural Network for Time Series Classification

**作者**: Ubaid Ur Rehman, Tanmoy Mondal

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17939v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17939v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17939v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Traditional time-series classification methods often operate as black boxes, providing a class prediction without indicating which time point or time interval within the series led to that prediction. To improve temporal interpretability, MILLET (Multiple Instance Learning with Temporal Explanation), which combines an InceptionTime encoder with Conjunctive Pooling, provides both classification and temporal explanations. However, MILLET contains approximately 424 K parameters, making it unsuitable for deployment on resource-constrained micro-controllers, and it achieves an overall time-series accuracy of 0.888 and an explanation score of 0.681 NDCG@n. We therefore propose SEA-Net, a lightweight architecture using small multi-scale depthwise-separable convolutional TSC encoders and improved Multiple Instance Learning (MIL) pooling heads. Our SEA-Net bottleneck encoder with Top-k pooling achieves 0.931 accuracy and 0.765 NDCG@n with only 41 K parameters. Another variant, using a gated encoder with Top-k pooling, achieves 0.955 accuracy and 0.750 NDCG@n with 62 K parameters. These results demonstrate that lightweight architectures can maintain strong classification performance and temporal explanations while substantially reducing model size. The proposed variants address specific limitations of the pooling strategy used by MILLET, providing a practical direction for interpretable time-series classification on resource-constrained edge devices.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

To improve temporal interpretability, MILLET (Multiple Instance Learning with Temporal Explanation), which combines an InceptionTime encoder with Conjunctive Pooling, provides both classification and temporal explanations. However, MILLET contains approximately 424 K parameters, making it unsuitable for deployment on resource-constrained micro-controllers, and it achieves an overall time-series accuracy of 0.888 and an explanation score of 0.681 NDCG@n. We therefore propose SEA-Net, a lightweight architecture using small multi-scale depthwise-separable convolutional TSC encoders and improved Multiple Instance Learning (MIL) pooling heads. Our SEA-Net bottleneck encoder with Top-k pooling achieves 0.931 accuracy and 0.765 NDCG@n with only 41 K parameters. Another variant, using a gated encoder with Top-k pooling, achieves 0.955 accuracy and 0.750 NDCG@n with 62 K parameters. These results demonstrate that lightweight architectures can maintain strong classification performance and temporal explanations while substantially reducing model size. The proposed variants address specific limitations of the pooling strategy used by MILLET, providing a practical direction for interpretable time-series classification on resource-constrained edge devices.

</details>

## 95. PUGS: Physics-Guided Uncertainty Modeling for Underwater Dynamic 3D Gaussian Splatting

**中文标题**: PUGS: Physics-Guided Uncertainty Modeling for Underwater Dynamic 3D Gaussian Splatting

**作者**: Tingjun Li, Yuxi Wang, Guoqiang Zhong, Zhaoxiang Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17936v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17936v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17936v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) provides an efficient framework for dynamic scene reconstruction. Most existing approaches model scene dynamics with deterministic deformation fields, assuming each Gaussian follows a distinct geometric trajectory over time. This assumption breaks down underwater, where light scattering, refraction, marine snow and low visibility create substantial physical ambiguity in captured images. We present PUGS, a physics-guided uncertainty model for underwater dynamic 3DGS. PUGS learns inherent uncertainty induced by underwater image formation and leverages it as a physical signal for dynamic scene modeling. Rather than estimating a single deterministic displacement, it parameterizes each Gaussian's motion as a probability distribution with expected deformation and physical uncertainty. We adopt stochastic deformation optimization to sample Gaussian trajectories via local reparameterization, exploring multiple physically plausible solutions during training. We further propose uncertainty stochastic annealing and a dual-path self-consistency distillation objective, converting stochastic exploration to deterministic reconstruction while preserving temporal coherence. Extensive experiments on underwater dynamic datasets and physics-aware benchmarks demonstrate superior rendering quality, temporal consistency and reconstruction robustness. Our method achieves 1.16 dB PSNR gain over the strongest baseline on UVEB and 3.99 dB on DRUVA.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dynamic 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) provides an efficient framework for dynamic scene reconstruction. Most existing approaches model scene dynamics with deterministic deformation fields, assuming each Gaussian follows a distinct geometric trajectory over time. This assumption breaks down underwater, where light scattering, refraction, marine snow and low visibility create substantial physical ambiguity in captured images. We present PUGS, a physics-guided uncertainty model for underwater dynamic 3DGS. PUGS learns inherent uncertainty induced by underwater image formation and leverages it as a physical signal for dynamic scene modeling. Rather than estimating a single deterministic displacement, it parameterizes each Gaussian's motion as a probability distribution with expected deformation and physical uncertainty. We adopt stochastic deformation optimization to sample Gaussian trajectories via local reparameterization, exploring multiple physically plausible solutions during training. We further propose uncertainty stochastic annealing and a dual-path self-consistency distillation objective, converting stochastic exploration to deterministic reconstruction while preserving temporal coherence. Extensive experiments on underwater dynamic datasets and physics-aware benchmarks demonstrate superior rendering quality, temporal consistency and reconstruction robustness. Our method achieves 1.16 dB PSNR gain over the strongest baseline on UVEB and 3.99 dB on DRUVA.

</details>

## 96. CineMem: Attention-Aligned Fine-grained Memory Retrieval for Multi-Shot Long Video Generation

**中文标题**: CineMem: Attention-Aligned Fine-grained Memory Retrieval for Multi-Shot Long Video Generation

**作者**: Xiaoxue Wu, Xinyu Gao, Fang Yu, Yu Qiao, Xinyuan Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17926v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17926v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17926v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autoregressive video generation relies on memory retrieval to model long-range dependencies. This capability becomes particularly critical for multi-shot generation, where models must reconcile substantial visual changes across shot transitions with persistent semantic continuity. Existing methods tend to perform memory retrieval at the frame level and share the same historical context across all query tokens, overlooking the heterogeneous information needs and leading to redundant retrieval and inefficient use of historical memory. To bridge this gap, we introduce CineMem, an attention-aligned fine-grained memory retrieval framework that combines online memory organization with query-adaptive retrieval for autoregressive multi-shot video generation. Specifically, guided by intrinsic memory-access patterns revealed through attention analysis, we propose an online-clustered KV cache as a memory organization strategy over all historical tokens, enabling semantic-aware memory grouping and more precise retrieval. In addition, we introduce a query-level retrieval mechanism that provides broader access to historical information under a fixed retrieval budget, thereby improving long-term memory utilization. Together, these designs enable the multi-shot video generator to preserve long-range consistency across shot transitions, particularly in revisitation scenarios, while approximating full-memory access under a constrained memory budget. To facilitate evaluation, we further introduce a suite of task-specific, VLM-assisted metrics for faithful inter-shot consistency assessment. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing methods tend to perform memory retrieval at the frame level and share the same historical context across all query tokens, overlooking the heterogeneous information needs and leading to redundant retrieval and inefficient use of historical memory. To bridge this gap, we introduce CineMem, an attention-aligned fine-grained memory retrieval framework that combines online memory organization with query-adaptive retrieval for autoregressive multi-shot video generation. Specifically, guided by intrinsic memory-access patterns revealed through attention analysis, we propose an online-clustered KV cache as a memory organization strategy over all historical tokens, enabling semantic-aware memory grouping and more precise retrieval. In addition, we introduce a query-level retrieval mechanism that provides broader access to historical information under a fixed retrieval budget, thereby improving long-term memory utilization. Together, these designs enable the multi-shot video generator to preserve long-range consistency across shot transitions, particularly in revisitation scenarios, while approximating full-memory access under a constrained memory budget. To facilitate evaluation, we further introduce a suite of task-specific, VLM-assisted metrics for faithful inter-shot consistency assessment. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework.

</details>

## 97. LinMorph: Learning to Morph Weights for Efficient Video DiT Linearization

**中文标题**: LinMorph: Learning to Morph Weights for Efficient Video DiT Linearization

**作者**: Lingyi Long, Congyue Zhao, Xiaoou Li, Yanmin Qian, Tao Huang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17914v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17914v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17914v1)

**项目主页**: https://lonelyling.github.io/LinMorph.

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Post-hoc linearization is an attractive way to accelerate pretrained Video Diffusion Transformers (Video DiTs), yet existing methods largely treat the quality loss induced by attention replacement as a recovery problem. We show that a more fundamental bottleneck lies upstream: \emph{initialization}. Random initialization discards pretrained knowledge, whereas manual weight reuse transfers it through rigid and incomplete source--target correspondences. We introduce \textbf{LinMorph}, which recasts Video DiT linearization as a learnable softmax-to-linear weight transformation problem. Instead of copying predefined counterparts, LinMorph learns a cross-parameter morphism that lets each target Gated DeltaNet (GDN) parameter draw jointly from pretrained query, key, value, and output projections, including for parameters with no direct softmax counterpart. A single morphism is shared across layers and learned through local teacher alignment, while producing layer-specific initializations from their respective pretrained weights. The resulting weights are then materialized as independent GDN modules for progressive distillation and joint recovery.Since the morphism operates in weight space rather than token space, it can be learned on short, low-resolution clips and directly reused at larger video scales. Across pretrained Video DiTs, LinMorph enables markedly faster recovery and better final generation quality than random initialization and heuristic weight reuse. Results show that efficient linearization depends not only on how a converted model is recovered, but critically on how pretrained knowledge is transferred before recovery. Project page: https://lonelyling.github.io/LinMorph.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We show that a more fundamental bottleneck lies upstream: \emph{initialization}. Random initialization discards pretrained knowledge, whereas manual weight reuse transfers it through rigid and incomplete source--target correspondences. The resulting weights are then materialized as independent GDN modules for progressive distillation and joint recovery.Since the morphism operates in weight space rather than token space, it can be learned on short, low-resolution clips and directly reused at larger video scales. Across pretrained Video DiTs, LinMorph enables markedly faster recovery and better final generation quality than random initialization and heuristic weight reuse. Results show that efficient linearization depends not only on how a converted model is recovered, but critically on how pretrained knowledge is transferred before recovery. </details>

## 98. What Makes a Good Visual Representation for Spatial Understanding in MLLMs?

**中文标题**: What Makes a Good Visual Representation for Spatial Understanding in MLLMs?

**作者**: Xiaohu Huang, Yumin Guo, Kai Han

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17844v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17844v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17844v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Spatial understanding is a necessary step in moving multimodal large language models (MLLMs) toward reasoning about the 3D physical world. Their visual pretraining data, however, is almost entirely 2D, so the visual tokens processed by MLLMs often lack inherent 3D structural information. A growing number of methods inject such structure by transferring representations from external vision or geometry models, and collectively establish that such transfer helps. Behind these methods lies a largely underexplored question: what makes a visual representation good for spatial understanding? To answer it, we first separate what is transferred from how it is transferred, and then trace the resulting differences back to properties of the representations themselves. What we transfer comes from 3D geometry models, self-supervised image and video models, vision-language models, and generative models, in the form of latent features or explicit depth and point maps. We transfer it either by distillation, using it as a supervision target, or by fusion, feeding it as an additional input. We find that a representation's 3D structure correlates strongly with how much it helps, measured by cross-view consistency for latent features and by depth and reconstruction accuracy for explicit geometry. We further show that the form of a representation dictates the mechanism it needs. Latent features gain as much from fusion as from distillation; explicit geometry gains little from fusion, and most of its distillation gain depends on a geometry decoding head. Guided by these findings, we distill both latent features and explicit geometry into a single MLLM. Taking only RGB video as input, it consistently improves over its base model on both static 3D and dynamic 4D spatial reasoning benchmarks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 99. QK-Edit: Revisiting Attention-based Injection in MM-DiT for Image and Video Editing

**中文标题**: QK-Edit: Revisiting Attention-based Injection in MM-DiT for Image and Video Editing

**作者**: Tiancheng Shen, Zilong Huang, Xiangtai Li, Zhijie Lin, Jiyang Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17829v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17829v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17829v2)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal Diffusion Transformers (MM-DiTs) have recently emerged as a powerful framework for unified text-vision synthesis, surpassing traditional U-Net architectures in generative tasks. One key innovation lies in its Multimodal Self-Attention (MM-SA) interaction, where image and text tokens are concatenated and processed via self-attention. However, this mechanism poses significant challenges for editing, rendering conventional U-Net-based attention manipulation methods ineffective. To address this limitation, we propose QK-Edit, a training-free framework that exploits the unique attention dynamics of MM-DiTs for precise text-guided image and video editing. By introducing a novel query-key manipulation strategy, our method isolates and adjusts critical attention components to achieve an optimal balance between prompt fidelity and structural consistency. This enables seamless edits across various tasks, including object addition, object removal, object replacement, changing background, changing material, changing color, and style transformation. Notably, it can be easily implemented with feature replacement in inference. QK-Edit demonstrates superior editing performance on state-of-the-art models, such as FLUX and HunyuanVideo, effectively bridging the gap between generative power and editable flexibility in MM-DiTs, and paving the way for scalable multimodal content creation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multimodal Diffusion Transformers (MM-DiTs) have recently emerged as a powerful framework for unified text-vision synthesis, surpassing traditional U-Net architectures in generative tasks. One key innovation lies in its Multimodal Self-Attention (MM-SA) interaction, where image and text tokens are concatenated and processed via self-attention. However, this mechanism poses significant challenges for editing, rendering conventional U-Net-based attention manipulation methods ineffective. To address this limitation, we propose QK-Edit, a training-free framework that exploits the unique attention dynamics of MM-DiTs for precise text-guided image and video editing. By introducing a novel query-key manipulation strategy, our method isolates and adjusts critical attention components to achieve an optimal balance between prompt fidelity and structural consistency. This enables seamless edits across various tasks, including object addition, object removal, object replacement, changing background, changing material, changing color, and style transformation. Notably, it can be easily implemented with feature replacement in inference. QK-Edit demonstrates superior editing performance on state-of-the-art models, such as FLUX and HunyuanVideo, effectively bridging the gap between generative power and editable flexibility in MM-DiTs, and paving the way for scalable multimodal content creation.

</details>

---

## 100. When, Where, and Why: Hierarchical Causal Explainability for Video Face Understanding with MLLMs

**中文标题**: When, Where, and Why: Hierarchical Causal Explainability for Video Face Understanding with MLLMs

**作者**: Anubhav Kataria, Shreya Ghosh, Jingwen Ye, Abhinav Dhall

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17791v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17791v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17791v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding facial behaviour in video requires answering three fundamental questions: when a key facial expression occurs, where the model attends on the face at that moment, and why that evidence is necessary rather than merely correlated. Today's multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can label and describe video, but they rarely provide faithful explanations, especially for small facial movements that change over time. We present VideoFace-X-LLM, a framework for explainable facial video understanding comprising two key components. First, we introduce ExplainFace, a weakly supervised video-text corpus for facial behaviour understanding, automatically constructed from CelebV-HQ videos and validated through human evaluation and a two-model audit (at most 2.3% contradictions), demonstrating consistent gains across two backbone architectures. Second, H-TCC (Hierarchical Temporal Spatial Causal Concepts), which finds the key temporal moments (When), the facial regions (Where), and verifies its causal necessity (Why) through targeted occlusion. Fine-tuning on ExplainFace raises appearance Macro F1 from 19.48% to 32.87% (+13.39), with gains of +4.38 on actions, +6.77 on temporal emotion, and +11.84 F1 on deepfake detection. We also measure the explanations pointing lift, face/edge lift, stability, an Adebayo sanity check, and a necessity test rather than only showing examples. Our models, code, and corpus are available on request for research use.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Understanding facial behaviour in video requires answering three fundamental questions: when a key facial expression occurs, where the model attends on the face at that moment, and why that evidence is necessary rather than merely correlated. Today's multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can label and describe video, but they rarely provide faithful explanations, especially for small facial movements that change over time. We present VideoFace-X-LLM, a framework for explainable facial video understanding comprising two key components. First, we introduce ExplainFace, a weakly supervised video-text corpus for facial behaviour understanding, automatically constructed from CelebV-HQ videos and validated through human evaluation and a two-model audit (at most 2.3% contradictions), demonstrating consistent gains across two backbone architectures. Second, H-TCC (Hierarchical Temporal Spatial Causal Concepts), which finds the key temporal moments (When), the facial regions (Where), and verifies its causal necessity (Why) through targeted occlusion. Fine-tuning on ExplainFace raises appearance Macro F1 from 19.48% to 32.87% (+13.39), with gains of +4.38 on actions, +6.77 on temporal emotion, and +11.84 F1 on deepfake detection. We also measure the explanations pointing lift, face/edge lift, stability, an Adebayo sanity check, and a necessity test rather than only showing examples. Our models, code, and corpus are available on request for research use.

</details>

## 101. Hierarchical Curriculum Learning for Surgical Video Generation: Focusing on Bilateral Axillo-Breast Approach Robotic Thyroidectomy Datasets

**中文标题**: Hierarchical Curriculum Learning for Surgical Video Generation: Focusing on Bilateral Axillo-Breast Approach Robotic Thyroidectomy Datasets

**作者**: Youngbin Kong, Seungho Lee, Jae Kyung Shin, Jong-hyuk Ahn, Hyoun-Joong Kong et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17684v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17684v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17684v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

High-fidelity surgical video generation is increasingly important for surgical education, simulation, and downstream AI training, as conventional simulation pipelines are limited by cost, realism, and scenario diversity. Adapting generic text-to-video diffusion models to surgical procedures remains difficult, however, due to complex anatomical structures, intricate tool-tissue interactions, the inherently hierarchical nature of surgical workflows, and the scarcity of curated training data outside a few well-studied procedures. We propose a surgical video generation framework that combines hierarchical procedural annotation with curriculum transfer learning. Surgical procedures are decomposed into three levels (phase, task, and action) and a pretrained text-to-video diffusion model is progressively adapted from coarse to fine-grained surgical context, supported by a dynamic sampling strategy that handles variable-resolution and variable-length surgical clips. We instantiate the framework on Bilateral Axillo-Breast Approach robotic thyroidectomy, a procedure for which no curated benchmark currently exists. Relative to the preceding task-level model, the final action-level model reduces FVD by 20.1% and FID by 15.7%, and a matched-budget ablation indicates that these gains arise from the curriculum design rather than from additional training. A user study with six clinicians rates visual realism highly while highlighting remaining gaps in anatomical accuracy and clinical reliability. The proposed framework offers a step toward AI-driven surgical simulation in procedures where curated data are scarce.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Adapting generic text-to-video diffusion models to surgical procedures remains difficult, however, due to complex anatomical structures, intricate tool-tissue interactions, the inherently hierarchical nature of surgical workflows, and the scarcity of curated training data outside a few well-studied procedures. We propose a surgical video generation framework that combines hierarchical procedural annotation with curriculum transfer learning. Surgical procedures are decomposed into three levels (phase, task, and action) and a pretrained text-to-video diffusion model is progressively adapted from coarse to fine-grained surgical context, supported by a dynamic sampling strategy that handles variable-resolution and variable-length surgical clips. We instantiate the framework on Bilateral Axillo-Breast Approach robotic thyroidectomy, a procedure for which no curated benchmark currently exists. Relative to the preceding task-level model, the final action-level model reduces FVD by 20.1% and FID by 15.7%, and a matched-budget ablation indicates that these gains arise from the curriculum design rather than from additional training. A user study with six clinicians rates visual realism highly while highlighting remaining gaps in anatomical accuracy and clinical reliability. The proposed framework offers a step toward AI-driven surgical simulation in procedures where curated data are scarce.

</details>

## 102. Deficit-Driven Stratified Acquisition for Building STRATA-S12: An Open SAR-Optical Corpus for Self-Supervised Learning

**中文标题**: Deficit-Driven Stratified Acquisition for Building STRATA-S12: An Open SAR-Optical Corpus for Self-Supervised Learning

**作者**: Christian Sanchez-Leon, Dongming Chen, Dongqi Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17680v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17680v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17680v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Building a co-registered SAR-optical corpus for self-supervised Earth Observation is above all an acquisition problem, yet the main multi-modal datasets are collected opportunistically, with no per-stratum quota control and no mechanism against the geographic dropout that non-uniform cloud cover leaves behind. We present Deficit-Driven Stratified Acquisition (DDSA), an algorithm that treats building a co-registered Sentinel-1/Sentinel-2 corpus as a constrained maximum-coverage problem over a hemisphere-corrected (class x season x region) stratification, enforces strict temporal pairing and a multi-stage quality gate, and closes the loop during acquisition with a human-in-the-loop deficit-repair stage. Measured on its own product, the algorithm yields three results a single-pass acquisition cannot report. Deficit-driven ordering makes a partial run a covering one: it holds every one of the 192 strata populated from the first few percent of the budget, where an abundance-first order leaves most strata empty until the end. Whether a failed attempt can be recovered depends on the temporal persistence of its cause, not the kind of defect: geometric failures such as nodata are among the most repairable (eta = 41.4%) while cloud is among the least (eta = 18.8%), because a cloudy location is cloudy on most dates of its season. The repair loop supplies 39.5% of the released pairs at no measurable quality cost, with 89.8% surviving manual review. The quality guarantee has a price: 2,928,370 acquisition attempts produced 740,746 released pairs. We release the corpus, STRATA-S12, with STAC/PDS4-aligned FAIR sidecars, and a pre-registered linear-probe check confirms it is fit for self-supervised pre-training. A metadata-level coverage comparison against SSL4EO-S12 and SEN12MS situates the corpus over a shared frame; systematic cross-corpus and cross-method comparison is left to follow-up work.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present Deficit-Driven Stratified Acquisition (DDSA), an algorithm that treats building a co-registered Sentinel-1/Sentinel-2 corpus as a constrained maximum-coverage problem over a hemisphere-corrected (class x season x region) stratification, enforces strict temporal pairing and a multi-stage quality gate, and closes the loop during acquisition with a human-in-the-loop deficit-repair stage.
