Whether a failed attempt can be recovered depends on the temporal persistence of its cause, not the kind of defect: geometric failures such as nodata are among the most repairable (eta = 41.4%) while cloud is among the least (eta = 18.8%), because a cloudy location is cloudy on most dates of its season. The repair loop supplies 39.5% of the released pairs at no measurable quality cost, with 89.8% surviving manual review. The quality guarantee has a price: 2,928,370 acquisition attempts produced 740,746 released pairs. We release the corpus, STRATA-S12, with STAC/PDS4-aligned FAIR sidecars, and a pre-registered linear-probe check confirms it is fit for self-supervised pre-training. A metadata-level coverage comparison against SSL4EO-S12 and SEN12MS situates the corpus over a shared frame; systematic cross-corpus and cross-method comparison is left to follow-up work.

</details>

## 103. Open-o3-Video-v2: Seek, Ground, and Reason with Verifiable Spatio-Temporal Evidence

**中文标题**: Open-o3-Video-v2: Seek, Ground, and Reason with Verifiable Spatio-Temporal Evidence

**作者**: Jiahao Meng, Zhongyuan Bao, Yue Tan, Haiquan Wen, Renrui Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17656v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17656v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17656v1)

**项目主页**: https://msalab-pku.github.io/projects/Open-o3-Video-v2.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reliable video reasoning requires models to produce correct answers and expose supporting visual evidence. Open-o3-Video enables verifiable reasoning by embedding timestamps and object-level bounding boxes into reasoning traces, but is restricted to frames from an initial sparse sampler. We introduce Open-o3-Video-v2, which formulates video reasoning as a unified agentic evidencegrounded process. The model identifies missing information, acquires additional observations, grounds the moments and objects supporting its reasoning, and writes this evidence into the trajectory before continuing or answering. To support this behavior, we construct STGR-v2-CoT135K, including 52K multi-turn agentic grounded reasoning trajectories, and STGR-v2-RL-13K for reinforcement learning. We further propose Clue-Seek GRPO (CS-GRPO), which tracks how tool calls change accumulated evidence, rewards clue coverage and step-wise gains, and penalizes excessive, repetitive, or uninformative retrieval. On V-STaR, Open-o3-Video-v2-4B achieves 40.4% mAM and 56.3% mLGM, outperforming its Qwen3-VL-4B backbone by 8.5% and 12.6%, respectively. It also delivers consistent gains across general video understanding, long-video reasoning, and temporal grounding benchmarks. These results show that active evidence acquisition and verifiable object-level grounding are complementary for reliable video reasoning. The code and models are released at https://msalab-pku.github.io/projects/Open-o3-Video-v2.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reliable video reasoning requires models to produce correct answers and expose supporting visual evidence. Open-o3-Video enables verifiable reasoning by embedding timestamps and object-level bounding boxes into reasoning traces, but is restricted to frames from an initial sparse sampler. We introduce Open-o3-Video-v2, which formulates video reasoning as a unified agentic evidencegrounded process. The model identifies missing information, acquires additional observations, grounds the moments and objects supporting its reasoning, and writes this evidence into the trajectory before continuing or answering. To support this behavior, we construct STGR-v2-CoT135K, including 52K multi-turn agentic grounded reasoning trajectories, and STGR-v2-RL-13K for reinforcement learning. We further propose Clue-Seek GRPO (CS-GRPO), which tracks how tool calls change accumulated evidence, rewards clue coverage and step-wise gains, and penalizes excessive, repetitive, or uninformative retrieval. On V-STaR, Open-o3-Video-v2-4B achieves 40.4% mAM and 56.3% mLGM, outperforming its Qwen3-VL-4B backbone by 8.5% and 12.6%, respectively. It also delivers consistent gains across general video understanding, long-video reasoning, and temporal grounding benchmarks. These results show that active evidence acquisition and verifiable object-level grounding are complementary for reliable video reasoning. The code and models are released at https://msalab-pku.github.io/projects/Open-o3-Video-v2.

</details>

## 104. 4LC: 4D Latent Cache for Efficient Conditioning in Dynamic World Modeling

**中文标题**: 4LC: 4D Latent Cache for Efficient Conditioning in Dynamic World Modeling

**作者**: Jianhui Zhang, Zuyao Lin, Pengxin Xu, Jingdong Wang, Xingyu Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17625v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17625v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17625v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

For video generation in dynamic world modeling, observed static and dynamic content should be efficiently reused to provide conditions for repeated generation at different times and viewpoints. We introduce 4LC, a 4D latent cache with composite visual representations and a flexible spatiotemporal access interface. As a latent cache, 4LC directly reuses visual representations of static and dynamic content to provide generation conditions, avoiding per-request RGB rendering and re-encoding. As a composite cache, 4LC binds visual representations from multiple visual encoders (e.g., VAE, DINO, V-JEPA, VGGT) to shared geometry and motion, allowing the composite representations to describe the dynamic world with rich information. Besides, we design a flexible spatiotemporal interface, which directly reads the cache at independently specified times and viewpoints and combines readouts across observations, providing a common conditioning format for diverse downstream tasks. Building on 4LC, we develop 4LC-Video to address multiple video generation tasks through different configurations of source observations, spatiotemporal requests, and historical reuse, including camera control, temporal control, novel-view synthesis, scene revisits, and long-term generation. Our experiments show more efficient condition acquisition, effective spatiotemporal controllability, and scene consistency across tasks using a single 4LC-Video model without task-specific fine-tuning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

For video generation in dynamic world modeling, observed static and dynamic content should be efficiently reused to provide conditions for repeated generation at different times and viewpoints. We introduce 4LC, a 4D latent cache with composite visual representations and a flexible spatiotemporal access interface. As a latent cache, 4LC directly reuses visual representations of static and dynamic content to provide generation conditions, avoiding per-request RGB rendering and re-encoding. As a composite cache, 4LC binds visual representations from multiple visual encoders (e.g., VAE, DINO, V-JEPA, VGGT) to shared geometry and motion, allowing the composite representations to describe the dynamic world with rich information. Besides, we design a flexible spatiotemporal interface, which directly reads the cache at independently specified times and viewpoints and combines readouts across observations, providing a common conditioning format for diverse downstream tasks. Building on 4LC, we develop 4LC-Video to address multiple video generation tasks through different configurations of source observations, spatiotemporal requests, and historical reuse, including camera control, temporal control, novel-view synthesis, scene revisits, and long-term generation. Our experiments show more efficient condition acquisition, effective spatiotemporal controllability, and scene consistency across tasks using a single 4LC-Video model without task-specific fine-tuning.

</details>

## 105. Exploring Interpretability in Deep Learning-Based Forest Classification - Separating Similar Forest Classes using Sentinel-2 Time Series

**中文标题**: Exploring Interpretability in Deep Learning-Based Forest Classification - Separating Similar Forest Classes using Sentinel-2 Time Series

**作者**: Maximilian Kirsch, Christine Preisach

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19270v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19270v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19270v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

The ecological insurance theory posits that diverse forests are more resilient and better able to adapt to changing environmental conditions. Increasing drought and heat stress in Central Europe promotes mixed forests for maintaining ecosystem services. However, a diverse species distribution complicates remote sensing-based species mapping, as distinguishing pure from mixed species compositions at the pixel level remains challenging. We evaluated the potential of recurrent neural networks (RNNs) for tree-species classification from Sentinel-2 time series and investigated the spectral-temporal features underlying class discrimination, with particular emphasis on separating pure from mixed Norway spruce pixels. We compared RNN classification models with Random Forest (RF) and Extreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost) and used Integrated Gradients (IG) to quantify feature attributions across spectral bands and time. The best-performing RNN achieved a Macro-F1 score of 0.851 on the test data, outperforming XGBoost by 0.012 and Random Forest by 0.103. Beyond classification performance, the attribution analysis revealed distinct spectral-temporal patterns associated with the separation of pure and mixed Norway spruce pixels. Attribution values increased particularly after approximately day of year (DOY) 125, while B11 showed the strongest contribution during periods of increasing spectral divergence between the two classes. These findings show that recurrent models can provide competitive classification performance while also revealing the phenological periods and spectral regions that are most relevant for distinguishing closely related forest compositions. Combining time-series classification with explainability therefore provides additional insight into the spectral-temporal mechanisms underlying tree-species discrimination and may support more detailed mapping of mixed forest structures.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The ecological insurance theory posits that diverse forests are more resilient and better able to adapt to changing environmental conditions. Increasing drought and heat stress in Central Europe promotes mixed forests for maintaining ecosystem services. However, a diverse species distribution complicates remote sensing-based species mapping, as distinguishing pure from mixed species compositions at the pixel level remains challenging. We evaluated the potential of recurrent neural networks (RNNs) for tree-species classification from Sentinel-2 time series and investigated the spectral-temporal features underlying class discrimination, with particular emphasis on separating pure from mixed Norway spruce pixels. We compared RNN classification models with Random Forest (RF) and Extreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost) and used Integrated Gradients (IG) to quantify feature attributions across spectral bands and time. The best-performing RNN achieved a Macro-F1 score of 0.851 on the test data, outperforming XGBoost by 0.012 and Random Forest by 0.103. Beyond classification performance, the attribution analysis revealed distinct spectral-temporal patterns associated with the separation of pure and mixed Norway spruce pixels. Attribution values increased particularly after approximately day of year (DOY) 125, while B11 showed the strongest contribution during periods of increasing spectral divergence between the two classes. These findings show that recurrent models can provide competitive classification performance while also revealing the phenological periods and spectral regions that are most relevant for distinguishing closely related forest compositions. Combining time-series classification with explainability therefore provides additional insight into the spectral-temporal mechanisms underlying tree-species discrimination and may support more detailed mapping of mixed forest structures.

</details>

## 106. ListQA: A Benchmark for Evaluating List-Formatted Factual Knowledge Retrieval in Large Language Models

**中文标题**: ListQA: A Benchmark for Evaluating List-Formatted Factual Knowledge Retrieval in Large Language Models

**作者**: Mayank Kulkarni, Rakesh Vaideeswaran

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19165v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19165v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19165v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CL

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Many real-world questions demand not a single fact but an organized collection of them, yet existing factual knowledge benchmarks almost exclusively target single answer retrieval. We introduce ListQA, a benchmark of 9,045 human-curated, cross-validated questions (audited error rate of 1.7%) that require LLMs to recall multiple facts and compose them into structured lists of 3-10 elements. Questions range from flat lists ("Which NCAA teams went undefeated between 2000 and 2024?") to hierarchical lists with sub-attributes ("...and what was their record and result?"), spanning eight categories across 60+ countries. An evergreen design (explicit temporal constraints or historically immutable facts) keeps ground truth valid without periodic updates. For evaluation, we frame element matching as a linear sum assignment problem: optimal bipartite matching paired with LLM-based semantic grading jointly captures factual accuracy, hallucination, and format compliance. We benchmark 34 models (8 families, 42 configurations across standard and thinking enabled inference) and find: the best standard-inference model scores below 37% LLM-Judge-F1, multi-faceted questions are harder across the board, and providing the source Wikipedia page lifts scores dramatically, confirming that models can organize facts but struggle to retrieve them from parameters alone. Extended analyses cover base vs. instruction-tuned checkpoints and token efficiency. We release the dataset and evaluation code.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Many real-world questions demand not a single fact but an organized collection of them, yet existing factual knowledge benchmarks almost exclusively target single answer retrieval. We introduce ListQA, a benchmark of 9,045 human-curated, cross-validated questions (audited error rate of 1.7%) that require LLMs to recall multiple facts and compose them into structured lists of 3-10 elements. Questions range from flat lists ("Which NCAA teams went undefeated between 2000 and 2024?") to hierarchical lists with sub-attributes ("...and what was their record and result?"), spanning eight categories across 60+ countries. An evergreen design (explicit temporal constraints or historically immutable facts) keeps ground truth valid without periodic updates. For evaluation, we frame element matching as a linear sum assignment problem: optimal bipartite matching paired with LLM-based semantic grading jointly captures factual accuracy, hallucination, and format compliance. We benchmark 34 models (8 families, 42 configurations across standard and thinking enabled inference) and find: the best standard-inference model scores below 37% LLM-Judge-F1, multi-faceted questions are harder across the board, and providing the source Wikipedia page lifts scores dramatically, confirming that models can organize facts but struggle to retrieve them from parameters alone. Extended analyses cover base vs. instruction-tuned checkpoints and token efficiency. We release the dataset and evaluation code.

</details>

---

## 107. Perceptual Characterization of Scan-induced Artifacts in Dynamic 3D Meshes

**中文标题**: Perceptual Characterization of Scan-induced Artifacts in Dynamic 3D Meshes

**作者**: Huong Hoang, Truong Nguyen, Pamela Cosman

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19173v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19173v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19173v1)

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic meshes reconstructed from real-world scenes often exhibit scan-induced artifacts such as surface jitter and missing geometry, which strongly impact perceived visual quality. Objective assessment remains challenging due to the absence of artifact-free ground truth and limited perceptual understanding of artifact interactions. This paper presents a systematic and controlled perceptual study of scan-induced artifacts in dynamic meshes, focusing on surface jitter and holes. To enable systematic analysis, we introduce a physically grounded artifact synthesis framework in which holes are synthesized using a reconstruction uncertainty model derived from multi-view geometry. The study reveals asymmetric cross-artifact interactions, non-additive integration, and motion-dependent temporal masking. Based on these findings, we propose a compact, perceptually grounded no-reference quality assessment framework that encodes the observed perceptual mechanisms.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dynamic meshes reconstructed from real-world scenes often exhibit scan-induced artifacts such as surface jitter and missing geometry, which strongly impact perceived visual quality. Objective assessment remains challenging due to the absence of artifact-free ground truth and limited perceptual understanding of artifact interactions. This paper presents a systematic and controlled perceptual study of scan-induced artifacts in dynamic meshes, focusing on surface jitter and holes. To enable systematic analysis, we introduce a physically grounded artifact synthesis framework in which holes are synthesized using a reconstruction uncertainty model derived from multi-view geometry. The study reveals asymmetric cross-artifact interactions, non-additive integration, and motion-dependent temporal masking. Based on these findings, we propose a compact, perceptually grounded no-reference quality assessment framework that encodes the observed perceptual mechanisms.

</details>

## 108. DualAffect: Dual-Path Paralinguistic Integration for Generative Audiovisual Emotion Understanding

**中文标题**: DualAffect: Dual-Path Paralinguistic Integration for Generative Audiovisual Emotion Understanding

**作者**: Yibo Wang, Jisheng Dang, Bimei Wang, Junhong Liu, Jiongpei Hou et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18073v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18073v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18073v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/YiboWang2002/DualAffect.

**类别**: cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding human emotion is essential for multimodal systems that interpret both the content of an interaction and the speaker's expressive state. However, connecting paralinguistic cues to generated answers remains challenging, particularly when vocal expression diverges from visual appearance. We propose DualAffect, a dual-path architecture that integrates visually conditioned acoustic features and temporally organized acoustic tokens into a shared language decoder. The two pathways reuse the same acoustic encoding. The fusion pathway associates sound with visual content through visual queries, while the direct pathway compresses the valid acoustic sequence into ordered tokens through an independent projector. This design gives the decoder access to both joint audiovisual context and acoustic evidence organized along its own timeline. Mixed-task adaptation with task-dependent output preservation supports emotion recognition and general audiovisual question answering within the same model. DualAffect achieves an average metric of 67.89 on CA-MER and accuracies of 62.88% on the EmotionTalk test set and 49.87% on Daily-Omni. Pathway interventions show that the trained decoder uses direct acoustic evidence alongside existing fusion. With model parameters and fusion inputs fixed, zeroing the direct tokens reduces EmotionTalk validation accuracy by 9.75 percentage points and the CA-MER average metric by 2.66 points. The largest CA-MER subset effect occurs when the overall emotion agrees with audio but differs from vision. These findings identify acoustic representation organization as a practical design axis for connecting paralinguistic perception to unified audiovisual generation. Code is available at https://github.com/YiboWang2002/DualAffect.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, connecting paralinguistic cues to generated answers remains challenging, particularly when vocal expression diverges from visual appearance. With model parameters and fusion inputs fixed, zeroing the direct tokens reduces EmotionTalk validation accuracy by 9.75 percentage points and the CA-MER average metric by 2.66 points. The largest CA-MER subset effect occurs when the overall emotion agrees with audio but differs from vision. These findings identify acoustic representation organization as a practical design axis for connecting paralinguistic perception to unified audiovisual generation. Code is available at https://github.com/YiboWang2002/DualAffect.

</details>

---

## 109. Contact Trajectory Prompting: In-Context Transfer of Contact-Rich Behaviors from a Single Demonstration

**中文标题**: Contact Trajectory Prompting: In-Context Transfer of Contact-Rich Behaviors from a Single Demonstration

**作者**: Xian Nie, Yujie Zang, Yuhang Zheng, Yupeng Zheng, Songen Gu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18848v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18848v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18848v1)

**项目主页**: https://ctp-robotics.github.io/project_page.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In-context robot learning enables robots to acquire new behaviors from demonstrations without test-time parameter updates. However, visual and kinematic specifications alone may not distinguish contact-rich behaviors that share similar motions but require different interaction intensities or patterns. We propose Contact Trajectory Prompting (CTP), a framework for one-shot transfer of contact-rich manipulation behaviors. CTP encodes motion and contact information from a single demonstration into an ordered contact trajectory prompt that captures their temporal evolution and specifies the desired physical interaction behavior. During execution, the policy uses online sensory feedback to retrieve interaction-relevant information from the prompt, conditioning closed-loop action generation on the demonstrated contact behavior. Across real-world wiping, rotation, and insertion tasks on objects excluded from training, CTP achieves 78.7% overall success, outperforming the strongest evaluated baseline by 13.0 percentage points. Changing only the reference demonstration enables CTP to follow contact specifications for both intensity and direction, including an intermediate wiping force level and clockwise or counterclockwise cap rotation. These results demonstrate that contact trajectories serve as effective behavioral specifications, extending in-context robot learning beyond motion imitation to physical interaction transfer. Project website: https://ctp-robotics.github.io/project_page.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

CTP encodes motion and contact information from a single demonstration into an ordered contact trajectory prompt that captures their temporal evolution and specifies the desired physical interaction behavior. During execution, the policy uses online sensory feedback to retrieve interaction-relevant information from the prompt, conditioning closed-loop action generation on the demonstrated contact behavior. Across real-world wiping, rotation, and insertion tasks on objects excluded from training, CTP achieves 78.7% overall success, outperforming the strongest evaluated baseline by 13.0 percentage points. These results demonstrate that contact trajectories serve as effective behavioral specifications, extending in-context robot learning beyond motion imitation to physical interaction transfer. Project website: https://ctp-robotics.github.io/project_page.

</details>

## 110. WAVE-0: Bridging VLA Policies and WAMs with Lightweight Latent Future Prediction

**中文标题**: WAVE-0: Bridging VLA Policies and WAMs with Lightweight Latent Future Prediction

**作者**: Haomin Wang, Anqiong Ji, Ke Wang, Zhongshan Liu, Ru Ying et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18568v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18568v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18568v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) policies excel at generalist robotic manipulation but generate actions without explicitly modeling their environmental consequences. World action models (WAMs) incorporate future prediction, yet most rely on costly video generation, while latent alternatives either use predicted futures only as auxiliary targets or infer latent actions from suboptimal visual transitions through costly large-scale video pretraining. We introduce WAVE-0 (World-model-Augmented VLA Execution), a framework that bridges VLAs and WAMs through a lightweight latent world model. By connecting the semantic understanding of pretrained VLAs with explicit future prediction, WAVE-0 allows anticipated action consequences to directly guide action generation. Built on a pretrained VLA, its 257M world model translates VLM-generated latent action plans into predicted future representations that condition the action expert, unifying latent planning, future prediction, and action generation. The world model is trained directly on ground-truth robot actions with a counterfactual ranking objective, requiring neither pixel reconstruction nor large-scale video pretraining. WAVE-0 achieves success rates of 91.47% on RoboTwin 2.0 and 98.7% on LIBERO, along with 88.0%, 61.4%, and 75.0% on LIBERO-Plus, LIBERO-Pro, and LIBERO-Para, respectively. On real-world tasks, it improves the success rate of $π_{0.5}$ from 38.3% to 73.3%. These results demonstrate strong manipulation performance and generalization across diverse distribution shifts.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language-action (VLA) policies excel at generalist robotic manipulation but generate actions without explicitly modeling their environmental consequences. World action models (WAMs) incorporate future prediction, yet most rely on costly video generation, while latent alternatives either use predicted futures only as auxiliary targets or infer latent actions from suboptimal visual transitions through costly large-scale video pretraining. We introduce WAVE-0 (World-model-Augmented VLA Execution), a framework that bridges VLAs and WAMs through a lightweight latent world model. The world model is trained directly on ground-truth robot actions with a counterfactual ranking objective, requiring neither pixel reconstruction nor large-scale video pretraining. WAVE-0 achieves success rates of 91.47% on RoboTwin 2.0 and 98.7% on LIBERO, along with 88.0%, 61.4%, and 75.0% on LIBERO-Plus, LIBERO-Pro, and LIBERO-Para, respectively. On real-world tasks, it improves the success rate of $π_{0.5}$ from 38.3% to 73.3%. These results demonstrate strong manipulation performance and generalization across diverse distribution shifts.

</details>

## 111. Behavioural self-monitoring for autonomous earth observation agents: The attribute reliability monitor (ARM)

**中文标题**: Behavioural self-monitoring for autonomous earth observation agents: The attribute reliability monitor (ARM)

**作者**: Alejandro D. Mousist

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18548v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18548v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18548v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Language and vision language models can support autonomous systems, but changing operating conditions may compromise the reliability of their semantic outputs. This work introduces the Attribute Reliability Monitor (ARM), a framework that accumulates evidence of suspicion over time to assess individual semantic attributes. ARM accommodates different detectors and temporal monitoring methods, while leaving intervention policies to the consuming system. An initial implementation was evaluated using a specialized vision language model developed for satellite thermal infrared imagery. Monitoring followed a black box approach, using only observable responses without access to internal model states, confidence scores, or external reference answers. Under controlled input degradation, disagreement between responses provided evidence for selective suspension of attributes. The results illustrate how external behavioral monitoring can inform continued use of model outputs, while distinguishing operational suspicion from semantic correctness.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

ARM accommodates different detectors and temporal monitoring methods, while leaving intervention policies to the consuming system. An initial implementation was evaluated using a specialized vision language model developed for satellite thermal infrared imagery. Monitoring followed a black box approach, using only observable responses without access to internal model states, confidence scores, or external reference answers. Under controlled input degradation, disagreement between responses provided evidence for selective suspension of attributes. The results illustrate how external behavioral monitoring can inform continued use of model outputs, while distinguishing operational suspicion from semantic correctness.

</details>

## 112. MemoryVLN: From Ego-Aware Memory Representation to Cross-Context Memory Reuse in Vision-Language Navigation

**中文标题**: MemoryVLN: From Ego-Aware Memory Representation to Cross-Context Memory Reuse in Vision-Language Navigation

**作者**: Xueyao Wei, Yifei Wang, Delun Li, Shaoan Wang, Junzhi Yu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18478v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18478v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18478v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Large vision-language models (LVLMs) have shown strong potential for embodied vision-language navigation (VLN), yet their performance degrades on long-horizon trajectories due to context-length limits, redundant historical observations, and unstable spatial alignment across time. These issues make it difficult to both compress long navigation histories and reliably reuse past spatial experience. To address these limitations, we propose \textbf{MemoryVLN}, a unified memory-driven navigation system built around memory construction and management. During memory construction, \emph{Long Sequence Compression} employs forgetting-curve-inspired temporal sampling to preserve dense recent evidence and sparse yet representative distant observations under a limited token budget. \emph{Ego-Aware Memory Representation} further integrates 2D semantic tokens, 3D geometric tokens, and 6D agent poses, allowing each memory to encode both what was observed and where it was observed. During memory management, \emph{Hierarchical Memory Bank} maintains in-episode and context-level memories through cross-context warm-start and confidence-controlled write-back. \emph{Memory Enhancement} then retrieves the Top-$K$ relevant memories according to the current navigation state and fuses them with the current visual tokens. Together, these mechanisms transform raw navigation history into compact, spatially grounded, selectively retrievable, and reusable navigation experience. Extensive experiments on R2R-CE and RxR-CE demonstrate consistent improvements over the state-of-the-art baselines by a substantial margin (i.e., 2\% and 6.5\% in successful rate).

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Large vision-language models (LVLMs) have shown strong potential for embodied vision-language navigation (VLN), yet their performance degrades on long-horizon trajectories due to context-length limits, redundant historical observations, and unstable spatial alignment across time. These issues make it difficult to both compress long navigation histories and reliably reuse past spatial experience. During memory management, \emph{Hierarchical Memory Bank} maintains in-episode and context-level memories through cross-context warm-start and confidence-controlled write-back. \emph{Memory Enhancement} then retrieves the Top-$K$ relevant memories according to the current navigation state and fuses them with the current visual tokens. Together, these mechanisms transform raw navigation history into compact, spatially grounded, selectively retrievable, and reusable navigation experience. Extensive experiments on R2R-CE and RxR-CE demonstrate consistent improvements over the state-of-the-art baselines by a substantial margin (i.e., 2\% and 6.5\% in successful rate).

</details>

## 113. Assessing the Quality of Concrete Scenarios Generated from Scenario Classes for Automated Driving

**中文标题**: Assessing the Quality of Concrete Scenarios Generated from Scenario Classes for Automated Driving

**作者**: Lina Putze, Thies de Graaff, Jan Steffen Becker, Christian Neurohr

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18169v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18169v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18169v1)

**类别**: cs.SE, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

A scenario-based development approach has become state of the art for automated driving systems. Once a set of scenario classes covering the operational design domain is available, concrete scenarios are generated from these scenario classes, e.g., to be used for hazard analysis and risk assessment or for the system's verification and validation. For such applications, a high diversity among the generated concrete scenarios is paramount. The question arises how the quality of collections of concrete scenarios can be assessed in such a setting. In this work, we aggregate scenario distance measures into so-called quality functions that quantify the diversity of concrete scenario collections in the context of automated driving. In particular, we consider the case where scenario classes are formalized as constraint systems and the associated concrete scenario space is not explicitly known. Based on a set of desired properties we construct candidates for such quality functions and give proofs for their property fulfillment. To show the practical relevance of quality functions, we perform a variety of experiments in the context of an abstract scenario-based virtual verification toolchain for automated driving systems. More specifically, we use quality functions to compare the effectiveness of variation methods for sampling, examine the choice of different scenario distance measures, and investigate the effects of temporal aspects on concrete scenario collection quality.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

For such applications, a high diversity among the generated concrete scenarios is paramount. The question arises how the quality of collections of concrete scenarios can be assessed in such a setting. In this work, we aggregate scenario distance measures into so-called quality functions that quantify the diversity of concrete scenario collections in the context of automated driving. In particular, we consider the case where scenario classes are formalized as constraint systems and the associated concrete scenario space is not explicitly known. Based on a set of desired properties we construct candidates for such quality functions and give proofs for their property fulfillment. More specifically, we use quality functions to compare the effectiveness of variation methods for sampling, examine the choice of different scenario distance measures, and investigate the effects of temporal aspects on concrete scenario collection quality.

## 114. RESTA: Time-Scaling Augmentation for Data-Efficient Robot Imitation Learning

**中文标题**: RESTA: Time-Scaling Augmentation for Data-Efficient Robot Imitation Learning

**作者**: Yunpeng Jiang, Jianshu Hu, Han Fang, Zhiyuan Guan, Paul Weng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.18163v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18163v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18163v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robot manipulation policies trained through imitation learning commonly rely on costly demonstrations recorded in one task direction and at one temporal resolution. We propose Reversal and Stride-based Trajectory Augmentation (RESTA), which exploits time-scaling data augmentation to improve data efficiency through trajectory reversal and temporal resampling. Trajectory-reversal augmentation reverses and relabels demonstrations between paired tasks with opposite objectives, reusing recorded rollouts without additional environment interaction. Because contact-rich manipulation is not exactly reversible, we treat reversal as an approximate paired-task prior rather than a physical symmetry. Temporal-stride augmentation varies observation and action sampling strides and conditions the policy on the resulting offsets, enabling fixed or randomized temporal schedules. Experiments across 16 paired bimanual manipulation tasks show that trajectory reversal is particularly effective in the low-data regime and that temporal-stride augmentation improves success under randomized temporal schedules. With 25 and 50 independently collected demonstrations per task, RESTA achieves 78.4% and 85.2% average success, respectively, matching or exceeding baselines trained with twice as many demonstrations (77.3% and 85.4%). These results show that RESTA can improve data efficiency in robot imitation learning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robot manipulation policies trained through imitation learning commonly rely on costly demonstrations recorded in one task direction and at one temporal resolution. We propose Reversal and Stride-based Trajectory Augmentation (RESTA), which exploits time-scaling data augmentation to improve data efficiency through trajectory reversal and temporal resampling. Trajectory-reversal augmentation reverses and relabels demonstrations between paired tasks with opposite objectives, reusing recorded rollouts without additional environment interaction. Because contact-rich manipulation is not exactly reversible, we treat reversal as an approximate paired-task prior rather than a physical symmetry. Temporal-stride augmentation varies observation and action sampling strides and conditions the policy on the resulting offsets, enabling fixed or randomized temporal schedules. Experiments across 16 paired bimanual manipulation tasks show that trajectory reversal is particularly effective in the low-data regime and that temporal-stride augmentation improves success under randomized temporal schedules. With 25 and 50 independently collected demonstrations per task, RESTA achieves 78.4% and 85.2% average success, respectively, matching or exceeding baselines trained with twice as many demonstrations (77.3% and 85.4%). These results show that RESTA can improve data efficiency in robot imitation learning.

</details>

## 115. GLST: Global-Local Spatio-Temporal Memory Decomposition Framework for Online Chained-Goal Navigation

**中文标题**: GLST: Global-Local Spatio-Temporal Memory Decomposition Framework for Online Chained-Goal Navigation

**作者**: Yousef Liang, Ryan Ardalan, Yarden Goraly, Chenhao Zhu, Yue Yin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17927v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17927v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17927v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autonomous navigation in real-world environments often requires long-horizon operation under partial observability and evolving goals. Existing mapless navigation approaches predominantly assume fixed goals throughout an episode, limiting applicability to practical scenarios where goals change online during execution. We study online chained-goal navigation, where goals evolve through existing-goal redirection or new-goal emergence without explicit transition signals. Under this setting, we find navigation may require either continued exploration or strategic revisitation, creating conflicting memory requirements. We propose Global-Local Spatio-Temporal Belief Learning (GLST), a memory-decomposition framework that separates latent beliefs into global-local spatial and long-short temporal representations for long-horizon reasoning. We further introduce goal-conditioned rewards to balance exploration and exploitation under evolving goals. Extensive experiments in simulation across diverse terrains demonstrate improved chained-goal navigation performance over baselines. Real-robot experiments using the Spot quadruped further validate our memory decomposition framework.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autonomous navigation in real-world environments often requires long-horizon operation under partial observability and evolving goals. Existing mapless navigation approaches predominantly assume fixed goals throughout an episode, limiting applicability to practical scenarios where goals change online during execution. We study online chained-goal navigation, where goals evolve through existing-goal redirection or new-goal emergence without explicit transition signals. Under this setting, we find navigation may require either continued exploration or strategic revisitation, creating conflicting memory requirements. We propose Global-Local Spatio-Temporal Belief Learning (GLST), a memory-decomposition framework that separates latent beliefs into global-local spatial and long-short temporal representations for long-horizon reasoning. We further introduce goal-conditioned rewards to balance exploration and exploitation under evolving goals. Extensive experiments in simulation across diverse terrains demonstrate improved chained-goal navigation performance over baselines. Real-robot experiments using the Spot quadruped further validate our memory decomposition framework.

</details>

## 116. WoVR+: World Model as Reliable Simulator for Versatile VLA Policy Optimization with RL

**中文标题**: WoVR+: World Model as Reliable Simulator for Versatile VLA Policy Optimization with RL

**作者**: Shangqing Zhou, Zhennan Jiang, Ning Ma, Xin Fu, Yupeng Zheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17922v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17922v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17922v1)

**项目主页**: https://youmo445.github.io/WoVR_Plus.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Action-conditioned world models can reduce costly real-robot interaction in reinforcement learning for Vision-Language-Action models (VLA-RL), but hallucinations can mislead policy optimization. Extending them to richer VLA interfaces and long-horizon tasks raises challenges in spatiotemporal and cross-modal consistency, exploration-reliability balance in RL, and compounding rollout errors. We present WoVR+, extending WoVR's hallucination-aware WM-RL framework by redesigning the learned environment and imagined interaction protocol: A VLA-interface-aligned world model provides multi-view prediction and proprioceptive updates while mitigating hallucinations. Chunk-wise noise preserves flow-based policy exploration while limiting prediction-degrading action jitter. For long-horizon tasks, stage-local imagined training combines stage-entry resets, stage-specific rewards, local horizons, and controllable sampling to shorten generated prefixes and balance training across stages. WoVR+ improves success over SFT by 21.09% and 16.7% on RoboTwin and 6 real-robot tasks. Long-horizon phone-packaging success rises from 30% to 75%. Videos are available at https://youmo445.github.io/WoVR_Plus.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Action-conditioned world models can reduce costly real-robot interaction in reinforcement learning for Vision-Language-Action models (VLA-RL), but hallucinations can mislead policy optimization. Extending them to richer VLA interfaces and long-horizon tasks raises challenges in spatiotemporal and cross-modal consistency, exploration-reliability balance in RL, and compounding rollout errors. We present WoVR+, extending WoVR's hallucination-aware WM-RL framework by redesigning the learned environment and imagined interaction protocol: A VLA-interface-aligned world model provides multi-view prediction and proprioceptive updates while mitigating hallucinations. Chunk-wise noise preserves flow-based policy exploration while limiting prediction-degrading action jitter. For long-horizon tasks, stage-local imagined training combines stage-entry resets, stage-specific rewards, local horizons, and controllable sampling to shorten generated prefixes and balance training across stages. </details>

## 117. Agentic Robot Creation through Generation, Verification, and Revision

**中文标题**: Agentic Robot Creation through Generation, Verification, and Revision

**作者**: Xuebing Li, Yibo He, Sitong Liu, Ran Ji, Jiayuan Gu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17881v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17881v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17881v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Can an AI agent turn high-level user intent into a functioning robot and teach it how to behave? We study this question from two complementary aspects: embodiment and behavior creation. For embodiment creation, we investigate how well current multimodal models capture mechanical requirements, and introduce computational metrics for evaluating generated designs before fabrication. For behavior creation, we present Roboforge, an agentic framework that turns task specifications into executable policies through motion generation, policy learning, and execution-grounded revision. Given a robot embodiment and task, the agent generates a reference motion, configures the learning objective, and trains a policy. Crucially, motion remains in the loop: rollout feedback can revise the reference motion, reward, or both. We evaluate a generated embodiment across multiple MLLMs and behavior creation across four diverse robots against reward-refinement and one-shot motion baselines. Our computational metrics expose recurring mechanical failures in generated designs, while behavior experiments show that execution-grounded refinement improves task-level behavior and execution quality. Finally, we fabricate an agent-generated Gorilla and directly deploy learned behaviors on the physical robot.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Can an AI agent turn high-level user intent into a functioning robot and teach it how to behave? We study this question from two complementary aspects: embodiment and behavior creation. For embodiment creation, we investigate how well current multimodal models capture mechanical requirements, and introduce computational metrics for evaluating generated designs before fabrication. For behavior creation, we present Roboforge, an agentic framework that turns task specifications into executable policies through motion generation, policy learning, and execution-grounded revision. Given a robot embodiment and task, the agent generates a reference motion, configures the learning objective, and trains a policy. Crucially, motion remains in the loop: rollout feedback can revise the reference motion, reward, or both. We evaluate a generated embodiment across multiple MLLMs and behavior creation across four diverse robots against reward-refinement and one-shot motion baselines. Our computational metrics expose recurring mechanical failures in generated designs, while behavior experiments show that execution-grounded refinement improves task-level behavior and execution quality. Finally, we fabricate an agent-generated Gorilla and directly deploy learned behaviors on the physical robot.

</details>

## 118. Learning Robot Policies for Whole-Body Mobility Assistance via Dynamic Human Motion Generation

**中文标题**: Learning Robot Policies for Whole-Body Mobility Assistance via Dynamic Human Motion Generation

**作者**: Shikun Ban, Shohei Nagai, Qingyang Song, Hanxi Wan, Eric Zhao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17750v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17750v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17750v1)

**项目主页**: https://rchi-lab.github.io/human-gen-mobility-assistance/

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robotic assistance for human mobility could benefit care recipients and reduce caregiver burden during physically demanding tasks such as Sit-to-Stand (STS) and Lift-and-Transfer (L&T). Learning robot policies for these whole-body assistive tasks is challenging: real-world data collection for human-robot interaction is costly and safety-critical, making simulation an attractive alternative. Prior work, however, typically treats the human as static or minimally responsive and thus fails to capture the cooperative nature of mobility assistance. We present a simulation-based framework for learning whole-body assistive robot policies. At its core is a human policy trained with an adversarial motion prior that produces realistic care-recipient motion in response to the robot's actions. A human-adaptive scripted controller interacts with this policy to collect diverse robot demonstrations at scale. From these demonstrations, we train 3D diffusion policies that map segmented human point clouds and robot joint positions to whole-body actions for a mobile manipulator, without relying on privileged human state at deployment. The resulting robot policies successfully perform both STS and L&T assistance in simulation. Preliminary experiments further demonstrate sim-to-real transfer for STS after fine-tuning on a small amount of real-world data. Videos are available on our project page: https://rchi-lab.github.io/human-gen-mobility-assistance/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robotic assistance for human mobility could benefit care recipients and reduce caregiver burden during physically demanding tasks such as Sit-to-Stand (STS) and Lift-and-Transfer (L&T). Learning robot policies for these whole-body assistive tasks is challenging: real-world data collection for human-robot interaction is costly and safety-critical, making simulation an attractive alternative. Prior work, however, typically treats the human as static or minimally responsive and thus fails to capture the cooperative nature of mobility assistance. We present a simulation-based framework for learning whole-body assistive robot policies. At its core is a human policy trained with an adversarial motion prior that produces realistic care-recipient motion in response to the robot's actions. A human-adaptive scripted controller interacts with this policy to collect diverse robot demonstrations at scale. From these demonstrations, we train 3D diffusion policies that map segmented human point clouds and robot joint positions to whole-body actions for a mobile manipulator, without relying on privileged human state at deployment. The resulting robot policies successfully perform both STS and L&T assistance in simulation. Preliminary experiments further demonstrate sim-to-real transfer for STS after fine-tuning on a small amount of real-world data. Videos are available on our project page: https://rchi-lab.github.io/human-gen-mobility-assistance/

</details>

## 119. Box-WAM: Box Trajectories as Compact State Representations for World Action Models

**中文标题**: Box-WAM: Box Trajectories as Compact State Representations for World Action Models

**作者**: Qinxi Yu, Wenzhou Ding, Gloria X. Wang, Albert J. Zhai, Shenlong Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17526v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17526v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17526v1)

**项目主页**: https://box-wam.github.io

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) are a category of policy models that ex- plicitly predict both actions and future world states. They have been shown to outperform action-only models across various robotic manipulation tasks. How- ever, representing the future with full-frame video prediction can be computation- ally expensive during both training and inference. This raises a central question: what compact state representation preserves the planning and supervision bene- fits of future prediction? In this paper, we propose BoxWAM, which replaces dense future frames with oriented bounding box trajectories of task-relevant ob- jects. By explicitly imagining object-centric future motion, our method provides a lightweight prediction target that captures the key physical changes induced by robot actions. We show that box trajectory prediction substantially reduces com- putational cost while improving action prediction performance, suggesting that object-centric compact states can serve as an efficient alternative to video-based world action modeling for robotic manipulation. Result videos can be found on https://box-wam.github.io

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) are a category of policy models that ex- plicitly predict both actions and future world states. They have been shown to outperform action-only models across various robotic manipulation tasks. How- ever, representing the future with full-frame video prediction can be computation- ally expensive during both training and inference. This raises a central question: what compact state representation preserves the planning and supervision bene- fits of future prediction? In this paper, we propose BoxWAM, which replaces dense future frames with oriented bounding box trajectories of task-relevant ob- jects. By explicitly imagining object-centric future motion, our method provides a lightweight prediction target that captures the key physical changes induced by robot actions. We show that box trajectory prediction substantially reduces com- putational cost while improving action prediction performance, suggesting that object-centric compact states can serve as an efficient alternative to video-based world action modeling for robotic manipulation. Result videos can be found on https://box-wam.github.io

</details>

## 120. From Detection To Anticipation: Continuous Onset Forecasting For Stuttered Speech

**中文标题**: From Detection To Anticipation: Continuous Onset Forecasting For Stuttered Speech

**作者**: Tampu Ravi Kumar, Ayoub Daliri, Vivek Gupta

**发布日期**: 2026-10-13

**arXiv ID**: [2610.19235v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19235v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19235v1)

**类别**: cs.SD, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Automatic stuttering systems primarily detect dysfluencies after they have occurred, limiting their use for anticipatory intervention. We address this gap by formulating stuttering prediction as continuous causal time-to-event forecasting, where a model estimates the risk of an upcoming stuttering onset every 100 ms using only preceding speech. To support this task, we augment FluencyBank Timestamped for 36 speakers with manually reviewed repetitions, prolongations, and blocks, yielding 2,872 forecasting events. We then model the temporal evolution of frozen Whisper-large-v3 speech representations using a compact causal Conformer with 3.67M trainable parameters. The proposed approach achieves an AUROC of 0.63. Temporal analysis further shows that explicitly modeling preceding speech states outperforms simply increasing the static acoustic window, while a discrete-time survival formulation provides coherent forecasts from 300 ms to 1.5 s using a single model. These results provide initial evidence that stuttering onset is preceded by measurable temporal information in speech and establish a framework for future anticipatory stuttering systems.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Automatic stuttering systems primarily detect dysfluencies after they have occurred, limiting their use for anticipatory intervention. We address this gap by formulating stuttering prediction as continuous causal time-to-event forecasting, where a model estimates the risk of an upcoming stuttering onset every 100 ms using only preceding speech. To support this task, we augment FluencyBank Timestamped for 36 speakers with manually reviewed repetitions, prolongations, and blocks, yielding 2,872 forecasting events. We then model the temporal evolution of frozen Whisper-large-v3 speech representations using a compact causal Conformer with 3.67M trainable parameters. The proposed approach achieves an AUROC of 0.63. Temporal analysis further shows that explicitly modeling preceding speech states outperforms simply increasing the static acoustic window, while a discrete-time survival formulation provides coherent forecasts from 300 ms to 1.5 s using a single model. These results provide initial evidence that stuttering onset is preceded by measurable temporal information in speech and establish a framework for future anticipatory stuttering systems.

</details>

## 121. Can a Frontier VLM Serve as a Robot Policy for Memory-Dependent Manipulation?

**中文标题**: Can a Frontier VLM Serve as a Robot Policy for Memory-Dependent Manipulation?

**作者**: Jack B. Jedlicki, Amish Sethi, Heng Yang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-12

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17350v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17350v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17350v1)

**项目主页**: https://embodied-astra.github.io.

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/jackbjed/embodied-astra-robomme.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We use a frozen frontier vision-language model, GPT-6-Astra, directly as the robot policy on RoboMME, a benchmark of 16 memory-dependent manipulation tasks spanning temporal, spatial, object, and procedural memory. The model keeps the whole episode in context and outputs every end-effector waypoint and gripper command, with no RoboMME-specific training. On the 800 test episodes it succeeds in 92.1%, above the previously reported human score of 90.5%, which was obtained with an oracle skill interface, and far ahead of the best leaderboard entry (60.8%) and of a hierarchical system that pairs the same model with a RoboMME-trained policy (79.1%). It matches or exceeds the human score on 13 of the 16 tasks. Its 63 failures concentrate in two tasks and are mostly premature completions and mistimed or unintended button presses. These results blur the usual division between long-horizon planning, memory and end-effector control, and raise the question of how much of this stack a single general-purpose model can take over in embodied systems. Project website: https://embodied-astra.github.io. Code, prompts and all episode logs: https://github.com/jackbjed/embodied-astra-robomme.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We use a frozen frontier vision-language model, GPT-6-Astra, directly as the robot policy on RoboMME, a benchmark of 16 memory-dependent manipulation tasks spanning temporal, spatial, object, and procedural memory. The model keeps the whole episode in context and outputs every end-effector waypoint and gripper command, with no RoboMME-specific training. On the 800 test episodes it succeeds in 92.1%, above the previously reported human score of 90.5%, which was obtained with an oracle skill interface, and far ahead of the best leaderboard entry (60.8%) and of a hierarchical system that pairs the same model with a RoboMME-trained policy (79.1%). It matches or exceeds the human score on 13 of the 16 tasks. Its 63 failures concentrate in two tasks and are mostly premature completions and mistimed or unintended button presses. These results blur the usual division between long-horizon planning, memory and end-effector control, and raise the question of how much of this stack a single general-purpose model can take over in embodied systems. Project website: https://embodied-astra.github.io. Code, prompts and all episode logs: https://github.com/jackbjed/embodied-astra-robomme.

## 122. LiDAR-based Humanoid Navigation in Dynamic, Non-Convex Environments via Spatio-Temporal Safe Corridors

**中文标题**: LiDAR-based Humanoid Navigation in Dynamic, Non-Convex Environments via Spatio-Temporal Safe Corridors

**作者**: Shubham S. Kumbhar, Panagiotis Artemiadis

**发布日期**: 2026-10-12

**arXiv ID**: [2610.17232v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17232v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17232v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Navigating humanoid robots through dynamic, non-convex environments requires tightly coupling onboard perception with footstep planning and dynamic locomotion control. Existing methods frequently discard complex free space through simplified convex obstacle bounds and fail to account for real-time dynamic occupancy predictions. This paper presents a real-time framework for humanoid navigation that directly constructs spatio-temporal safe corridors from LiDAR-based particle representations and couples them with Linear Inverted Pendulum (LIP)-based planning and Whole Body Control (WBC) to enable navigation around and within moving non-convex structures without restrictive geometric assumptions. Simulations on the Digit humanoid yield a 96% success rate in navigating around and inside moving non-convex obstacles in a 24 by 24 m^2 arena. Ablation studies confirm that the proposed iterative polytope generation significantly outperforms single-iteration baselines in narrow corridors, while evaluations demonstrate robustness to clutter, sensor noise, control delays, and force perturbations.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Navigating humanoid robots through dynamic, non-convex environments requires tightly coupling onboard perception with footstep planning and dynamic locomotion control. Existing methods frequently discard complex free space through simplified convex obstacle bounds and fail to account for real-time dynamic occupancy predictions. This paper presents a real-time framework for humanoid navigation that directly constructs spatio-temporal safe corridors from LiDAR-based particle representations and couples them with Linear Inverted Pendulum (LIP)-based planning and Whole Body Control (WBC) to enable navigation around and within moving non-convex structures without restrictive geometric assumptions. Simulations on the Digit humanoid yield a 96% success rate in navigating around and inside moving non-convex obstacles in a 24 by 24 m^2 arena. Ablation studies confirm that the proposed iterative polytope generation significantly outperforms single-iteration baselines in narrow corridors, while evaluations demonstrate robustness to clutter, sensor noise, control delays, and force perturbations.

</details>

## 123. ConDenseDrive: Knowledge-Dense, Token-Sparse Visual Encoder for End-to-End Autonomous Driving

**中文标题**: ConDenseDrive: Knowledge-Dense, Token-Sparse Visual Encoder for End-to-End Autonomous Driving

**作者**: Shengzhen Li, Yongkang Li, Hao Gao, Chenxi Wu, Yajing Sun et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-12

**arXiv ID**: [2610.16908v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16908v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.16908v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

End-to-end driving requires rich spatiotemporal representations to support scene understanding and trajectory planning under strict latency constraints. However, most systems initialize their visual encoders from generic image pretraining and densely process all patch tokens for each camera view and input frame, limiting both representation quality and inference efficiency. To address these challenges, we present ConDenseDrive, a knowledge-dense and token-sparse visual encoder for end-to-end autonomous driving. Our three-stage framework first makes the encoder knowledge-dense by distilling complementary visual, semantic, and geometric knowledge from three vision foundation models. It then adapts the distilled encoder to multi-frame end-to-end driving and integrates spatiotemporal context into register tokens for planning. Finally, it makes the encoder token-sparse by applying register-guided token compression, reducing redundant patch computation and yielding a 2.0$\times$ speedup while improving planning performance. ConDenseDrive achieves 94.5 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 90.8 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2, and reaches 37.4 HD-Score in zero-shot evaluation on HUGSIM. Extensive experiments and qualitative analyses demonstrate that visual encoder design provides an effective route to reliable and efficient end-to-end driving.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

End-to-end driving requires rich spatiotemporal representations to support scene understanding and trajectory planning under strict latency constraints. However, most systems initialize their visual encoders from generic image pretraining and densely process all patch tokens for each camera view and input frame, limiting both representation quality and inference efficiency. To address these challenges, we present ConDenseDrive, a knowledge-dense and token-sparse visual encoder for end-to-end autonomous driving. Our three-stage framework first makes the encoder knowledge-dense by distilling complementary visual, semantic, and geometric knowledge from three vision foundation models. It then adapts the distilled encoder to multi-frame end-to-end driving and integrates spatiotemporal context into register tokens for planning. Finally, it makes the encoder token-sparse by applying register-guided token compression, reducing redundant patch computation and yielding a 2.0$\times$ speedup while improving planning performance. ConDenseDrive achieves 94.5 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 90.8 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2, and reaches 37.4 HD-Score in zero-shot evaluation on HUGSIM. Extensive experiments and qualitative analyses demonstrate that visual encoder design provides an effective route to reliable and efficient end-to-end driving.

</details>

## 124. Belief Space Planning for Risk Signal Temporal Logic via Mixed-Integer Convex Programming

**中文标题**: Belief Space Planning for Risk Signal Temporal Logic via Mixed-Integer Convex Programming

**作者**: Shaohang Han, Joris Verhagen, Kaier Liang, Jana Tumova

**发布日期**: 2026-10-12

**arXiv ID**: [2610.16696v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16696v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.16696v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Signal Temporal Logic (STL) specifies robot tasks with spatio-temporal requirements, and its robustness semantics allow planning by trajectory optimization. In practice, robots estimate their state from noisy measurements, and when the information gained from a measurement depends on the state, the robot can actively reduce its uncertainty by planning in the belief space. The resulting problem is, however, generally non-convex, for which gradient-based methods are neither complete nor globally optimal. In this paper, we consider Risk STL (RiSTL) planning in Gaussian belief space and show that an information-form parameterization of the belief, together with a convex relaxation of the belief dynamics, yields a mixed-integer convex program (MICP). We prove that the MICP is sound and complete, and that the relaxation preserves the globally optimal input trajectories. We validate the effectiveness of our method in numerical simulations.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Signal Temporal Logic (STL) specifies robot tasks with spatio-temporal requirements, and its robustness semantics allow planning by trajectory optimization. In practice, robots estimate their state from noisy measurements, and when the information gained from a measurement depends on the state, the robot can actively reduce its uncertainty by planning in the belief space. The resulting problem is, however, generally non-convex, for which gradient-based methods are neither complete nor globally optimal. In this paper, we consider Risk STL (RiSTL) planning in Gaussian belief space and show that an information-form parameterization of the belief, together with a convex relaxation of the belief dynamics, yields a mixed-integer convex program (MICP). We prove that the MICP is sound and complete, and that the relaxation preserves the globally optimal input trajectories. We validate the effectiveness of our method in numerical simulations.

</details>

## 125. PINT: A Unified Model with Shared Predictive Intent for Robot Manipulation

**中文标题**: PINT: A Unified Model with Shared Predictive Intent for Robot Manipulation

**作者**: Xinyu Guo, Chao Jing, Li Yi, Xingyu Chen, Zhengxing Wu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-12

**arXiv ID**: [2610.16355v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16355v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.16355v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Generalist robot manipulation can benefit from pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) for semantic reasoning and video generation models (VGMs) for world modeling. However, existing unified models that directly fuse them through shared attention at multiple layers can make it harder to draw on their specialized capabilities. We introduce PINT, a unified model that coordinates semantic reasoning, world modeling, and action generation through a shared chunk-level Predictive Intent while preserving expert-specific pathways. PINT inserts learnable Intent Tokens into the VLM sequence to form this representation, conditioning both the VGM and an Action Expert (AE). It replaces only the VGM's prompt conditioning and retains the VGM at inference, where the AE reads its representations through one-way attention. Rather than prescribing what Predictive Intent should encode, PINT learns it jointly from the native prediction objectives of the VLM, VGM, and AE, which provide complementary views of the same upcoming task execution. Training uses two stages: Stage 1 freezes the VGM so that future video prediction shapes Predictive Intent with task-relevant future dynamics, while Stage 2 fixes the learned Intent and adapts the VGM and AE. Without embodied pretraining, PINT achieves strong in-distribution (ID) performance and substantially improves out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization, reaching 15.5% on RoboTwin 2.0-Hard compared with 1.8% for Fast-WAM and 3.2% for StarVLA, and 79.5% on LIBERO-Plus compared with 51.5% and 75.0%, respectively. Across five real-world bimanual tasks, PINT reaches 68% ID and 24% OOD success, compared with 35%/9% for pretrained Motus and 67%/35% for pretrained $π_{0.5}$.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Generalist robot manipulation can benefit from pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) for semantic reasoning and video generation models (VGMs) for world modeling. However, existing unified models that directly fuse them through shared attention at multiple layers can make it harder to draw on their specialized capabilities. We introduce PINT, a unified model that coordinates semantic reasoning, world modeling, and action generation through a shared chunk-level Predictive Intent while preserving expert-specific pathways. PINT inserts learnable Intent Tokens into the VLM sequence to form this representation, conditioning both the VGM and an Action Expert (AE). It replaces only the VGM's prompt conditioning and retains the VGM at inference, where the AE reads its representations through one-way attention. Rather than prescribing what Predictive Intent should encode, PINT learns it jointly from the native prediction objectives of the VLM, VGM, and AE, which provide complementary views of the same upcoming task execution. Training uses two stages: Stage 1 freezes the VGM so that future video prediction shapes Predictive Intent with task-relevant future dynamics, while Stage 2 fixes the learned Intent and adapts the VGM and AE. Without embodied pretraining, PINT achieves strong in-distribution (ID) performance and substantially improves out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization, reaching 15.5% on RoboTwin 2.0-Hard compared with 1.8% for Fast-WAM and 3.2% for StarVLA, and 79.5% on LIBERO-Plus compared with 51.5% and 75.0%, respectively. Across five real-world bimanual tasks, PINT reaches 68% ID and 24% OOD success, compared with 35%/9% for pretrained Motus and 67%/35% for pretrained $π_{0.5}$.

</details>

## 126. PACE: Temporal-Role Tactile Conditioning for Chunk-Based Insertion Policies

**中文标题**: PACE: Temporal-Role Tactile Conditioning for Chunk-Based Insertion Policies

**作者**: Zhiyuan Chen, Shixiong Xu, Shuai Chen, Yangwei You

**发布日期**: 2026-10-12

**arXiv ID**: [2610.16308v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16308v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.16308v1)

**项目主页**: https://pace-insertion.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Contact-rich insertion requires robots to combine visual alignment with tactile adjustment. However, existing visuotactile chunk-based policies collapse tactile feedback into a single generation-time fusion pathway, leaving persistent contact states, abrupt contact events, and future contact consequences implicitly modeled in a single representation. We present PACE (Phase-Aware Contact-Event Conditioning), a temporal-role tactile conditioning framework for chunk-based insertion policies. PACE organizes a shared tactile stream into three temporal roles: persistent contact states condition action-chunk generation, abrupt contact events correct the generated chunk, and contact-consequence prediction shapes action representations during training. This design preserves chunk-level action continuity while assigning distinct pathways to different temporal roles of contact feedback. Across five real-robot insertion tasks, PACE achieves an average success rate of 90.7%, outperforming vision-only and generation-time tactile-fusion baselines by over 44 percentage points; on the ManipulationNet benchmark, PACE maintains 88.7% success across six geometry-clearance configurations, including an industrial-grade 0.1-mm clearance. Ablations confirm the complementary contributions of the three temporal roles, mechanistic analyses show that PACE separates normal insertion progress from contact events requiring immediate correction, and cross-signal and cross-architecture experiments indicate that this temporal-role principle extends across different physical feedback signals and policy backbones. Videos and materials are available at https://pace-insertion.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

PACE organizes a shared tactile stream into three temporal roles: persistent contact states condition action-chunk generation, abrupt contact events correct the generated chunk, and contact-consequence prediction shapes action representations during training. This design preserves chunk-level action continuity while assigning distinct pathways to different temporal roles of contact feedback.
